**A-46 · `fuzz_assembly`, replayed here and run on Linux** ✅ *Windows replay 2026-10-31, T5; the 60 s log and the soak's 2026-11-04, from run 41832843136's and soak 41833219442's logs; re-read at T19*
**Steps** `ctest -R "fuzz.assembly_replay"` (the body over the corpus and the adversarial inputs, headless); the `portable-fuzz` job's log; the soak's.
**Expect** No crash, no hang beyond the body's time bound; 60 s and ten minutes with no finding, read from the **logs**. **If it fails** S1 for a crash; each finding gets a regression case before it counts as fixed.
**Result (Windows replay)** `unit.fuzz.assembly_replay: ...` -- `tests/fuzz/AssemblyFuzzBody.h`, the target's body: *"199 inputs, 123 reached the copy"* (every corpus PDF, each `assembly/` file cut at a half, a third and a fifth, and three hostile graphs -- resources naming the tree, a node listed twice, content naming itself and its page), every invariant held: refusal by key, the payload opening with no repair, one catalog and one node, every reference closed, each page its own inheritable keys, the insert and the replace composed and VERIFIED and undone to nothing.
**Result (the 60 s log, 2026-11-04) -- NOT RUN, raised as I-09.** Run **41180314162** on `bcfbb1b`, T5's push, read from its `Portable libFuzzer` log: configure counted *"fuzz_assembly (6, as expected)"* and the build linked `bin/fuzz_assembly`, and **no step ran it** -- the job fuzzed the other five, 60 s each, with no finding, and `fuzz-soak.yml`'s matrix lists the same five, so the four daily soaks since (41189182957 … 41622024204) did not run it either. Both workflows now run it, and `gates.fuzz_workflows.*` refuses a target either omits; **the 60 s half is read at the fix's push.**
**Result (the 60 s log, T6's push) -- RAN, and found I-12.** Run **41758087362** on `b3083f9`: `fuzz_assembly` loaded 1,484 seeds -- 1,462 from a `corpus/malformed/` the job's lexer and parser steps had written into -- and stopped on its first pass over them, *"the payload holds the source's tree"*, the input kept nowhere (§8 **I-12**, fixed, with the job's directories and artifacts corrected and a gate). **The 60 s with no finding is still owed**, at the next push.
**Result (the 60 s log, run 41770714539 on `d238834`) -- RAN, and found I-12 again.** Seeded from the 46 checked-in files (22 `assembly/`, 24 `malformed/`), it stopped on a mutation that left a `/Type /Pages` node with no `/Kids` -- a page to the walk, copied saying `/Pages` -- and the input was KEPT this time; fixed (§8 **I-12**). The job's five other targets each ran 60 s with no finding.
**Result (the 60 s log, run 41808943466 on `37ec179`) -- RAN, and found I-12 a third time**, about 9 s in: a page's `/Annots` naming the tree node above it, copied as an annotation; the input kept, fixed (§8 **I-12**). The five other targets again ran 60 s each with no finding.
**Result (the soak, run 41816834053 on `37ec179`, 2026-11-04) -- RAN for the first time, and found I-12** about 5 s into its 600: the same `/Annots` path the push's run found, the input kept (`fuzz-soak-fuzz_assembly`), and green against the fix (§8 **I-12**). The soak's five other targets ran their ten minutes with no finding.
**Result (the 60 s log, run **41832843136** on `279fe66`, 2026-11-04)** ✅ *"Done 11347 runs in 61 second(s)"*, no finding and no sanitizer line -- the first push since I-09's fix whose `fuzz_assembly` ran its 60 s through. The five other targets 60 s each with no finding (lexer 140,031 runs, parser 141,552, round trip 2,425, text 5,015, annotations 3,362).
**Result (the soak, run **41833219442** on `279fe66`, 2026-11-04)** ✅ dispatched by hand at 600 s: *"Done 92800 runs in 601 second(s)"*, *"no sanitizer line in fuzz_assembly's log"*; the five others ten minutes each with no finding (lexer 1,382,973 runs, parser 1,009,309, round trip 11,659 in 604 s, text 25,805, annotations 39,966). What the two claim, and only this: libFuzzer, seeded from `corpus/assembly/` and `corpus/malformed/`, drove the body for that long under ASan + UBSan and found no input breaking an invariant `tests/fuzz/AssemblyFuzzBody.h` checks -- not that the copier is correct, and each run is one independent draw.

### 5.8 Save As that rewrites (T6) — D7-25

**A-122 · Save As writes one revision of what is reachable, and loses nothing** ✅ *T6 2026-11-04; the `qpdf` half ✅ from run 41770714539's log (I-13 closed); re-run at T19* — §3.9
**Steps** `ctest -R "fullwriter"` over every editable corpus file, each after a `MovePages` and an `AddAnnotation`; the `qpdf` step's log.
**Expect** §3.9's bars for every file. **The control (rule 5):** a planted writer that skips one reachable object type (`/XObject` resources) fails the raster bar on the image fixtures. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-04, T6.* `unit.fullwriter every editable corpus file Saved As after a move and a highlight is one revision of what it reaches`: *"122 files Saved As -- 118 rewritten, 16739 objects kept, 25 dropped; 4 signed, kept by appending; 0 failures"* (not editable by the first-party writer: `merge-not-a-pdf.pdf`, `objstm-corrupt.pdf`, `truncated-mid-stream.pdf`) -- each rewrite opened with no repair, ONE section and no `/Prev`, what it holds exactly what it reaches and exactly what the composed file reached (a walk of the case's own, not the writer's), every object value for value and generation for generation the composed file's, every dropped number a free row at its generation, `/Size` above every dropped number. The raster bar: `render.fullwriter every page of every Save As that rewrote draws as the save it replaces`: *"118 files Saved As, 114 rewritten, 7708 renders, 0 differ or refused"* -- every page at 72 and 144 dpi, annotations on, against the APPEND of the same state reopened in a fresh engine (the bound: a file over 200 pages at 144 dpi every ninth page). The four signed files are appended, nobody having been asked (A-126). **Controls:** P1 (every in-use object kept) and P2 (`/XObject` resources skipped) red -- refused by the rewrite's own verify (`unreachable`, `reachable`) before any pixel -- so **P2b**, the same plant with the verify's two reachability checks disabled, is the raster bar's own control: *"26 differ or refused"*, the five the runner kept each a page drawing differently (`decorated-elsewhere.pdf`, `inherit-resources.pdf`, `compare/base.pdf` among them); P3 (a dropped number's free row at generation + 1) red; P10 (Save As appending, as before T6) red. **The `qpdf` half** is the push's: the kept files are `written/rewrite-*.pdf`.
**Result (the `qpdf` half, T6's push).** Run **41758087362**'s `Portable ASan + UBSan` log: *"qpdf check: 210 file(s), 3 failed"* -- every rewrite clean but the three of `malformed/bad-flate-stream.pdf`, `duplicate-object-number.pdf` and `pages-loop.pdf`, each refused for the damage its source carries (§8 **I-13**). The rewrites draw every page as PDFium draws their ORIGINALS, which a new case now holds every file to: `render.fullwriter an unedited Save As draws every page as the original drew it` (`[I-13]`), *"118 files Saved As unedited, 7708 renders, 0 differ"* -- the oracle the source before anything was written, not the append (P2b red in it). Those rewrites are now kept as `written/rewrite-malformed-<name>.pdf` and judged with `--source`, a failure accepted only where qpdf refuses the source too.
**Result (the `qpdf` half, run 41770714539 on `d238834`)** ✅ *"qpdf check: 210 file(s), 0 failed, 3 inherited from a source qpdf refuses too; the truncated-stream control rejected"* -- every other rewrite *"No syntax or stream encoding errors found"*, and each of the three refused for the same fault qpdf names in its source.

**A-123 · What is unreachable goes, and the file shrinks** ✅ *T6 2026-11-04*
**Steps** `ctest -R "fullwriter.compaction" -V`: delete 800 of `pages-900.pdf`'s pages; Save, and Save As, to two scratch files.
**Expect** The rewrite holds **no** object unreachable from its trailer (counted); it is smaller than both the incremental save and the original, all three sizes printed. **The control:** a planted writer that keeps every in-use entry reachable or not fails the count. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-04, T6.* `unit.fullwriter.compaction ...`: *"pages-900.pdf, 800 pages deleted -- original 126451 bytes, save 126960 (1629 objects unreachable), Save As 76241 (0 unreachable, 219 objects)"*: the incremental save GREW the file and kept 1,629 unreachable objects (the control the case asserts), the rewrite holds none and is 40 % smaller than either. **Control:** P1 (every in-use object kept) red; P10 red.

**A-124 · Numbers kept: undo, identities and recovery survive a Save As** ✅ *T6 2026-11-04*
**Steps** `ctest -R "fullwriter.identity"`: annotate, move pages, Save As; undo every step, redo every step; then the A-28 rotation with a kill after the Save As; then `documents/saveMode = full` and `Ctrl+S`.
**Expect** Every surviving object's number and generation equal before and after; each undo and redo after the Save As rasterizes as the same step did before it (0 px); every annotation identity resolves; the replay after the kill equals the document before it; with `full`, `Ctrl+S` rewrites as Save As does. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-04, T6.* `unit.fullwriter.identity ...` over `assembly/refs-every-kind.pdf`: a highlight, a move and a two-page delete (the highlighted page among them), Saved As -- every surviving object at its number and generation, value for value; the deleted pages gone from the file; then every undo reaching its step's model hash and composing (what the rewrite dropped written back, D7-29), saved by appending, and the file's pages the original objects in the original order, each dictionary but `/Parent` and every annotation it lists value for value the original's; every redo after it; then `documents/saveMode = full` -- `Save` rewrote, one section, nothing unreached, two retired revisions held. The pixels: `render.fullwriter.identity ...` -- each undo and redo after the rewrite drawn, live, as its step drew before it (0 differ, 252 renders), which found **I-10** (§8). Recovery: `platform.recovery.kill_rewrite ...` (`rawpage_killprobe rewrite`, a mode added for it) -- the page cycle, a Save As that rewrote, then twenty kills: *"between apply and the flush: 12; inside apply: 2; inside the flush: 6; most steps replayed after the rewrite: 8"*, the journal naming the rewrite each time, the probe's rewrite and the case's own byte-identical, and every replay equal -- by model hash and by the composed bytes -- to the same steps run over that rewrite opened fresh. Fresh, not the model carried across the save, and measured why: after an APPEND save as after a rewrite, a model carried across it differs from a fresh reading of its file in two ways neither is the replay's question -- the headless backend reads no `/PageLabels`, and a created annotation keeps its `/NM` identity in its own session where a reading names it by its object. The `Ctrl+S` half through the shell: `ui.documents/saveMode full makes Save rewrite ...`. **Controls:** P4 (no revival) and P5 (no retired fallback) red; P7 (I-10's fallback removed) red in the raster case; P11 (a rewrite's save not telling the recovery journal the document moved) red in the kill case. **Not covered** (stated at the kill case): an undo, after a save, of a step from before it, replayed by recovery -- which does not replay for a page delete after any save, **I-11** (§8).

**A-125 · Save a Copy leaves the tab where it was** ✅ *T6 2026-11-04*
**Steps** `ctest -R "ui.save_a_copy"`.
**Expect** The copy written by the rewrite at the chosen path and passing A-122; the tab keeps its own path, title and unsaved state, and a later `Ctrl+S` writes to its own file. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-04, T6.* `ui.Save a Copy writes the rewrite elsewhere and the tab keeps its file, title and changes` (`file.saveACopy` dispatched, the path answered by the twin): the copy written by the REWRITE form, the tab's path, title and dirty state unchanged, its own file untouched; a later `file.save` appended to its own file and left the copy as it was. The coordinator's half, over a real file: `unit.fullwriter.copy ...` -- the copy one section with nothing unreached, the journal dirty and on its own path, a later Save appending to the original. **Control:** P9 (the copy adopted) red in both.

**A-126 · A signed document asks; an encrypted one is copied** ✅ *T6 2026-11-04*
**Steps** `ctest -R "fullwriter.signed|fullwriter.encrypted|a4_product" -V` over the four `corpus/signed/` files and `corpus/encrypted/`; the question answered each way, and once with no user.
**Expect** *Keep the signatures*: copied and appended, and OpenSSL verifies all four, each with one byte flipped first as its control; *rewrite*: written, the question having named the signatures as becoming invalid; **no user: the signatures kept** (convention 16). Encrypted: copied and appended, the output's prefix byte-identical to the original. A **repaired** document's forced Save As now rewrites, and the output opens with **no repair**. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-04, T6.* `unit.fullwriter.signed ...` over the four `corpus/signed/` files: each counts its signature (`pdf/ops/SignatureFields`, D7-20's reading); *cancel* writes nothing and leaves no temporary file, the question having been handed the count; *keep* appends, the opened bytes a prefix; *rewrite* writes one section; nobody to ask appends. And an unsigned file is never asked (the control). `render.a4_product.saveas.openssl`: a duplicate then a Save As with nobody to ask, on each signed file, and OpenSSL *"8 of 8 controls rejected, 4 of 4 signatures verify"*, each signed range ending where the original's did (`approval.pdf`: byte 19370 of 19768). The question through the shell: `ui.Save As asks before a rewrite breaks a signature ...` -- each answer writing what it says, *cancel* reporting no failure. Encrypted: `render.fullwriter.encrypted ...` -- the one `corpus/encrypted/` file the engine opens with no password Saved As, byte for byte the original, the document then living at the copy. Repaired: `unit.fullwriter.repaired ...` -- *"12 repaired files rewritten"*, each opening with no repair, one section, nothing unreached. **Controls:** P6 (signatures ignored) red; P8 (the encrypted copy short) red.

**A-134 · An undo after a save, of a page command from before it, is recovered from a checkpoint** ✅ *2026-11-22, I-11, D7-43*
**Steps** `ctest -R "undo past the last save|undo past a save that rewrote|keeps one checkpoint|refuses a checkpoint|which undo reaches back"` (`tests/unit/tst_recovery.cpp`, `tests/unit/tst_journal.cpp`, `[S7-I-11]`, the first-party backend); `ctest -R "recovers an undo after a save of a page delete"` (`tests/composition/tst_recoveryproduct.cpp`, the product's composition and the real engine).
**Expect** I-11's own scenario -- `assembly/refs-every-kind.pdf`, page 2 deleted, saved, the delete undone, a rotation after it, the session gone -- recovered EXACTLY: the journal a `base` line and a `run`, one checkpoint beside it; the candidate naming it; the replay applying the rotation from it, nothing stopped; 12 pages, the model hash, the outline and links the live session's; the document carrying the user's file's identity, so a Save writes over that file and re-reads as it. The same after a Save As that REWROTE (two pages deleted) and after a RENUMBER. Each further undo past the base replacing the checkpoint (`-1`, then `-2`), a redo after it an ordinary line; a save and a discard removing it; a recovery's next journal starting from a copy of it, which a second crash recovers the same. A checkpoint with one byte changed, missing, or named outside its directory refused as damage (`error.recovery.checkpoint`); a `base` line after an operation malformed where it stands. The journal telling its recorder which undo reaches back past the base -- a save moving it, an undo from before it moving it, a redo bringing a step back into it. Through the product: the undo by `edit.undo` writing `checkpoint-1.pdf` through the real backend, the recovery prompt recovering it into the file with the banner's checkpoint line, and a Save over the file removing it. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** Every check above as written. *The control in the case itself* (convention 7): the same scenario with no checkpoint writer is I-11 as it was -- the replay stopped at step 1, no step applied, 11 pages. *Controls*, all red: Q1 the recorder writing a line where it should checkpoint; Q2 the journal never saying before-base; Q3 a redo not counting as since the base; Q4 a save not moving the base; Q5 the replay refusing the `base` line; Q6 a checkpoint's hash not checked; Q7 the next journal not given the checkpoint; Q8 a save leaving it; Q9 the window recovering onto the source, not the checkpoint; Q10 the window not giving it the source's identity; Q11 the tab installing no checkpoint writer. *What it does not cover* (convention 19): no process is killed -- the product case copies a live session's directory without its lock, which is how a crashed one looks, and the unit cases drop the journal object; the kill harness (A-68, Stage 3's guide) has no cycle that undoes past a save.

### 5.9 New documents: extract and split (T7) — Q1 ✅

**A-47 · Every new document opens everywhere it can be checked here** ✅ *T7 2026-11-05; re-run by T10 (✅ 2026-11-09) and T16; both `qpdf` halves ✅ 2026-11-15 from run 43555926975's log (A-25)*
**Steps** `ctest -R "assembler"`; the `qpdf` step's log.
**Expect** Each output: **one** revision (one `xref`, no `/Prev`), a fresh `/ID`, PDFium opens it with **no repair**, every page as planned and §3.4's bar against its source page; `qpdf --check` clean. **If it fails** S1.
**T10's re-run** ✅ *2026-11-09.* Every merge `tst_merge` makes is read back first-party as ONE section with no repair, and kept in RAWPAGE_WRITTEN_DIR as `assembly-merge-*.pdf` -- **9 files** -- for the `portable-asan` job's `qpdf --check`; every merge `tst_mergeengine` and `tst_mergeproduct` make is opened by PDFium with an empty repair report. **The `qpdf` half** ✅ run 43555926975 on `e52b719`, read 2026-11-15: all nine *"No syntax or stream encoding errors found"* (A-25).
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-05, T7.* **First-party** (`unit.assembler every corpus file assembled whole and in part ...`): every corpus file but `encrypted/`, opened by `pdf/ops/DocumentAssembler::Source` and made whole, at its odd pages and at its last page, each read back by the first-party reader against its SOURCE read the same way -- *"A-47: 121 files, 253 new documents, 121 kept for qpdf; 4 refused by key"* (`merge-not-a-pdf.pdf` `error.pdf.noObjects`, `objstm-corrupt.pdf` and `truncated-mid-stream.pdf` `error.pdf.noPageTree`, `pages-loop.pdf` `error.pdf.pageTreeDamaged`), **0 failures**: one section and no `/Prev`, a two-part `/ID` not the source's, nothing held unreached, the page count, each page's rotation, media box and content-stream bytes its source page's. `unit.assembler a 900-page document is a balanced tree ...`: at 1, 32, 33 and 900 pages every node lists at most 32 kids, every page at one depth, every `/Count` and `/Parent` true, the objects numbered from 1 with no gap; two documents of the same pages have two identifiers. **Through the engine** (`render.assembler every new document opens with no repair and draws as its source`): every file the shipped backend opens, its snapshot assembled whole and opened from its bytes as an extract is -- *"118 files, 714 pages compared at 72 dpi, 13 pages with a widget skipped, 0 differ"*, each with an EMPTY repair report and refused for editing by `error.edit.unsavedAssembly`; seven refused (six the engine does not open, `pages-loop.pdf` by key). **`qpdf`**: the first-party sweep keeps each whole-file document in `written/` (`assembly-NNN.pdf`, and `assembly-malformed-<source>.pdf` for a damaged source, whose failure `check-qpdf.py --source` accepts only where qpdf refuses the source too -- I-13's rule, `ci.yml` given the prefix); ✅ read from run 43555926975's `portable-asan` log 2026-11-15 -- all 121 clean but `assembly-malformed-bad-flate-stream.pdf` and `assembly-malformed-duplicate-object-number.pdf`, each inherited from a source qpdf refuses too (A-25). **Controls:** P1 (no balanced tree), P2 (the `/ID` not fresh), P3 (verify skips the objects) and P14 (a page left naming the payload's node as its parent) red -- P14 first-party only: PDFium walks `/Kids` and never reads a page's `/Parent`, so the engine's split cases stayed green under it, which is why the balanced-tree case checks every `/Parent` itself; and `unit.assembler verify refuses what it was not built to be` -- a content byte flipped, a second revision appended, another document's three pages -- each refused.

**A-48 · Extract: to one, per page, and with delete** ✅ *T7 2026-11-05*
**Steps** `ctest -R "extract"`.
**Expect** The right pages by identity in each output; *extract and delete* deletes **only after** the write verifies — **the control:** a planted write failure leaves the source document untouched and no file. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-05, T7.* `render.assembler extract to one and per page ...` over `odd-count-9.pdf`: pages 2, 5 and 9 as one document show `C09-0002`, `C09-0005`, `C09-0009` in order, as PDFium extracts them; pages 1 and 7 as a file each, opened from their paths with no repair, show theirs. **The document as it stands**: `render.assembler extract takes the shipped backend's document as it stands ...` -- a rotation and a move made and not saved, then pages 2 and 5 extracted: the turned page at 90 degrees and the moved page where the move put it, the file on disk untouched (`unit.assembler takes the document as it stands ...` the same over `CosBackend`). **Extract and delete through the product** (`ui.composition.extract and delete removes the pages in one undo step ...`): the new tab holds 2 pages, the source 7, and ONE undo gives back 9. **The control** (`ui.composition.extract and delete a planted write failure ...`): the third of three per-page files corrupted after it is built, two already on disk -- the extract refused, the source 9 pages, clean, nothing to undo, the folder empty; the same choice unplanted writes three files and deletes three pages. The file half alone, `unit.assembler extract a file that fails its verify takes back every file ...`: no file and no temporary left. **Controls:** P5 (a failed verify takes nothing back), P6 (a written file not read back), P10 (the snapshot the file on disk, unsaved edits ignored) and P11 (the delete run before the write) red.

**A-49 · The five splits, graded against the labels** ✅ *T7 2026-11-05*
**Steps** `ctest -R "split"` over `split-blanks.pdf`, `refs-every-kind.pdf`, `tagged-headings.pdf` and `pages-900.pdf`.
**Expect** Every N; at every top-level bookmark; at a chosen level; at blank pages (by the **content box**, so the white-ink page is blank to the eye and **not** blank to the split — its label says which); at a text pattern — each output's first and last identities as labelled; **by size**: every output at or under the bound, except a single page larger than the bound, written alone and reported. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-05, T7.* Six rules, not five -- `Features.md` §5 lists top-level bookmarks and a chosen level apart, and both are built (D7-30). Through `core::SplitRunner` as the product runs it -- the engine's content box one page per engine task, the search engine's match over the text index -- each written file opened by PDFium from its path with no repair, its first and last identity extracted and compared with the label: **`split-blanks.pdf`** at blank pages *SB-0001…SB-0002, SB-0004…SB-0005, SB-0007…SB-0009* -- the two pages with no object left out, the white-ink page OPENING the third file -- at the label's `headingPattern` and at its top-level bookmarks *SB-0001…page 3 (which shows none), SB-0004…SB-0007, SB-0008…SB-0009*; **`refs-every-kind.pdf`** at top-level bookmarks and at level 2, the starts read from the label's outline; **`tagged-headings.pdf`** at a level-1 heading pattern, the starts its label's level-1 headings; **`pages-900.pdf`** every 250 pages (250, 250, 250, 150, each file's first and last page drawn as the source's, 0 px) and by size under 40,960 bytes -- *"11 files ... 40807 40893 40904 ... 19477"*, 900 pages, and no two neighbouring files would have fit as one. The planning half, `unit.splitplan ...` (seven cases), against hand values and the split-blanks label read as facts; a heavy page alone and flagged. Through the product: `ui.composition.page.split at blank pages writes its files through the product's pumps`. **Controls:** P7 (a blank page written), P8 (bookmarks at every depth), P9 (a pattern splitting after its page) and P13 (a heavy page not flagged) red. P7 first CRASHED the planning binary -- a case read `error()` of a rule that, planted, succeeded -- so its refusal checks now read a key or `(success)` (`keyOf`), and P7 is red without a crash.

**A-50 · Nothing is overwritten, and nothing lands outside the chosen folder** ✅ *T7 2026-11-05*
**Steps** `ctest -R "split.collision"`: a scratch folder holding one of the names a split would write.
**Expect** The split refused before writing anything, the collision named; a directory listing of the scratch root before and after shows only the chosen folder changed. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-05, T7.* `render.assembler split.collision ...`: a split of `odd-count-9.pdf` into threes over a folder already holding `doc part 2.pdf` -- refused `error.assembly.exists` naming that path, and the listing of the whole scratch root, before and after, IDENTICAL (nothing written, not even the first file); `unit.assembler split.collision ...` the same through the snapshot directly, the existing file's bytes unchanged, plus `../escape.pdf`, `sub/x.pdf`, `..` and an empty name refused `error.assembly.badName` and `x.pdf` beside `X.pdf` `error.assembly.duplicateName`, the listing unchanged after each. A file is written beside its target and MOVED onto it, which a rename does only where nothing is (`unit.assembler write makes every file, read back from its path`: the folder created and holding exactly the two files). **Control:** P4 (a name the folder holds not checked) **stayed GREEN at first in both cases** -- the rename's own refusal, and the write taking back the file it had already made, left the listing unchanged and named the same path, while the promise *before anything is written* was broken. The unit case now counts the files BUILT through the write seam -- none, for a write refused up front -- and P4 is red there; the render case grades the listing and is kept as it was.

**A-51 · An assembled document opens as an untitled tab** ✅ *T7 2026-11-05*

**Result** ✅ *2026-11-05, T7.* `ui.composition.ui.untitled_assembly ...`, the product as `main.cpp` composes it over `odd-count-9.pdf`: `page.extract` of pages 2-3 opens a second tab titled `shell.document.untitled`'s text, of 2 pages, DIRTY, not editable (`error.edit.unsavedAssembly`, D7-30), the source unchanged and clean, and the scratch folder's listing unchanged; closing it with nobody to ask is refused `error.close.needsDisposition`; `Ctrl+S` (`file.save`, enabled for it) asks for a path, proposing one beside the source, and the saved file is opened in the tab's place -- an ordinary document, editable, clean, of 2 pages. **Control:** P12 (an assembly not dirty) red -- the close went through unasked. *Not covered (convention 19):* the dialogs are answered through their twins; their widgets are `ui.pageops ...`'s (four cases) and the walker's.

### 5.10 The thumbnails pane edits (T8)

**A-52 · The whole Page Thumbnails pane table, from key events** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8*
**Steps** `ctest -R "ui.thumbnails_edit"`: for each row of `Features.md` §8's **Page Thumbnails pane** table, a `QKeyEvent` sent through `ShortcutBinder`.
**Expect** Each chord runs its command on the **selection** (`Ctrl+R` on a set, D7-13); `Delete`, `Alt+↑`/`Alt+↓`, `Ctrl+Shift+D` one undo step each; `Ctrl+Shift+X` opens extract with the selection filled in; `Ctrl+A` selects every page; the census rows in `tst_keybindings` compare §8 row for row. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8.* `ui.thumbnails_edit every row of the Page Thumbnails table runs from its key on the set` (`tests/ui/tst_thumbnailsedit.cpp`), real key events to the focused pane through the window's own binder over a twelve-page editable document: `Ctrl+A` selects 12; `↓` moves a one-page set, `Shift+↓` twice extends it to {3, 4, 5}; `Alt+↓` and `Alt+↑` each ONE `MovePagesCommand` and the set follows its pages ({4, 5, 6}, back to {3, 4, 5}); `Ctrl+Shift+D` one duplicate (15 pages); `Ctrl+R` the dialog offered the SET, one `RotatePageCommand`, one undo step, exactly the set turned 90; `Ctrl+Shift+X` extract handed the set; `Ctrl+C` the set on the clipboard under `application/x-rawpage-pages`, `Ctrl+V` one insert landing after the set's last page; `Ctrl+X` a copy and ONE delete; `Delete` the set with no question; `Enter` the view to the selected page; `+`/`-` the size -- and seven undos restore twelve unturned pages, one gesture each. `ui.thumbnails_edit a set at the end ...`: `Alt+↓` on the last page and `Alt+↑` on the first DISABLED, the key left alone and nothing run; every page selected, `Delete` refused by `error.edit.noPagesLeft` and `Ctrl+X` disabled. The census: `tst_keybindings`' §8 rows unchanged (every row was bound since Stage 1) and green; `tst_shell` and `tst_documenttabs` re-counted 186 registered (+10), `tst_panes`' Stage 4 case of unregistered rows turned round to a census of registered ones. **Controls:** P5 (the set not remapped), P10 (the window counting the engine's pages, not the model's), P12 (a later move one place too far) red; **P13 (a paste landing before the set) STAYED GREEN** until this case checked where the sink was told the paste lands -- it had counted pages only. *Not covered (convention 19):* the fake backend draws no page; what each page SHOWS after a move, a paste or a cut is A-54's and A-55's product halves.

**A-53 · Selection is a set, and follows its pages** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8*

The view sent to page 10 leaves a set of several as it was (D7-31(a)); a `Ctrl`+click does not move the view, a plain click does. After `{1, 4}` moved to the end the set is `{10, 11}`; after a delete of 10 from `{0, 10, 11}` it is `{0, 10}`. **Control:** P5 red.

**A-54 · A drag reorders in one step, and copies across windows** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8*
**Steps** `ctest -R "thumbnails.drag"`: drag a set within a pane; drag it into another window's pane of the same process.
**Expect** Within: one `MovePages`, one undo step, the drop position as indicated. Across: one `InsertPages` in the target, **the source unchanged**, the pages showing their identities in the target. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8.* Real `QDragEnterEvent`/`QDragMoveEvent`/`QDropEvent`s sent to the target's viewport with the source pane's `dragData()`: within, the marker reads the insertion index while the drag is over the pane and clears on leave; `{1, 2}` dropped before page 5 is ONE `MovePagesCommand`, one undo step, landing at {3, 4}; dropped where they already are, no command. Across, two windows of one process: `{0, 3}` dropped before page 2 of the other is ONE insert there, copied out of the SOURCE (`AssemblySource::copy`), the source clean and unchanged; a drag naming another process's id refused, nothing run. Through the PRODUCT (`ui.composition.thumbnails.drag ...`, `tests/composition/tst_pagesproduct.cpp`): `refs-every-kind.pdf`'s pages 3 and 4 dropped into `odd-count-9.pdf` read **P-0003, P-0004** between C09-0001 and C09-0002 by the live engine; after P-0012 is moved to the front of the source, a second drop takes **P-0012** -- the source as it stands at the drop. **Controls:** P8 (another process's drag accepted) and P14 (the drop copying the target's own pages) red. *Not covered (convention 19):* `QDrag::exec`, the source half, a nested event loop -- M-01 step 4.

**A-55 · Cut, copy and paste of pages — across processes** ✅ *in-process 2026-11-07, T8; the second process `[D7-13]` 2026-11-07, by hand on `windows` with the user's leave*
**Steps** `ctest -R "thumbnails.clipboard"` (in-process); `$env:QT_QPA_PLATFORM='windows'; rawpage_composition_tests.exe "[D7-13]"` with your leave — **it replaces the system clipboard's contents** (Stage 5's A-50). *(The binary was `rawpage_ui_tests` as drafted; the case needs the real engine to read the pasted pages' identities, so it is the composition binary's.)*
**Expect** Copy puts `application/x-rawpage-pages` on the clipboard; paste in a **second process** inserts the pages, showing their identities; cut is copy then one `DeletePages`. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (in-process)** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8.* The payload's form (`unit.thumbnails.clipboard the payload round-trips ...`): `{version, pages, graph}` round-trips a 70 KB graph; refused by `error.clipboard.malformed` -- not JSON, version 2, no version, 0, 1.5, two million or a string of pages, no graph, a graph not base64, a GIF, and a payload past the bound unread; the same shape right read (the control). The snapshot's copy (`unit.thumbnails.clipboard the snapshot's copy ...`): two pages of `refs-every-kind.pdf` with an UNSAVED rotation, through the clipboard's form and inserted into `odd-count-3.pdf` -- the rotation carried, the source untouched. Through the product (`ui.composition.thumbnails.clipboard ...`): pages 1 and 12 copied and pasted after page 1 read **P-0001, P-0012** between P-0001 and P-0002; cut, ONE undo step, the document back to twelve; pasted into another window's `odd-count-3.pdf` at its end, **P-0001, P-0012**. **Control:** P3 (a graph not beginning `%PDF-` accepted) red. **The second process:** `[D7-13]` copies pages 5 and 7 of `refs-every-kind.pdf`, runs this binary again as `[.][D7-13-child]` -- hidden from every other run -- which pastes the clipboard into a copy of `odd-count-3.pdf` and saves, and reads the saved file's pages 4 and 5 for **P-0005, P-0007**. Under `ctest` it SKIPS (offscreen, the clipboard never leaves the process) -- a sixth named skip.
**Result (the second process)** ✅ *2026-11-07, T8, on `windows` with the user's leave and hands off the desktop.* *"All tests passed (14 assertions in 1 test case)"*: the child read this process's clipboard (2 pages), pasted them at the end of its copy of `odd-count-3.pdf` and saved; the saved file, opened by a fresh product here, has 5 pages, its 4th and 5th **P-0005** and **P-0007**. **Its first run FAILED** -- the child never finished inside 120 s -- and the defect was the CASE's: Windows hands a clipboard's bytes over by delayed rendering, asking the COPYING process for them when another reads, and the parent sat in `QProcess::waitForFinished`, which pumps nothing, so it could not answer its own child. The parent now pumps its event loop while it waits, as a person's first RawPage does; the product was not changed. No process was left behind either time.

### 5.11 Boxes, crop, resize, renumber (T9)

**A-60 · The five boxes, written and read back** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9*
**Steps** `ctest -R "pageops.boxes"`; `serialize:`/`revert:CropPagesCommand`.
**Expect** Each box set alone and together, read back by PDFium (`FPDFPage_Get*Box`) as written; a box outside the MediaBox clipped to it and reported; *undo a crop* removes `/CropBox`. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `CropPagesCommand` (`page.crop`): what each page SHOWS (`core::PageBoxes`), the keys the sink's (D7-32(a)). **First-party** (`unit.pageops.boxes ...`, three cases): on `boxes.pdf`'s page 1 (all five, from its label) each box alone, saved, read back as asked, and each undone AFTER the save read back as the label's; all five together; a crop of pages 1 and 3 undone before a save writes nothing; *undo a crop* -- the CropBox removed -- leaves no `/CropBox` and the model showing the MediaBox; **on `inherit-resources.pdf`, a page INHERITING its CropBox**, the removal shows the MediaBox (written as its own CropBox, D7-32(a)) and its undo the inherited one; page 2's CropBox reaching outside on three sides written clipped to the label's `cropBoxClippedToMedia`, a TrimBox likewise, the report two `PageBox` references `Clipped`; a box that does not touch the MediaBox and a CropBox under 3 points refused, nothing written. A crop carried by a move and copied by a duplicate, every step undone after a save: the file's own boxes. **By PDFium** (`render.pageops.boxes ...`): a fresh engine reads all five as written, within a hundredth of a point (it reads floats), page 2's clipped crop, and after *undo a crop* the CropBox and ArtBox as the MediaBox; the live engine's page tree the same. `[serialize:CropPagesCommand]` two samples, the T9 hostile table's ten crop lines refused; `[revert:CropPagesCommand]` over every editable corpus file. **Controls:** P1 (a revert that does not tell the sink) red; **P2 (an inherited CropBox not overridden when a crop is removed) STAYED GREEN** until the inheriting case was written; P4 (the engine's page tree not told the new boxes) red; P12 (a MediaBox asked and never written) red.

**A-61 · White margins are removed to the CONTENT box** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9 -- graded, for a stroked path, with the engine's stroke allowance (I-16)*
**Steps** `ctest -R "crop.white_margins"` over `boxes.pdf` and `cropbox-margins.pdf`.
**Expect** The CropBox equals Stage 4's content box (`IDocumentBackend::contentBox`) padded by `documents/cropRemoveWhiteMargins`, and the cropped page's **ink** (rule 6) touches each edge within the padding ± 1 device pixel at 144 dpi; *all pages of the same size* crops exactly those pages. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `render.crop.white_margins ...`: at paddings 0 and 9 points the written CropBox is the live engine's content box padded, read back by a fresh engine within a hundredth of a point; the ink, at 144 dpi, is **`boxes.pdf`'s page 3 (a filled rectangle) within one device pixel of the padding on every edge**, and **`cropbox-margins.pdf`'s panel (stroked 2 pt wide) one POINT further on every edge** -- the engine's content box of a stroked path reaches 2 pt past the path where the ink reaches 1 (I-16), graded as measured; a crop 3 pt inside the content box, the control, leaves ink at the edge. `unit.crop.white_margins ...`: `whiteMarginBox` padded, never past the CropBox, a negative padding none, content outside left alone; `pagesOfSameSize` over `mixed-page-sizes.pdf`, A4 portrait and landscape told apart as shown. **Through the product** (`ui.composition.pages remove white margins ...`): the dialog's choice measures the three pages on the render thread and runs ONE crop, page 3's box the content box padded by 4; one undo puts all three back; a delete landing while the pages are read refuses the run with `error.pages.documentChanged` and crops nothing. `documents/cropRemoveWhiteMargins` declared (0 ... 72 pt, 0 by default) on the Documents page. **Control:** P6 (the padding ignored) red.

**A-62 · A resized page's content is scaled and placed as asked — on the ink** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9*
**Steps** `ctest -R "resize"` over `text-letter.pdf` → A4 and `mixed-page-sizes.pdf`.
**Expect** With scaling: the ink's bounding box scaled by the ratio asked and centred (or anchored as asked) within ± 1 device pixel at 144 dpi; without scaling: the boxes alone change and the ink does not move; the wrapper streams marked (D7-7), and undo byte-identical. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `ResizePagesCommand` (`page.resize`): `planResize` from the model (D7-32(e)), then the boxes and -- with scaling -- the wrapper. **On the ink** (`render.resize ...`): `text-letter.pdf` to A4 scaled and centred, its ink measured at 288 dpi before and at 144 dpi after, each edge where the plan's matrix puts it within one device pixel plus the reference's quarter point; the live engine's tree A4; undone and saved, the letter page and its ink exactly as before. Without scaling to 700 x 900 the MediaBox `-44 -54 656 846` and the ink within a pixel of where it was. `mixed-page-sizes.pdf`'s unturned pages to US Letter, scaled, bottom-left: each page's ink where its own matrix puts it. **The wrapper** (`unit.resize ...`): `/Contents` three items -- a stream beginning `q 0.9727 0 0 0.9727 0` and ending `re W n`, and one `Q`, both marked `/RawPageDecoration << /Kind /Resize /Id <the command's key> >>`, the page's own content untouched between them; undone before a save, nothing to write; undone AFTER a save, `/Contents` the page's own reference again and the MediaBox back; two resizes nest (five items) and the inner one comes out alone. The plan's arithmetic against hand values: centred, a cropped page from its own corner, a page turned 90 (its sides swapped, top-left its own bottom-left, and **top-centre its own left edge halfway up**), and without scaling centred and bottom-right; a target under 3 or over 14,400 points refused. `[serialize:ResizePagesCommand]` two samples, eight hostile lines refused; `[revert:ResizePagesCommand]` over every editable corpus file. *Not covered:* a comment keeps its rectangle under scaling and is reported, not moved (D7-32(e)). **Controls:** P3 (the two streams swapped) and P7 (a quarter turn's anchor read across for down -- red only once the top-centre check was added; top-left cannot tell the axes apart) red.

**A-63 · Renumbering pages** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9*
**Steps** `ctest -R "renumber"`; `serialize:`/`revert:RenumberPagesCommand`.
**Expect** Each style (arabic, roman upper and lower, letters upper and lower), a prefix and a start, per range, read back by `FPDF_GetPageLabel` against hand-written tables; ranges outside the selection unchanged. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `RenumberPagesCommand` (`page.renumber`) over a page set (D7-32(f)). **By PDFium** (`render.renumber ...`): on `odd-count-9.pdf`, five commands -- roman lower from 1, letters upper with `App-` from 26, roman upper from 14, letters lower from 3, no number with `Cover` -- and a fresh engine's labels are the hand table `i ii iii App-Z App-AA 6 XIV c Cover` (page 6, outside every range, its own number), the model's the same; all undone and saved, no page has a label. **First-party** (`unit.renumber ...`): a document with no labels numbered `i ii iii 4 5 ... 9`, undone after the save with `/PageLabels` taken out of the catalog; each of the six styles with `P-` from 26 against its hand table, the pages either side unchanged; `outline-labels.pdf`'s own numbering kept on every page but the one renumbered, and an undo after the save writes it back exactly. `[serialize:RenumberPagesCommand]` two samples (one prefix not Latin), seven hostile lines refused; `[revert:RenumberPagesCommand]` over every editable corpus file. **Control:** P9 (a style-less label numbered as asked, where a reader counts it within its range) red -- found while writing the case: such a save failed its own verify.

**A-64 · The crop tool's box is where it was dragged** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9 -- at scale 1; the raster at 1.5 is M-08's*
**Steps** `ctest -R "ui.croptool"`, rendering into an image at 1.5 at an odd logical offset (Stage 5's A-123).
**Expect** The written CropBox, drawn back, coincides with the dragged rectangle on the **raster** within 1 device pixel; snapping as `core/Snap.h` resolves it; `Esc` cancels with no command. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `ui.croptool ...` (`tests/ui/tst_croptool.cpp`, three cases): the tool alone over a host mapping a pixel to a point hands ONE page-space box, its left corner snapped onto a guide 1 point away, `y` turned up; a click, a drag under the marquee's threshold either way and an `Esc` mid-drag hand nothing. Through the window: `page.crop` arms it (cross cursor, sticky); `Esc` mid-drag runs no command; a drag with nobody to ask runs ONE `CropPagesCommand` of page 1, the CropBox written within a device pixel of the page points under the two pixels, the model showing it, only the CropBox changed -- and **drawn back through the view's layout, its corners within one pixel of the pixels dragged between**; with a person (the dialog's twin), the dialog is offered the dragged box's margins, and widening it to three pages crops each from its own MediaBox, one command. **Bound, as drafted:** at the offscreen platform's scale of 1 and graded on the layout the raster is placed by; A-123's 1.5 at an odd offset was not built -- the dragged box at 150 % is M-08 step 1. **Control:** P8 (`Esc` leaving the drag going) red.

**A-65 · Crop through the product, saved, undone** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9*
**Steps** `ctest -R "composition.assembly_shown"` with a crop and a resize in its rotation.
**Expect** §3.3's bar. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-08, T9.* `ui.composition.assembly_shown ...` (A-29's case) gained five steps after its six: a page cropped, one resized to A4 scaled and centred, one to 500 x 700 unscaled, three pages renumbered, every page given a thumbnail -- each saved and every page compared live against the saved file reopened fresh at 72 dpi, 144 dpi and turned: **0 differing bytes, 16 x 5 more pages**; every step undone after the saves, every page as the original draws it. **Its first run failed** on the unscaled step: the page was drawn **700 pixels tall live and 701 from its saved file** -- the engine's page tree held the new box in doubles (700.0) while a fresh open reads PDFium's floats (700.000015), and a page's tile height is rounded up. The tree now carries each edge as the engine reads it (D7-32(b), `DocumentBackendImpl::showEdits`) -- and the plain `double(float(x))` of that fix came back unchanged from that translation unit under GCC 13.1 at `-O3`, so the narrowing goes through a `volatile`, measured. **Control:** P5 (the narrowing dropped) red. *Not covered:* the commands are constructed, not made by their surfaces -- §3.3's *through its surface* is A-119's sweep at T19.

### 5.12 Merge (T10)

**A-66 · A bookmark per file, the sources' outlines beneath, landing on the right pages** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.outline"` over `merge-a`, `merge-b`, `merge-c`, with `documents/mergeBookmarkPerFile` each way.
**Expect** One top-level bookmark per input named from its file name or `/Title` as set, each landing on that input's first page **by the identity it shows**; every source bookmark beneath its file's, landing as §3.2 grades. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* **Through the engine** (`render.merge.outline every bookmark lands on the page it names ...`), `merge-a`, `merge-b`, `merge-c` merged in each of the setting's three ways, written and opened by PDFium with no repair, the outline PDFium resolves graded by the identity it extracts from each target page: *"A-66 none: 9 bookmarks landed by identity"*, *"fileName: 12"*, *"title: 12"* -- each file's bookmark titled `merge-a` ... (or *Merge source A* ...), CLOSED, on the page showing its file's first identity, and every source bookmark on the page showing the identity it is titled with. **First-party** (`unit.merge.outline a bookmark per file ...`): every item's `/Parent` and `/Prev` naming the item that lists it; with `none`, the sources' items the top level in order. **A range** (`unit.merge.outline a range leaves a bookmark ...`, merge-a's pages 1 and 3): `MA-0002`'s bookmark kept with no destination and reported (`OutlineItem`, `Cleared`, under its input), `MA-0003`'s on the copy of its page (D7-2's rule, D7-33).

**A-67 · Links and comments come with their pages** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.links"`.
**Expect** A link between two pages of one source lands on the copy **by identity**; every comment of every input present on its page, its thread intact. **If it fails** S1.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* `render.merge.links ...` over `merge-a`, `merge-b`, `merge-c` and `annots-on-pages.pdf`: each link read from the merged bytes (PDFium reads links nowhere this product reaches -- `tst_reference_repair`'s reason) and graded by the identity PDFium extracts where it lands: *"A-67: 7 links landed by identity, 11 comments on their pages"* -- every comment found by its `/NM` on the page showing its label's identity, each reply's `/IRT` and each note's `/Popup` naming an annotation of that page. A link to a page a range left out is A-66's range case.

**A-68 · Colliding fields: renamed, or merged on purpose, and reported** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.fields"` with `documents/renameCollidingFields` each way.
**Expect** Renamed: every field present, the colliding top-level names suffixed, each widget's `/Parent` chain consistent, PDFium's form layer reading every name as the report states. Merged: one field per colliding name whose widgets share its value. **Either way the report lists every collision.** **If it fails** S1 for a field or value lost; S2 otherwise.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* **Renamed** (`unit.merge.fields renamed ...`, against the three labels with the renames the policy implies): *"11 fields, 5 collisions renamed"* -- `name_2`, `date_2` (B), `name_3`, `date_3`, `address_2` (C) -- every value the source's, every widget's `/Parent` naming the node whose `/Kids` lists it; the report's collisions exactly those, input by input. **Merged** (`unit.merge.fields merged on purpose ...`): *"7 fields, 5 collisions merged"* -- `name` and `date` three widgets each, the FIRST source's value (*Ana Lima*, *2026-10-01*) and one appearance on all three, `address` holding B's `street` and C's `city`, `/NeedAppearances` set (D7-33(d)). **Through PDFium's form layer** (`render.merge.fields ...`, `IDocumentBackend::searchUnits`): the renamed document's 11 fully qualified names and values as stated, the merged one's 7 with one value per field across its widgets; and the renamed document's **9 pages drawn as their sources at 72 dpi, 0 differ**, widgets included. A file merged with itself three times renames every top-level name `_2`, `_3`.

**A-69 · Layers unioned** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10 -- first-party, `LayerTree` being T17's*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.layers"` over `layers.pdf` and `merge-a.pdf`.
**Expect** Every input's groups in `/OCGs`, `/D`'s `/Order` grouped per input, `/ON`/`/OFF`/`/Locked` unioned; `LayerTree` (T17) or, before it, the first-party reader lists them as the labels say. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* `unit.merge.layers ...` over `layers.pdf` and `merge-a.pdf`, read first-party against `labels/assembly-layers.json` and merge-a's label: `/OCGs` the **10** groups of both -- the label-only parent *Annotations* included, which no page reaches (merge takes a document's layers whole, `ObjectGraphCopier::Options::allLayers`, D7-33(e)) -- `/Order` two labelled arrays, *layers* and *merge-a*, each holding its input's groups; `/OFF` *French* and *Print only*, `/Locked` *Base drawing*, `/RBGroups` the *English*/*French* pair, `/AS` kept. Its first run read 9 groups -- the copier's T5 narrowing to the groups a page reaches, which a merge must not apply -- and the option was added inside the task.

**A-70 · Skipped inputs are reported with their reason** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.skipped"`: an encrypted input, a file that is not a PDF, a truncated PDF.
**Expect** The merge completes with the others; the report names each skipped input and its reason by key. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* `unit.merge.skipped ...`: `merge-a`, `encrypted/aes-128.pdf`, a path with no file, `merge-not-a-pdf.pdf`, `malformed/truncated-mid-stream.pdf`, `merge-b` -- merged 2 inputs, 5 pages; skipped, by input and key, `error.pdf.encrypted`, `error.open.notFound`, `error.open.notPdf`, `error.merge.repairedWithLoss`, each with its path; inputs that are all skipped refuse the whole by `error.merge.nothingMerged`. **The window** (`ui.merge.documents refused by the backend ...`, `ui.merge.dialog lists each file ...`): a refusal said and nothing opened; a file the merge will skip already says so in the dialog's row.

**A-71 · A separator page between inputs** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10*
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.separator"`.
**Expect** One blank page between consecutive inputs, at the size of the page before it, none after the last. **If it fails** S3.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* `unit.merge.separator ...`: A, B, C with separators -- 11 pages, the blanks at 4 and 7 with no `/Contents` or `/Annots`, the MediaBox of `MA-0003` and `MB-0002` as labelled, none after `MC-0004`; C then A -- the blank after C **A4, its last page's size, not its first page's A5** (added when the first order's controls could not tell the two apart: every page of A and of B is one size); without separators 9 pages.

**A-72 · A 30-way merge, inside its bound** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10* — §3.5
**Steps** `ctest -R "merge.scale" -V` (`[run-serial]`).
**Expect** §3.5's four bounds, each printed; the result graded by A-47 and A-66. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** ✅ *2026-11-09, T10.* `ui.composition.merge.scale a 30-way merge, inside its bound` (`[run-serial]`, `x64-release`, the product as `main.cpp` composes it, the dialog's twin answering): *"A-72: 31 inputs, 990 pages -- wall 0.24 s, live-heap peak +17.5 MB (before 39.1 MB), UI gap during the merge 11 ms, the open after it 178 ms, cancel to stopped 53 ms; 30 bookmarks landed by identity"* -- against §3.5's 30 s, 1 GiB, 100 ms and 1 s; the merged document opened with no repair, 31 bookmarks one per input in order, each identity input's on its first page by the identity PDFium extracts; the cancelled run left no tab and no file. **The open after the merge** -- the engine opening a 990-page document and its tab built, ONE event on the UI thread as any *File › Open* is -- is printed beside the bound, not under it: §3.5 bounds the thread *during the merge*. The live heap is sampled on the UI thread while the merge runs, every twentieth pass, and once the tab is open. **The dialog with thirty files** -- each counted as it is added, on the UI thread -- is M-03's.

**T10's planted controls** (`pdf/ops/DocumentMerger`, `ObjectGraphCopier`, `core/doc/MergeRunner`, `ui/dialogs/MergeDialog`, `ui/shell/MainWindowMerge.cpp`; each built, its build's exit code read, run against its suite and restored): **P1** a rename joined with `-` (A-68), **P2** a merged widget keeping no `/Parent` (A-68), **P3** a file's bookmark on its LAST page (A-66), **P4** a cleared bookmark not reported (A-66), **P5** the layers' `/Order` not labelled per input (A-69), **P6** a separator after the last input (A-71), **P7** a separator sized from the input's first page (A-71 -- red only through the case's merge-c-then-a order, added for it), **P8** a lossy repair merged (A-70), **P10** the copy bringing no fields (A-68), **P11** the copy bringing only the layers a page reaches (A-69), **P12** the dialog dropping a typed range, **P13** the result opened as an attachment copy, **P14** a copied widget losing its field (A-68), **P15** a merged widget keeping its own appearance (A-68), **P16** a file's bookmark open (A-66) -- **all red**. **P9, the runner never stopping for a cancel, STAYED GREEN**: the runner also turns a run that ENDS cancelled into `error.merge.cancelled`, and the case's three 200 ms steps fit inside its one-second bound; the case now merges five inputs (seven steps, 1.4 s to the end) and counts the steps the backend ran, and P9 is red.

### 5.13 Bookmarks and destinations (T11, T12)

**A-73 · The whole Bookmarks pane table, from key events** ✅ *2026-11-10, T11*
**Steps** `ctest -R "ui.bookmarks_edit"`: each row of `Features.md` §8's **Bookmarks pane** table through `ShortcutBinder`.
**Expect** `F2` renames in place; `Delete` asks about children; `Ctrl+B` adds from the current view; `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` demote and promote; `Alt+↑`/`Alt+↓` move among siblings; `Ctrl+Shift+D` sets the destination to the current view — each one undo step; the navigation rows Stage 4 registered unchanged. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/ui/tst_bookmarksedit.cpp` (`ui.bookmarks_edit: ...`, four cases), every key sent to the focused widget through `ShortcutBinder`, each read back from the JOURNAL: `F2` opens the editor on the selected row and `Enter` commits ONE `EditBookmarkCommand` (an editor closed unchanged commits nothing); `Delete` asks about children -- kept, promoted into the place, then deleted with them -- and a leaf is deleted unasked; `Tab` makes the bookmark the last child of the one before it and `Shift+Tab` the next sibling of its parent, the moved bookmark kept selected; `Alt+Up`/`Alt+Down` swap it with its neighbour; `Ctrl+B` from the pane adds a bookmark to the current view after the selected one, its title open for typing; `Ctrl+Shift+D` sets the destination to the current view; each ONE step of history; a key with nowhere to go DISABLED (convention 16) and nothing done; `Right`/`Left` unchanged, and no step. A drop requests one move and a drop onto the item's own child is refused by `error.outline.cycle` -- **through `BookmarksPane::requestDropAt`, which `dropEvent` calls: the offscreen platform has no drag manager, so a synthetic `QDropEvent` never reaches the view (convention 19); M-05 drags with a mouse**. An outline whose readings disagree: header showing the reason, rows not editable, every editing action disabled, `F2` and `Tab` doing nothing; the same outline agreeing, editable and the header hidden. The properties dialog's answer -- bold, a colour, a URI action -- is ONE step through a journal group, undone whole. `tst_panes`' Stage 4 control now presses the eight rows on a document the fake backend will not edit: each registered, disabled, consumed, nothing changed. **Found by its first run, before any commit:** the rename editor lost `Enter` to `bookmark.goTo` and `Tab` to `bookmark.demote` -- a `QLineEdit` claims the editing keys through `ShortcutOverride` but not those -- so a typed title was never committed; the pane's delegate now makes an editor that claims `Enter`, `Esc` and `Tab` (`ui/panes/BookmarksPane.cpp`). **Controls:** P8 (the editor not claiming `Enter`) red -- the case failed at the commit and the process ended without its summary line; P12 (`Tab` placing the bookmark first, not last) red.

**Result** `tst_viewercommands`' census: **53 Viewer rows registered and 0 not** (52 and 1 since Stage 6 T9). `tests/ui/tst_bookmarksedit.cpp` (`ui.viewer_commands: Ctrl+B with the page focused ...`): with the page view focused on page 6, `Ctrl+B` is one step, the new bookmark created (a key, not an object), after the bookmark the pane has selected, going to the current page at the view's zoom (`/XYZ`, its zoom the controller's), and not opened for typing -- that is the pane's `Ctrl+B`. `--list-actions`: **396 declared, 206 registered, 190 not** (392 / 193 / 199 at T10): T11 registers the eight editing rows, `bookmark.generate`, and the minted `.properties`, `.import`, `.export` and `.tableOfContents`.

After every command saved, **each item's `/Parent`, `/Prev`, `/Next`, `/First`, `/Last` and signed `/Count`, and the root's, read from the dictionaries as written and consistent**; a move makes no new object; undone before a save, nothing written; after one, the tree written back. A move onto its own descendant, and onto itself, refused by `error.outline.cycle`, the model and the update unchanged; the control, onto a sibling's child, runs. **PDFium's half** (`tests/render/tst_outlinereader.cpp`): a create, a reparent, a delete, an open-state edit and a page move made through the shipped backend, saved, and read by a fresh PDFium -- the tree, titles, open states and every page as the model has them; the new bookmark landing on the page that shows its identity after the move; the backend's own outline after the show the model's; the saved file's two readings agreeing. `serialize:`/`revert:` for the six commands: the registry sweep (two each, nine targets) and the reversibility sweep -- **6,174 combinations over 121 editable corpus files, 0 failures**. **Controls:** P7 (no cycle check) red; P9 (the sink preferring its own target over the disk's) red in the reversibility sweep -- an undo wrote an update; P10 (a page delete's undo not putting back the outline) red there too; P11 (the backend's outline not the sink's after a show) red.

**Steps** `ctest -R "outline.toc"`.
**Expect** One inserted page, one undo step; each entry's link lands on the page its bookmark names **by identity**; its text drawn in D6-7's fonts, a title it cannot draw refused by name (Q5(e) ✅); golden. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/render/tst_outlinegenerate.cpp` (`outline.toc: ...`), the shipped backend: `refs-every-kind.pdf`'s seven bookmarks made a contents page, inserted at the front by ONE `InsertPagesCommand`, **then a target page moved to the end** -- a link that named its page by index would now land on the wrong one -- and saved. Read back: the page's text holds the heading and each title; **each of its 7 links, by `/Dest`'s page reference, lands on the page that shows its bookmark's identity** (the bookmarks are titled with the identity they target, D7-17). The move and the insert undone, the page count as it was. A title of `Ω` refused by `error.edit.typedCharacterRefused` (Q5(e)); a Traditional Chinese title drawn. **Deviation:** no golden image -- the page is graded by what PDFium extracts from it and where its links land (D7-34(i)). **Control:** P4 (the payload's links left by index) red.

**A-81 · Import and export as indented text and as XML** ✅ *2026-11-10, T11*
**Steps** `ctest -R "outline.exchange"`.
**Expect** Export then import gives the same tree (titles, depth, targets); a malformed file refused by key with nothing changed; **an XML external entity never resolved** (a planted entity naming a scratch file is not read). Written through `pdf/ops` (A-09). **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/unit/tst_outlineedit.cpp` (`outline.exchange: ...`), through the backend's `writeOutlineExchange` / `readOutlineExchange` (`pdf/ops/OutlineFiles`, so no waiver -- layer-lint still 15 escapes): `refs-every-kind.pdf`'s outline, a bookmark given a colour and bold, exported, the outline deleted, the file imported as ONE `GenerateBookmarksCommand` -- **the same titles, depths, pages and fits, in text and in XML**, every item new; XML keeping the colour and the style; a second export refused by `error.outline.exportExists`. Ten hostile files refused by `error.outline.importMalformed` with the model unchanged: a skipped depth, a page that is no number, a view that is no view, a line of two fields, **an XML external entity naming a scratch file** -- refused whole at its DTD, so its words appear nowhere -- an internal entity, an element that is no bookmark, a colour that is no colour, an unclosed file, an action of kind `other`; the control, a good file, read, its page past the last kept going nowhere. **Control:** P6 (a DTD allowed) red.

**A-82 · Named destinations created, renamed, deleted** ✅ *2026-11-12, T12*
**Steps** `ctest -R "destinations"`; `serialize:`/`revert:` for the three commands; over `dests-named.pdf`.
**Expect** Created from the current view; renamed with every link and bookmark naming it renamed in the same command; deleted with the links naming it cleared and reported; the tree written flat, a pair not changed written as it was; listed and sorted by name and by page. **If it fails** S1 for a reference broken; S2 otherwise.
**Result** `tests/unit/tst_destinations.cpp` (`destinations: ...`, seven cases) through the shipped sink, read back first-party (`pdf/ops/DestinationReader`): `dests-named.pdf`'s six entries read in their tables -- four in the tree, two in the `/Dests` dictionary, the ghost kept unresolved -- and listed by name and by page, the unresolved one last. A destination CREATED from a view of page 3, one renamed in each table and one deleted from each, saved: **the tree one flat node**, its keys sorted by their bytes; each entry in the table it was in; **every pair not changed -- the `<< /D [...] >>` form and the ghost's dangling reference among them -- the very value the file had, and a renamed one's value its old one**, only its key new; the made one naming page 3 by reference at its view. **Undone after the save**, all four: the six entries back, each its own value and table. An undo before a save writes nothing, and seven refusals by key change nothing (`error.destination.nameTaken`, `.badName` twice -- a CJK name, a padded one -- `.badDestination`, `.noSuchName` twice, and a rename onto a name taken). Over `refs-every-kind.pdf`: a **rename of `dest-beta` renames the link that names it (object 21) and a bookmark made to name it, one step** -- the link written `/Dest (dest-beta-2)`, a string, the tree's form; **a delete of `old-delta` clears its link (22) and says so** (`clearedLinks`: object 22), the link written with no target, the other named links not rewritten; both undone after the save, the link written `/Dest /old-delta` again -- a NAME, the dictionary's form -- and `/Dest (dest-beta)`. A page command remaps the table; a delete leaves an entry unresolved, the save leaves it out, and the undo after the save puts it back at its page. A table planted read-only refuses all three commands by `error.destination.readingsDisagree`; twelve hostile journal lines refused by `Result`. `serialize:`/`revert:` for the three commands: the registry sweep and the reversibility sweep (A-88's run). **PDFium's half** (`tests/render/tst_destinations.cpp`): the shipped backend's two readings agree on **both corpus files that carry named destinations** -- `dests-named.pdf` and `refs-every-kind.pdf`, measured -- and a planted disagreement (a name, a page, an entry missing) is refused by key; a table created, renamed and deleted, a page then moved, and saved, read by a fresh PDFium: every name to the page that **shows** its identity, the saved file's two readings agreeing, and the renamed link FOLLOWED (`core::followLink`) to P-0007. **The pane's half** (`tests/ui/tst_linktool.cpp`, `ui.destinations ...`): create from the current view, rename and delete from the pane's selection, each one step, the pane showing the model after each and after each undo. **Controls:** P2 (the agreement not comparing pages) red; P3 (a pair not changed written afresh) red; P4 (a rename leaving its links) red.

**A-130 · A double-click goes to a bookmark, and a second one there renames it** ✅ *2026-11-22, D7-40*
**Steps** `ctest -R "bookmarks_edit: a double-click"`: the mouse sent to the pane's viewport -- press, release, double-click, release -- over the fake backend's three-level outline.
**Expect** The first double-click on B goes to its page and opens nothing; the second, the view unmoved, opens the rename editor; a view moved since, or another bookmark selected in between, makes the next double-click go and only the one after rename; a double-click on another bookmark goes there; `Enter` always goes; a read-only outline goes and never renames; no step of history for any of it. **If it fails** S2 -- a rename where a person meant to go is a title typed over.
**Result** `tests/ui/tst_bookmarksedit.cpp`, `[A-130][D7-40]`, two cases, every check above as written. *What it does not cover* (convention 19): events sent to the viewport below Windows' input queue, so the platform's double-click interval and a pointer moved between clicks are not here -- a person's double-click is the walk's. *Controls* (planted, each built and the cases run): P1 the second double-click never renaming, P2 a moved view still renaming, P3 another bookmark selected forgetting nothing -- red each, at its own check. *Runs:* 2026-11-22, after the controls and a full rebuild: **`x64-release` 2122/2122 (285 s)**, six named skips; `checks` clean; `x64-debug` not run.

**A-131 · A right-click selects a bookmark without going there and offers its four rows** ✅ *2026-11-22, D7-40*

**Expect** The bookmark under the pointer selected and the view where it was; the menu's rows *Rename*, *Set Destination*, *Delete* -- each with its chord after a tab and no shortcut -- a separator, then *Bookmark Properties…* with none; *Set Destination* points the bookmark at the view the right-click left alone, one step; *Rename* opens the editor; *Delete* asks the key's question about children, one step; *Properties* opens on the bookmark the menu was opened on; the Menu key opens it on the current row; no menu past every row or on a read-only outline. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/ui/tst_bookmarksedit.cpp`, `[A-131][D7-40]`, and the read-only half in A-130's second case: every check above as written. *What it does not cover*: the menu is read and its rows triggered, never clicked. *Controls*: P4 the right-click going to the bookmark -- red, the view on page 3 and B set to its own page -- and P5 the menu without *Set Destination* -- red, three rows.

### 5.14 Links (T12)

**A-83 · A link drawn, or made from text, covers what was meant** ✅ *2026-11-12, T12*
**Steps** `ctest -R "ui.linktool"`.
**Expect** A dragged rectangle written as dragged (rule 6, on the raster); a link from a text selection one per line, its quadpoints on the **glyphs' ink**; each made by one `AddAnnotation` with `/Subtype /Link`. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/ui/tst_linktool.cpp` (`ui.linktool ...`): the tool alone hands its host the page-space box, a click and a drag under the marquee's threshold and an `Esc` mid-drag nothing; through the window, `link.create` with no text selected ARMS the Link tool, a drag asks where the link goes once and makes **ONE** `AddAnnotationCommand` -- a `Link` on page 1 within a device pixel of the two corners dragged between, its target, border, dash and highlight as answered -- undone in one step, and a cancelled dialog makes nothing. **Through the product** (`tests/composition/tst_linkproduct.cpp`, real engine): `link.create` with three lines of `text-a4.pdf` selected by a drag of the Selection tool makes **three links, one per line, one step**, every selected glyph's box inside exactly one link's quadrilateral; the Link tool's rectangle dragged on `refs-none.pdf` written within 1.5 device pixels of where it was dragged, and **on the raster** the engine draws its border red along all four edges -- *"136 of 136 edge samples red"* -- and nothing inside it. **Bound, at the case:** the from-text quadrilaterals are graded on the glyphs' BOXES, the line's extent Stage 6's markup path takes (guide A-49 grades that path's ink); a link has no fill to grade them on.

**A-84 · URLs found in text, in one step** ✅ *2026-11-12, T12* *(over a page the case builds -- D7-35(i))*

**Expect** Exactly the labelled URLs linked, no near-miss; one command, one undo step. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/unit/tst_links.cpp` (`links.autodetect: ...`): over a page model of seven lines -- four addresses (`https://` with a query, `mailto:`, `www.` followed by a full stop, one in brackets with brackets of its own), two on one line in capitals, and a line each of near-misses: `example.com`, `http:/broken.example`, `www.nodot`, `http://localhost`, `ftp://files.example.com`, a bare `emailme@example.com`, `xhttps://bad.example` -- **exactly the six addresses**, each quadrilateral on its glyphs' boxes, `www.` opened as `http://`; through the shipped sink as **ONE** `AddAnnotationCommand`, each read back with its `/URI` and quadrilateral; a second run finds every address linked; one undo puts the model back. **The engine's half** (`tests/composition/tst_linkproduct.cpp`): a page of the same lines in Helvetica, BUILT by the case and opened through the product, `link.autoDetect` from the window -- the pages read on the render thread -- makes **exactly the four addresses on it, one step**, and a second run nothing. **Deviation:** no corpus fixture -- the page is the case's own (D7-35(i)). **Control:** P7 (a sentence's punctuation kept in an address) red.

**A-85 · Following a link — and what is never done** ✅ *2026-11-12, T12*
**Steps** `ctest -R "links.follow"` with `Services::openExternally` replaced by a recorder.
**Expect** `GoTo` and a named destination navigate to the page **by identity**; `GoToR` asks, then opens the file in a tab at its destination; `URI` asks, then reaches the recorder once; `Launch` of an executable refused by D6-17's list with **nothing** recorded; a JavaScript, form, media or print action does nothing and says so. **The control:** `objdump -p rawpage.exe` and the PDFium DLL import no script engine (PA-4 asserts the build). **If it fails** S1 for a program launched; S2 otherwise.
**Result** `tests/unit/tst_links.cpp` (`links.follow: ...`): `core::followLink` over every kind -- a page and a name navigate (the name looked up in the table as it stands), a page or name gone goes nowhere and says which; another PDF and a file ASK; six names Windows runs (`setup.exe`, `Setup.EXE`, `evil.exe.`, `tool.bat`, `script.ps1`, `short.lnk`) refused as a `GoToR` and as a `Launch`; four web schemes followed and `www.` taken as `http://`, six refused (`javascript:`, `file:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, `ms-settings:`, a bare `example.com`); a script, four form actions, a menu command and an `Other` NOT RUN, each saying which. **Through the product** (`tests/composition/tst_linkproduct.cpp`, the shell a recorder): on `refs-every-kind.pdf` with the Hand tool, a click on each of the five links by reference, by GoTo, by name in the tree, in the dictionary and through a GoTo action lands on the page that **shows** the label's identity; a URL link asked -- *no* hands the recorder nothing, *yes* the address **once**; `calc.exe` refused unasked; a script whose planted side effect is a URL handed to the shell NOT RUN, the recorder unchanged; a print not run; a file asked and handed over by its path beside the document; the `GoToR` asked and its file -- this one, its tab in front -- at the page showing P-0005; a drag from a link pans and follows nothing; `Enter` with a link selected follows it. **The control:** `objdump -p` over `build/x64-release/bin/rawpage.exe` and `pdfium.dll`, read 2026-11-12 -- `rawpage.exe` imports `Qt6Core`, `Qt6Gui`, `Qt6Multimedia`, `Qt6Widgets`, `libstdc++-6`, `libgcc_s_seh-1`, `libwinpthread-1`, `msvcrt`, `pdfium.dll`, `libz`, `libjpeg-62`, `libopenjp2` and the Windows system DLLs `KERNEL32`, `ADVAPI32`, `USER32`, `SHELL32`, `SHCORE`, `DWrite` and `WTSAPI32`; `pdfium.dll` imports `KERNEL32`, `ADVAPI32`, `GDI32` and `USER32` alone -- **no script engine in either** (no `v8`, `jscript`, `chakra`); `PA-4` asserts the non-V8 build at configure. **Controls:** P5 (a program not refused) red; P6 (any scheme followed) red; P10 (a URL opened whatever the answer) red; P13 (a drag from a link followed) red -- its first plant did not COMPILE (`-Werror=unused-variable`), was recorded as no result, and was planted again.

**Result** `tests/unit/tst_links.cpp` (`links.appearance: ...`): twenty links -- five border styles by four highlight modes, three widths, two colours -- and an invisible one, one command, saved and read back first-party: each width, style, dash array, colour and `/H` as written, each target a `/Dest` naming page 2 by REFERENCE with no `/A`, each with an `/AP` of its own -- the invisible one's draws nothing (D7-35(g)). The dialog's answer (`tests/ui/tst_linktool.cpp`): each target kind and each appearance field read from its widget, invert (ISO's default) written as no `/H`. **What PDFium draws:** a link RawPage makes carries its border in its appearance, so the engine draws it -- graded where it is ours, A-83's *"136 of 136 edge samples red"*; a producer's link with no `/AP` is drawn by PDFium as nothing, by Acrobat and Firefox from `/BS` and `/C` -- M-06's.
