**A-88 · Links ride on Stage 6's commands** ✅ *2026-11-12, T12*
**Steps** `ctest -R "serialize:AddAnnotationCommand|revert:EditAnnotationCommand|reversibility"` with Link payloads; `mutation-lint --explain`.
**Expect** Every link field round-trips through the existing commands; reversible over the corpus; no link command class exists (D7-12). **If it fails** S2.
**Result** The registry sweep (`tests/unit/tst_command_serialize.cpp`) carries a link of **every target a command may carry, and `Other` as an annotation carries it**, each with a dashed border, a colour and a highlight, through `AddAnnotationCommand`'s JSON and back, identically -- 89 round-trips in all. The reversibility sweep gains links (one to the last page with a dashed border and a highlight, one from a line of text to a web address) in `annot.add`, every link on a page retargeted twice in `annot.edit`, and the three destination commands: **6,520 combinations over 121 editable corpus files, 0 failures** on both presets (6,174 at T11). `tests/unit/tst_links.cpp` (`links: ...`): a link the file holds RECOLOURED keeps its whole dictionary -- key for key, in order, but `/C` -- its `/Dest` the file's bytes; RETARGETED, written `/Dest` naming page 3 by reference at its `/XYZ`, no `/A`; a page move after rewrites neither link; a delete of the retargeted page leaves it unresolved in the model and with no target in the file, and its undo after the save writes the reference back; **a link MADE to a page, that page deleted and the file saved, then undone, is written to its page again** (the sink keeps such a target across the save, D7-35(f)); a link to a page a duplicate made before any save written to it; a page past the last refused. `mutation-lint --explain`: **no link command class** -- the 30 commands are Stage 6's nine annotation and two attachment commands, the page and bookmark commands, and the three destination commands. **Controls:** P1 (every link's target rewritten from the model) **STAYED GREEN** against the case's first check -- link 19's `/Dest` is its last key and a `/Fit` array rewritten is the same bytes -- and stayed green when the case compared the whole dictionary in order; it went **red** once the case also recoloured link 20, whose target is a GoTo ACTION a rewrite turns into a `/Dest`, and once P1's run took in the orphan check below; P9 (a target whose page is gone not kept across a save) red; P11 (a page command not remapping a link) red; P14 (an unresolved target compared by page object again) red. **Found writing this case's neighbours, before any commit:** a link to a page OUTSIDE the tree, recoloured, lost its producer's `/Dest` -- the sink compared the disk's page object with the model's, which holds none for an unresolved target; compared now by where each points (`refs-by-index.pdf`'s orphan link, `links.verify`'s case).

### 5.15 A custom stamp from a PDF page (T13)

**A-89 · The stamp shows the page** ✅ *2026-11-13, T13* *(its raster bar REGISTERED to one device pixel, the user's choice that day -- I-21)*
**Steps** `ctest -R "stamps.page_source"`.
**Expect** A stamp made from page 3 of `refs-every-kind.pdf`, placed at its natural size, rasterizes within `golden/README.md`'s thresholds of the source page at that scale and shows its identity; made through `CustomStampDialog`, which now lists PDF files. ***Amended 2026-11-13*** (the user's choice, I-21): graded at 400 %, each stamp pixel against the nearest of the source's 3 × 3 around it -- at most 1 % above 16 per channel and **none** above 96 -- the golden thresholds printed beside it every run and not graded. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** **The ink** (`tests/composition/tst_stampink.cpp`, `stamps.page_source: ...`, real engine): made AS THE DIALOG MAKES IT -- the backend's `stampPage` on page 3, then `MainWindow::addCustomStamp`, which keeps it in a scratch library, chooses it in the palette and arms the tool -- its picture drawn on the render thread and kept, 128 pixels tall; one CLICK of the tool places it, one command, a page stamp 216 points tall. Placed on a blank Letter page shown at **400 %** with its corner on whole device pixels, at its natural size ROUNDED to the 611 × 864 pixels the engine draws the page into (152.73 × 216 points; the engine sizes a page by rounding up), against page 3 drawn by a fresh engine into the same pixels, which the engine reads as **P-0003**: *"registered to a pixel, 0.000378857 % above 16, 0 % above 96, the worst 19; unregistered (I-21, not graded), 0.645382 % above 8, 0.147186 % above 96"*. **Control:** page 4 graded as page 3 -- *"0.166697 % above 16, 0.0583439 % above 96"* -- fails the registered bar. **Why 400 %:** graded first at 100 %, planted P1 -- every reference of the page's form written as `null`, so PDFium set the text in its stock Helvetica, regular for bold -- **STAYED GREEN** there: a pixel's registration forgave a stroke one pixel lighter. At 400 % the same plant is red. **The dialog** (`tests/ui/tst_stamps.cpp`, `stamps.page_source: ...`): a fourth source, *PDF Page*, whose browser lists PDF files and the image browser still none (A-78); the page read through the window's reader AS IT IS CHOSEN -- page 3 of 12, its number and size said under it, named for its file and page, filed under the person's category -- a page the reader refuses refused by its key under the source with OK disabled, a name the person typed kept as pages change, a file that is no PDF refused by name; through the window a page stamp kept, listed, chosen with its page read ONCE and placed by the tool as a page stamp, sized as an image is. **The file** (`tests/unit/tst_stamps.cpp`, `stamps.page_source: ...`): sizes read as shown -- A4, a page turned 90 (stated and inherited), a CropBox inside the MediaBox (576 × 756) and one past it cut to it (400 × 641.89); sized up to 72 and down to 216; two pages, no PDF and a size no page has refused by key; the page-to-box matrix lands each corner where a reader shows it at 0, 90, 180 and 270; the form over every page of three files -- `decorated-elsewhere.pdf`'s ARRAYS of contents joined into one -- is a closed graph whose content decodes to the page's own; a page stamp travels in a journal line, six hostile ones refused (`[serialize:AddAnnotationCommand]`), and a recovery replays one turned and undone to the same model and bytes. **Deviation:** no golden baseline of a PDF-page stamp (the plan's §5.5 names one): the oracle is the source page drawn by the engine, which a stored capture of our own output would be weaker than.

*I-31 (2026-11-22):* the palette's picture is graded on its PIXELS as well as its size -- against page 3 drawn by a fresh engine at the picture's scale, registered to a pixel as the stamp is (*"0.403503 % above 16, 0 % above 96, the worst 22"*); the control, an inkless picture, fails it (*"1.14183 % above 16, 0.137363 % above 96"*). Page 4 -- the stamp's own control -- cannot be this one's: at 128 pixels the identity is some seven pixels tall and a 3 and a 4 are inside a pixel's registration (*"0.455014 % above 16, 0 % above 96"*, measured). Its plant -- `drawStampPicture` back on its old line -- red.

**A-90 · Placed twice, written once; kept across processes** ✅ *2026-11-13, T13*
**Steps** `ctest -R "stamps.page_source_shared|profile_store.restart"`.
**Expect** Two placements share one form XObject graph in the saved file (counted); the stamp library lists it in a second process. **If it fails** S2.
**Result** `tests/unit/tst_stamps.cpp` (`stamps.page_source_shared: ...`): page 3 placed on page 1 of `text-a4.pdf` and, turned 30 degrees, on page 2 -- two commands, one save: two appearances, each naming **one** form object (*"two placements of page 3, one form object 16, 2 font(s) of the page's written once"*); the saved file holds **exactly the document's fonts plus the page's two** and **one** form of the page's box; the turn in the placement's `/Matrix`, not the page's; read back, the stamp is FOREIGN -- no page in the model, a turn refused. `unit.profile_store.restart`: the writer adds a page stamp with its picture beside Stage 6's library -- **8 files**, 5 before -- and a second process reads the page byte for byte, its size and its picture; the empty-root control still fails the reader. `tests/unit/tst_profilestore.cpp` (`[A-90]`): a page with no picture whole; a picture that is no PNG refused; a damaged picture no picture and the stamp still loading; a damaged page reported by `pageOf`, a size no page has reported at load by name; removed, all three files gone; a page that is no page, or no size, refused before anything is written.

**A-133 · The properties bar shows itself, floats over the page, and `Ctrl+E` is gone** ✅ *2026-11-22, D7-42*
**Steps** `ctest -R "shows itself for an armed tool"` (`tests/ui/tst_annotoverlay.cpp`, `[A-45][D7-42]` -- Stage 6's A-45 case, rewritten); `ctest -R "twelve Write-mode collisions|resolve through the viewer"` (`tests/unit/tst_keybindings.cpp`).
**Expect** Hidden with nothing armed or selected, and `Ctrl+E` bringing nothing in the Viewer or in Annotation tools; an armed tool shows it with the tool's defaults, a selection with its properties, the selection cleared with the tool armed back to the defaults, and the tool put away with nothing selected hides it; the page's place and size the same with the bar shown as without, the bar over the page's top edge and across its width; a control of the bar holding the focus when the bar goes gives it to the page; `Ctrl+E` bound in neither context, §8's collisions 8 + 4 + 0 = 12, and `view.togglePropertiesBar` and `annot.properties` unregistered (the window's 212). **If it fails** S3.
**Result** Every check above as written, offscreen. *What it does not cover* (convention 19): the bar's strip over the page is graded for place and size, never for what it hides or a press landing on it, and the platform's real focus chain is offscreen's. *Controls*: P4 the bar never showing -- red; P5 the bar back in the column -- red, the page moved and shrank; P6 an armed tool alone showing nothing -- red; P7 the bar never hiding -- red; P8 the focus not given back -- red, on a button elsewhere; P9 `Ctrl+E` still bound in the Viewer -- red in `tst_keybindings`, the census at 13. The first run found the focus lost BEFORE the bar hid -- a change of subject disables the focused control and Qt moves the focus on -- and the case was right: the bar now reads its focus before its controls change.

### 5.16 Headers, footers, Bates (T14)

**A-91 · Apply, then remove: byte-identical** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14* — §3.8
**Steps** `ctest -R "decoration.roundtrip"` for headers, footers and Bates over every editable corpus file.
**Expect** §3.8's bar. **If it fails** S1.
**Result (T14)** First-party half (`tests/unit/tst_decoration.cpp`): over **every editable corpus file** -- a header and footer with *shrink* (unshrunk where a page has no room, refused by key and applied again), saved; a Bates number, saved; the header removed, saved, the Bates number still listed; the Bates number removed, saved -- each page, its `/Contents` and its `/Resources` (a reference's object too) **equal to the original's in value on every page of every file**, none refused; repaired files saved as Save As requires. Engine half (`tests/render/tst_decorationengine.cpp`): the same cycle over every editable file, its first and last page **0 differing pixels** at 72 dpi against the original. Also: a removal undone AFTER a save lists the very streams it took, by reference, and the apply undone after its save leaves the pages as they were; *update* leaves ONE header on every page and one undo puts the odd pages' back; a remove that finds nothing is refused by key with no undo step; a page whose `/Contents` is a reference to an ARRAY object and whose `/Resources` two pages share comes back exactly, the shared object never edited (built in the case -- no corpus file has either shape; planted control P2 found that gap, and **I-34**). And through the product: `ui.composition.assembly_shown` with four more steps -- a shrunk header and footer on the odd pages, Bates numbers, the header updated, the numbers removed -- each page drawn as saved and every undo as the original. **Deviations:** the raster half reads the first and last page of each file, not every page (§3.8 says every; the object half reads every page, and a removal that leaves the objects equal in value draws them as they were); and it takes PDFium's first unknown-font substitution before its sweep (**I-14**, met again here: `malformed/bad-name-hex-escape.pdf` ~54,000 pixels apart on each page with its objects equal).

**A-92 · Every token, on every page** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14*
**Steps** `ctest -R "unit.headerfooter"` with a fixed clock and file name.
**Expect** Page number, count, label, date, time, filename, path, custom text and Bates expanded per page against hand-written tables; extracted from the rendered page by PDFium where drawn. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T14)** `unit.headerfooter.fields` -- fourteen rows of a hand-written table (`<<page>>`, `<<count>>`, `<<label>>` and its fallback to the number, `<<date>>`, `<<date:dd MMM yyyy>>` in the C locale, `<<time>>`, `<<time:HH:mm:ss>>`, `<<file>>`, `<<path>>`, `<<bates>>` numbering and not, an unknown field and a lone `<<` left as typed) and the pages a range and odd or even reach; `unit.headerfooter.layout` -- each area's line where the spec puts it on the page AS SHOWN at 0, 90, 180 and 270 degrees, reading upright. PDFium (`render.headerfooter.fields`, a fresh engine on the saved file): on every page of `outline-labels.pdf` every area's expansion extracted -- the labels not the numbers there, `Page n of 3`, `2026-11-23 16:45`, the path -- and on `rotated-pages.pdf` (three rotations) `TOPMARK`'s glyph origins along the top of the page as shown, centred, `FOOTMARK`'s 30 points from the shown left edge, each within a point. Through the dialog (`ui.decorate the header/footer dialog …`): every field read into a spec, *Insert field* typing into the area last focused, and the preview's lines expanded for the page chosen -- a page the spec does not reach previewed bare.

**A-93 · Bates across open documents is one run** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14*
**Steps** `ctest -R "bates.across"`: three open documents, a chosen order.
**Expect** Consecutive numbers across the three in that order; the report's first and last per document equal what each document's pages show; each document's own undo removes only its numbers. **If it fails** S1 for a gap or a repeat.
**Result (T14)** `render.bates.across` -- `merge-b`, `merge-a`, `merge-c` in that order, each in its own journal, saved and read by a fresh engine: `CASE-000001` onwards with no gap or repeat across the three, each document's first and last page showing its share's first and last, and the middle document's undo taking its numbers alone. The allocator alone (`unit.bates.allocator`): consecutive shares, a refused document's share given back with no gap, a run past its digits refused with nothing taken. Through the window (`ui.decorate add, update, remove and Bates …`): two documents numbered in the order the dialog gives, one command in each journal, the report naming both shares, and `bates.remove` taking the front document's alone.

**A-94 · Another producer's decoration is not ours** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14*
**Steps** `ctest -R "decoration.foreign"` over `decorated-elsewhere.pdf`.
**Expect** Its header and watermark neither listed nor removed; applying ours beside them leaves theirs byte-identical. **If it fails** S1.
**Result (T14)** Neither page lists a decoration or wrapper of ours; both removes are refused by key (`error.decorate.noneFound`); ours applied beside theirs keeps every stream they list, in order, between our wrapper's pair, and both their forms (`Xi0`, `Xi1`) named; ours removed, each page, its `/Contents` and `/Resources` equal to the original's in value. Planted control P3 -- any unmarked stream at the end of `/Contents` read as ours -- red.

**A-95 · Shrinking content to clear the header** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14*
**Steps** `ctest -R "headerfooter.shrink"`.
**Expect** The page's ink and the header's do not overlap on the raster (rule 6); removing the header removes the wrapper too (§3.8). **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T14)** `render.headerfooter.shrink` on `split-blanks.pdf`, the header and footer placed OVER the first page's top and bottom ink, at 144 dpi, the header drawn alone on the same-sized blank page 3 as the oracle: shrunk, inside each line's box the decorated page draws exactly the header alone (0 pixels apart) and every pixel the content adds lies BETWEEN the two lines (0 outside); the CONTROL, unshrunk, > 50 pixels apart in both. The second half was added at T14 when planted control P7 -- the content placed below the footer, clear of both lines -- stayed green against the first. First-party: the content drawn through ONE wrapper marked with the header's own id, not a decoration, which its removal takes; the scale and place against hand values (`unit.headerfooter.shrink`), and a band that leaves no room refused by key.

**A-96 · Drawn text outside the fonts is refused by name** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14* *(Q5(e) ✅)*
**Steps** `ctest -R "decoration.characters"`.
**Expect** Latin and CJK drawn and read back by PDFium as typed; a Cyrillic letter refused at input with its name, nothing applied. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T14)** PDFium reads back, as typed, `Résumé àéîõü ß`, `第一章 概要`, `ページ 한국어` and `€ “quotes” — dash` from the saved page. A Cyrillic letter is refused by `error.decorate.characterRefused` naming it (`U+0413`, `U+0420`) -- by `core::validate`, in the dialog before OK closes it, and by the command with nothing applied and no undo step; a Bates prefix too (`U+03A9`); and a FIELD that expands to one -- a file named in Cyrillic -- is refused by the layout, in the dialog and by the command. The key is a page's refusal, not Stage 6's comment sentence; the rule and the naming are D6-7's.

**A-97 · Profiles saved, loaded, deleted — across processes** 🟡 *2026-11-23, T14's and T15's kinds; T16's at its task*
**Steps** `ctest -R "profile_store"` for the three new kinds.
**Expect** Each kind round-trips its whole spec; a second process lists it; a case's profile root is a scratch directory. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T14, the header/footer kind)** `unit.profile_store.restart`: two header/footer profiles written by ONE process, each whole spec read back by ANOTHER under a scratch root, and the empty-root control refused. In-process (`[A-97]`): saved again under its name REPLACES it (one file), deleted, a damaged file named and skipped while the other loads, the stamp library beside it untouched. Through the dialog: saved from the fields, chosen to fill them again, deleted.
**Result (T15, the watermark kind)** The same restart carries two watermark profiles -- a print-only two-line text one and an image one whose picture is past the 64 KiB a header/footer profile may be -- each whole spec, the picture's bytes included, read back by the second process (`unit.profile_store.restart`). In-process: beside the header/footer ones and apart from them (removing one kind's never takes the other's), a new store reads them. Through the dialog: an image profile saved, chosen to fill the fields again with its picture, deleted; a profile of a picture KEPT from the document refused by key (`error.decorate.profileKeepsPicture`), nothing saved. Planted control P12 -- a watermark profile read at the header/footer bound -- red.

**A-98 · Headers and footers look as specified** ✅ *2026-11-23, T14*
**Steps** `ctest -R "render.golden.decoration"`.
**Expect** Each alignment, font size, colour, underline and edge distance within `golden/README.md`'s thresholds; each baseline **looked at** before it is kept, recorded in `golden/UPDATES.md`. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T14)** Nine cases (`tests/golden/DecorationCases`), each page written THROUGH THE SINK and the writer and verified as a save verifies: the six areas, 24 points, red and underlined with a field, each edge its own distance, a page turned 90 and one 270, *shrink*, CJK with Latin, and a Bates number -- captured by `golden-update --decoration` against PDFium 153.0.8009.0, **each looked at before it was kept** (`golden/UPDATES.md`, 2026-11-22T16:36:44Z), and both directions held. The `cjk` case draws this machine's CJK faces and is declared machine-dependent (`render.golden.only the declared machine-dependent baselines …`).

### 5.17 Watermarks (T15)

**A-99 · Every source and placement** ✅ *2026-11-23, T15*
**Steps** `ctest -R "render.golden.watermark|watermark"`.
**Expect** Text, image and PDF-page sources; opacity, rotation, absolute and relative scale, anchor and offset, tiling — golden, looked at; **behind** is hidden where the page's own content is opaque and **in front** is not (measured on the raster). **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T15)** ✅ *2026-11-23.* **Golden** (`render.golden.watermark every case matches its baseline`): eight cases written THROUGH THE SINK -- DRAFT turned 30 degrees behind the bars, red COPY in front at half opacity, TL and BR anchored 12 points in, a gradient with alpha tiled and turned 90 degrees, a PDF page turned 15 degrees in its transparency group, TOP across a page turned by `/Rotate` -- captured by `golden-update --decoration --filter watermark-` and **each looked at before it was kept** (`golden/UPDATES.md`, 2026-11-23T02:14:24Z). **Pure** (`unit.watermark: …`, `tst_watermark`): each source's natural size, each anchor, offset, a quarter turn, 45 degrees, both scales and each `/Rotate` against a matrix worked by hand; tiling's copies and their order; a 3-point picture's 53,000 copies refused by key. **Raster** (`render.watermark: …`, `tst_watermarkengine`): over `boxes.pdf`'s filled panel an opaque red picture tiled BEHIND changes 0 of the panel's interior pixels and IN FRONT all of them, and the empty page red under both; black text at 50 % over white reads 124 … 131 at its darkest; a page of `refs-every-kind.pdf` as the source -- its identity text extracted from the watermarked page. **Surface** (`ui.decorate the watermark dialog …`): every field into a spec, the preview's copies the layout's, a Cyrillic letter and too many tiles refused before OK. Planted controls P5 (anchor row and column swapped), P6 (an image drawn unscaled), P8 (the opacity never written), P9 (a page not a transparency group -- green against the raster cases, red in the golden one) and P14 (no tile bound) red.

**A-100 · Apply, update, remove** ✅ *2026-11-23, T15* — §3.8
**Steps** `ctest -R "decoration.roundtrip"` for watermarks; `serialize:`/`revert:` for both commands.
**Expect** §3.8's bar; an update is one command and leaves one watermark. **If it fails** S1.
**Result (T15)** ✅ *2026-11-23.* First-party half (`tst_watermarksink`): over **every editable corpus file, 121** -- a print-only text watermark behind, saved; a tiled image one in front, saved; the TEXT one removed by choice, saved (the image one still listed, the group gone); every one removed, saved -- each page, its `/Contents`, its `/Resources` AND the catalog, its `/OCProperties` and that dictionary's `/D` **equal to the original's in value**, none refused. An update replaces only the one chosen, KEEPING its picture: after a save, every page's form names the very image object (not written again); before one, the bytes it carried -- ONE image object in the file; one undo puts the old one back; an update of a watermark gone refused (`error.decorate.noneFound`), and a kept picture found nowhere (`error.decorate.sourceGone`), no undo step either. Another producer's watermark neither listed nor removed. An image made a page: 400 x 200 pixels, a 300 x 150 point page, its image XObject with its mask. Engine half (`tst_watermarkengine`): the same cycle over every editable file, its first and last page **0 differing pixels** at 72 dpi against the original. Reversibility: **8,365 combinations over 121 files, 0 failures**, both commands in it, a removal of ONE chosen beside another it must leave. Through the product: `ui.composition.assembly_shown` with four more steps -- a screen-only text watermark, a tiled picture, that picture updated keeping its bytes, every one removed -- each drawn as saved and every undo as the original. Journal lines: both commands read back, the ids chosen read from the line itself, a hostile id list and a picture with no bytes refused. **Deviations:** the raster half reads each file's first and last page (A-91's, the same reason), and takes PDFium's first unknown-font substitution first (I-14). Planted controls P1, P3, P4, P7, P11, P13 and P15 red -- **P13** (a removal's ids dropped from its journal line) **STAYED GREEN** first: the round trip compared a line with a line that had lost them too; the case now reads the ids, and P13 is red against it.

**A-101 · Print-only and screen-only** ✅ *2026-11-23, T15*
**Steps** `ctest -R "watermark.usage"`.
**Expect** A print-only watermark drawn when PDFium renders with its print flag and not without; screen-only the reverse; its group **the watermark's own**, made and taken out with it by the same command -- *rewritten 2026-11-23 at T15 by the user's choice (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-45(a)); as written it read "its group created by `CreateLayer` inside the same command"*. **If it fails** S2.
**Result (T15)** ✅ *2026-11-23.* `render.watermark.usage: …` on `text-letter.pdf`, a fresh engine on each saved file: print-only 0 pixels apart on screen and thousands with `RenderFlags::Printing` (`FPDF_PRINTING`), screen-only the reverse, always both -- and the LIVE engine drawing the unsaved update the same. First-party: on `layers.pdf` one group of ours added to its own groups, `/Usage` print ON and view OFF, two applications (View, Print) appended to its `/D /AS` and theirs unchanged, its `/Order` untouched, the form naming the group as `/OC`; removed, the catalog as it was. On a file with no layers an `/OCProperties` made with an empty `/Order`, taken away whole with the watermark, and the removal undone after that save lists the very group again. ## 6. Manual verification — you run this

Fourteen cases, **each on copies of corpus files** in `tests\manual\<case>\`, each asking for a
judgement no assertion makes. **Walk each at the task that makes it walkable**, not at the end:

| Walk at | Cases |
|---|---|
| **T8** | M-01, M-12, M-13 |
| **T9** | M-08 |
| **T10** | M-02, M-03 |
| **T11** | M-05 |
| **T12** | M-06 |
| **T13** | M-14 |
| **T14** | M-07 |
| **T15** | M-09 |
| **T16** | M-04 (**P-3 open**) |
| **T17** | M-10 |
| **T18** | M-11 |

Three things about the state you will be walking, so a planned absence is not reported as a defect:

- **Every change is undoable and changes the file only when you save**; a merge, an extract to one
  file or an imposition is an **untitled tab** until you Save As (Q1 ✅).
- **RawPage does not print** — M-04 and M-09 print from Acrobat (Q5(b) ✅).
- **Walk on copies in `tests\manual\<case>\`**, never on the corpus files themselves. **Before any walk
  that uses a chord, check that no other program holds it** (`CLAUDE.md` → *Traps*; Stage 5's I-17,
  Stage 6's I-23).

---

**M-01 · Moving pages by hand** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-17*
- **Goal** The thumbnails pane judged by a person: selecting, dragging, cutting and pasting pages feels like a file manager and lands where it looks like it will.
- **Pre** T8. Copies of `pages-900.pdf` and `refs-every-kind.pdf` in `tests\manual\M-01`; both monitors; two RawPage windows, then two RawPage processes.
- **Steps**
  1. In `refs-every-kind.pdf`, select pages 3 to 5 by click and `Shift`+click; `Alt+↓` twice; `Ctrl+Z` once.
  2. `Ctrl`+click pages 1, 7 and 9; drag them to the end. Open the Bookmarks pane and click each bookmark.
  3. `Ctrl+X` page 2, select page 8, `Ctrl+V`. `Ctrl+Shift+D` on page 4. `Delete` on page 6; read the report.
  4. Drag pages from `pages-900.pdf` in one window into `refs-every-kind.pdf` in the other, then across monitors.
  5. Copy two pages in one RawPage process; paste them in a second.
  6. Save; close; reopen; click every bookmark again.
- **Expect** Every drop where the insertion marker said; every bookmark opens the page showing the identity its title names (the generator titles them so); the deleted page's bookmark says it has no destination; undo steps one gesture at a time; after reopening, all as before.
- **If it fails** Note the step, the pages and the window; a bookmark landing on the wrong identity is S1.
- **Walked 2026-11-17 by the user** (release build of `81295a6`, both monitors, the second process the `x64-debug` build): steps 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 as expected. Step 3: the duplicate landed after P-0005, `Delete` on slot 6 took P-0008, and P-0008's bookmark *says it has no destination* as a greyed title with the tooltip *"Goes nowhere — its page is not in this document"*, a click doing nothing -- enough, the user's reading; but P-0006, cut and pasted back, came back with its bookmark greyed too -- **I-23** (S2), fixed the same day (D7-37). **Step 3 walked again 2026-11-17 by the user** on the release build with the fix, a fresh copy of the corpus file: P-0006 cut and pasted after P-0012, its bookmark and page 1's second link opening the pasted page, the other bookmarks their own, undo and redo, saved and reopened, and P-0001 cut and pasted with its bookmark and its two links -- *"All matches"*. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-17.**

**M-02 · What other readers do with our pages** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-17*
- **Goal** D7-18's fourth oracle: Acrobat and Firefox follow our references and open our new documents.
- **Pre** T10. M-01's saved file; an extract, a split folder and a merge of `merge-a`, `merge-b`, `merge-c` saved in `tests\manual\M-02`; P-1, P-2.
- **Steps**
  1. Open each in **Acrobat** and in **Firefox**.
  2. Click every bookmark and every link; read the page labels in each reader's page box.
  3. In Acrobat, open the merged file's form fields and layers panels.
  4. As a **control**, open them in Chrome: it should agree with RawPage, because it is PDFium.
- **Expect** Each reader opens every file **without a repair message**; every bookmark and link lands on the page showing the identity it names; labels as RawPage shows them; the merged fields and layers listed as the merge report said.
- **If it fails** Note the reader, the file and the reference; a reader refusing a file is S1, a reference landing elsewhere S1.
- **Walked 2026-11-17 by the user** in Acrobat 26.2, Firefox 158 and, as the control, Chrome, on `m01-walk1.pdf` (M-01's first walk, before I-23's fix), `m01-rewalk.pdf` (step 3 again, after it), `extract-3-8.pdf`, the three files of a split at every top-level bookmark and `merged-abc.pdf`, all in `tests\manual\M-02`: every file opened in every reader with no repair, every bookmark and link landed where RawPage lands it, the merged file's fields and layers as its report said (*"Merged 3 file(s) into a new document of 9 page(s). 5 form field name(s) renamed."*). **Its preparation raised I-25 (S1)** -- the first extract had no bookmarks -- fixed the same day (D7-38), and the extract and the split were made again on the fixed build and walked again. Read back independently by `pypdf` the same day: the extract's outline `P-0001 (nowhere)[P-0003] P-0005[P-0006, P-0008]`; the split's `P-0001[P-0002, P-0003]`, `P-0005[P-0006, P-0008]`, `P-0012`, each item on the page it names, part 1's `old-delta` link on P-0004 and its links to pages outside cleared; the merge's eleven fields with their values (`name`, `name_2`, `name_3`, `address.street`, `address_2.city` ...), six layers and labels 1 ... 9; `m01-walk1.pdf`'s P-0006 and P-0008 bookmarks going nowhere, as that file was saved. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-17.**

**M-03 · A 30-way merge** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22*
- **Goal** The roadmap's emphasis: a large merge, judged by the person running it.
- **Pre** T10. Thirty copies of corpus files in `tests\manual\M-03\in`, one of them encrypted and one a `.txt` renamed `.pdf`.
- **Steps**
  1. Open the merge dialog; add the thirty by drag from Explorer; reorder five; remove one; set a page range on two.
  2. Merge; watch the progress; cancel once and start again.
  3. Read the report; look at the result's bookmarks.
- **Expect** The dialog takes thirty files without lag; the progress moves and cancel stops promptly with nothing left behind; the encrypted file and the false PDF are reported with reasons; the result's bookmarks are one per input in the order you chose.
- **If it fails** Note the step; a lost input is S1, a frozen window S2.
- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** on the release build of `20bf88c`, thirty copies in `tests\manual\M-03\in` numbered `01-` … `30-` so each per-file bookmark names its place: `11-aes-256.pdf` (encrypted), `17-merge-not-a-pdf.pdf` (text named `.pdf`), `merge-a`, `-b`, `-c`, nine 900-page files and sixteen others. Step 1: the thirty dragged from Explorer without lag, each row counted, `11` and `17` marked *"Will be skipped"* with a reason; `07` and `08` moved up under `01`, `14-text-900` removed, ranges `2-3` on `03-merge-c` and `1-5` on `10-odd-count-17`. Step 2: the progress moved and Cancel stopped the merge with no tab and no file left -- prompt, the user's judgement, though the log measures **1.42 s** against §3.5's 1 s (**I-28**). Step 3, three runs read from the product's log: the second, the removal forgotten, 30 inputs, *"merged 28 of 30 file(s) -- 8163 page(s)"*; the third, `14` removed again, 29 inputs, *"merged 27 of 29 file(s) -- 7263 page(s)"*, 4.3 s from Merge to the tab against §3.5's 30 s -- the page counts exactly the inputs' with both ranges applied. The report: `11` skipped as password-protected, `17` as *"%1 is not a PDF file."* (**I-27**), `name` and `date` of `02-merge-b.pdf` renamed `name_2` and `date_2`, `name` of `06-forms-acroform.pdf` renamed `name_3`, and `03-merge-c.pdf`'s 2 references to its unmerged pages lost -- `MC-0001` and `MC-0004` greyed in the pane. The Bookmarks pane, checked by the user: one closed bookmark per merged input in the list's order -- 28 in the second run, 27 in the third, `14-text-900`'s absent -- each input's own bookmarks beneath its own. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22** -- I-28's 1.42 s *"fast enough"* for a 30-way merge, the user's word, and I-27 fixed the same day by the user's choice.

**M-04 · A booklet on paper** ☐ — **needs P-3**
- **Goal** The roadmap's exit criterion: imposition correct on paper.
- **Pre** T16; **a duplex printer and paper (P-3)**. `odd-count-17.pdf` and `pages-900.pdf`'s first 16 pages extracted, in `tests\manual\M-04`.
- **Steps**
  1. Impose `odd-count-17.pdf` as a booklet, left binding, with crop marks; Save As.
  2. Print it **from Acrobat**, duplex, flip on the short edge; fold the sheets.
  3. Impose the 16 pages 4-up, across; print one-sided.
  4. Repeat step 1 with right binding.
- **Expect** §3.6: the folded booklet reads P-0001 … P-0017 in order, upright, blanks at the end, binding on the side chosen; the 4-up sheets read their cells across; crop marks where the sheet would be trimmed.
- **If it fails** Photograph the sheets; a page out of order or upside down is S2.

**M-05 · Bookmarks by hand** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22*
- **Goal** Hierarchy editing by drag and by chord, judged by a person — the exit criterion's own words.
- **Pre** T11. Copies of `outline-labels.pdf` and `tagged-headings.pdf` in `tests\manual\M-05`.
- **Steps**
  1. From the page, `Ctrl+B`; type a title. In the pane, `F2`, `Tab`, `Shift+Tab`, `Alt+↑`, `Alt+↓`, `Ctrl+Shift+D`, `Delete` (both answers).
  2. Drag bookmarks to re-parent and re-order; try dropping one onto its own child.
  3. Generate bookmarks from headings on `tagged-headings.pdf`; make a table-of-contents page; click its lines.
  4. Save; open in Acrobat.
- **Expect** Each key does what §8 says and nothing else; the drop onto its own child is refused visibly; the generated tree is the document's headings; Acrobat shows the same tree and the TOC page's lines link.
- **If it fails** Note the key or the drag; a bookmark lost is S1.
- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** on the release build of `f4eadc6`, copies in `tests\manual\M-05`, every chord the walk uses swept first with `RegisterHotKey` -- fifteen free, the sweep's control (`Ctrl+B` held by the sweep itself) reported held (1409). Every step as expected, the user's word: `Ctrl+B` from the page (*Untitled*, and a selection's text as the title), `F2`, `Tab`, `Shift+Tab`, `Alt+↑`, `Alt+↓`, `Ctrl+Shift+D`, `Delete` with both answers; drags re-parenting and re-ordering, the drop onto its own child refused; the headings generated on `tagged-headings.pdf` and a contents page made; both saved and opened in Acrobat. **One instruction of Claude's was wrong, not the product:** the hand-over said a single click on a bookmark opens it, and `Ctrl+Shift+D` then looked broken -- a click SELECTS a bookmark and a double-click or `Enter` opens it (`ui/panes/BookmarksPane.cpp`, on purpose), so after `Ctrl+Shift+D` a click moved nothing and `Enter` went where the view already stood; a second `Ctrl+Shift+D` was refused, *"Nothing about the bookmark would change"* (the log, 06:44:25). Walked again in the right order -- select, scroll the view, `Ctrl+Shift+D`, scroll away, `Enter` -- as expected. Read back independently by `pypdf` the same day: `tagged-headings.pdf` 4 pages, its outline `1 Assembly overview[1.1 Moving pages[1.1.1 Page maps] 1.2 Copying pages[1.2.1 Shared resources]] 2 New documents[2.1 Splitting 2.2 Merging[2.2.1 Field names]]` -- the label's `expectedOutline` exactly, each item on the page showing its `TG-` identity -- and its new page 1, the contents page, nine links to TG-0001 ×3, TG-0002 ×3, TG-0003 ×3, in the outline's order; `outline-labels.pdf` 8 pages, its labels `i … iii, 1 … 5` kept, the edited tree two levels deep with the walk's bookmarks under `1 front matter and body`. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22.** **After the sign-off the user asked for D7-40** (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` §3) -- a right-click menu on a bookmark, a double-click that goes and then renames, and the ribbon's *Generate* named *Generate Bookmarks* -- graded by A-130 and A-131; not yet tried by hand.

**M-06 · Links by hand** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22*
- **Goal** Making and following links, and what another reader shows of them.
- **Pre** T12. Copies of `text-a4.pdf` and `refs-every-kind.pdf` in `tests\manual\M-06`; P-1, P-2.
- **Steps**
  1. Draw a link to page 3; make a link from a selected sentence to a URL; auto-detect URLs.
  2. Set one visible with a red dashed border and inverted highlight, one invisible.
  3. Follow each with the Hand tool: the page link, the URL (answer *No*, then *Yes*), the `GoToR`.
  4. Run *Verify links*. Save; open in Acrobat and Firefox and click each.
- **Expect** Each link where it was drawn; the URL asks before anything opens; *Verify links* lists what is broken; Acrobat and Firefox show the borders and highlights as RawPage does and follow each link.
- **If it fails** Note the link and the reader; anything opened without asking is S1.
- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** on the release build of `75346c6`, copies in `tests\manual\M-06`, every chord the walk uses swept first with `RegisterHotKey` -- twelve, all free, the sweep's control (`Ctrl+Z`, registered by the sweep itself first) reported held (1409). **Deviation:** neither `text-a4.pdf` nor `refs-every-kind.pdf` has a web address in its text, so step 1's auto-detect was walked on `urls.pdf`, a one-page Helvetica file written for the walk with A-84's composition lines (four addresses, two lines of near-misses) -- a walk file, not a corpus one. Every step as expected, the user's word (*"All tests are pass"*): on `text-a4.pdf` a link drawn to page 3, red, dashed, inverted highlight; a sentence on page 2 linked to `https://example.com/`, invisible; a third link, drawn to a page of `not-here.pdf`, so *Verify links* had something to list; *Auto Detect* on `urls.pdf` and a second run; each followed with the Hand tool -- the URL answered *No*, then *Yes*, `refs-every-kind.pdf`'s `GoToR` to P-0005, and `not-here.pdf`'s answered *Yes* and refused at open (the log, 07:43:23, *"open: no such file"*); *Verify links*; both files saved (appended, 07:50:49 and 07:50:50) and opened in Acrobat and Firefox. **Two instructions of Claude's were wrong, not the product:** the hand-over named *Organize → Document Navigation → Create* -- the group is *Navigation*, the ribbon has a *Create* TAB, which was clicked and has no link tool, and the group holds two buttons both labelled *Create* (`link.create`, then `destination.create` after *Verify Links*); and it did not say that a detected link is invisible (D7-35(g)) with no cursor of its own over it, so *Auto Detect* first looked to do nothing -- its only sign the status bar's line, until each address was followed. Neither was logged as an issue; the user did not ask. Read back independently by `pypdf` the same day: `text-a4.pdf` page 1's link `/Dest` to page 3, `/BS << /W 1 /S /D /D [3 2] >>`, `/C [0.8 0 0]`, no `/H` (ISO's default, invert); page 2's sentence four links, one per line, each `/URI (https://example.com/)` with `/W 0`; the `GoToR` to `not-here.pdf` page 1; every one with an appearance of its own; `urls.pdf` exactly four links -- `https://example.com/path?q=1`, `mailto:someone@example.org`, `http://www.rawpage.test/docs`, `https://example.com/a_(b)` -- none on lines 5 and 6. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22.**

**M-07 · Headers, footers and Bates numbers** ☐ *walkable since T14 (2026-11-23), not walked*
- **Goal** The preview, the result and removal, judged by a person, and the numbering across documents.
- **Pre** T14. Copies of `mixed-page-sizes.pdf`, `rotated-pages.pdf` (both in `corpus/ordinary/`), `merge-a`, `merge-b`, `merge-c` (`corpus/assembly/`) in `tests\manual\M-07`; P-1.
- **Steps** *(as built at T14: the **Organize** tab's **Page Marks** group -- **Add Header and Footer…** (the large button), **Update Header and Footer…**, **Remove Header and Footer**, then **Add Watermark…** (T15's, disabled at T14; with **Update Watermark…** and **Remove Watermark** beside it since T15), **Add Bates Numbering…**, **Remove Bates Numbering**, **Booklet…** (T16's). In the header/footer dialog the six fields are **Header** / **Footer** by **Left** / **Centre** / **Right**; **Insert field** puts the chosen field -- *Page number*, *Page count*, *Date* … -- into the area last clicked, as `<<page>>`, `<<count>>`, `<<date>>`; **Every page in the range** / **Odd pages only** / **Even pages only** beside **Pages**; **Profile**, a **Save as** name, **Save Profile** and **Delete Profile** across the top; the **Preview** at the right with **Preview page** under it. The Bates dialog ticks the open documents in a list with **Move Up** / **Move Down**, and the report is titled **Bates Numbering Report**.)*
  1. Add a header with a date and a footer `Page <n> of <count>`, on odd pages only; compare the preview with the result.
  2. Save the configuration as a profile; update the header in place; remove it.
  3. Open the three `merge-*` files; Bates-number them in an order you choose with a prefix; read the report.
  4. Save; open in Acrobat; look at print preview.
- **Expect** The result is what the preview showed, on every page size and rotation; update leaves one header; remove leaves the page as it was; the Bates numbers run on across the three; Acrobat shows and previews the same.
- **If it fails** Note the page; a number repeated or skipped is S1.

**M-08 · Crop and resize** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22*
- **Goal** The crop tool and *remove white margins* judged by eye, on both monitors.
- **Pre** T9. Copies of `boxes.pdf` and `text-letter.pdf` in `tests\manual\M-08`; both monitors.
- **Steps** *(as built at T9: **Organize → Page Size → Crop** arms the tool -- `page.crop`, also in the tool finder; **Set
  Page Boxes…** beside it is the dialog -- `page.setBoxes`; **Resize** and **Renumber** open theirs)*
  1. On the 150 % monitor, arm **Crop** and drag a box on page 1 of `boxes.pdf`; the **Set Page Boxes** dialog opens with the
     box's margins filled in -- press **OK**. Move the window to the 100 % monitor and do the same on page 2.
  2. **Set Page Boxes…**, **Remove white margins**, **All pages the size of the first page in the range**, **OK**.
  3. Open `text-letter.pdf`; **Resize**, **A4**, **Scale the content to fit** on, **Centre**, **OK**. **Undo**, then
     **Resize** to A4 again with scaling off.
  4. Back in `boxes.pdf`, **Undo** (`Ctrl+Z`) until the crops are gone.
- **Expect** The crop is the box you dragged on both monitors; white margins go to the edge of the ink -- on page 3, the
  filled panel; on a stroked frame one point outside it (I-16, known); the A4 page holds the letter content centred, and
  unscaled the content stays where it was on a larger sheet; undo restores the page.
- **If it fails** Note the monitor and the page; a crop off the dragged box is S2.
- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** on the release build of `75346c6` (`d677203` is documents only), copies in `tests\manual\M-08`, the two monitors as `--print-screen-metrics` reads them -- `MAG 272URDF` at scale 1.5, primary, and `LS27B61x` at 1 -- and the walk's chords swept first with `RegisterHotKey`, all free, the sweep's control reported held (1409). **Deviation in step 2:** *All pages the size of the first page in the range* takes every page whose SHOWN size is the first's (`core::pagesOfSameSize`), and each page of `boxes.pdf` already shows a size of its own -- page 1 Letter, page 2's crop box smaller than page 3's A4 -- so the step was walked as two passes: **2a**, range `1-3`, the crop box, *Remove white margins*, a margin of 0, the option off (the log: *"white margins of 3 page(s) measured … 3 with content"*, 08:03:09 and 08:03:42); undone; **2b**, range `3` with the option on (*"… of 1 page(s) …"*, 08:05:11 and 08:07:19). Every step as expected, the user's word (*"All tests are pass"*): the dragged crop on page 1 on the 150 % monitor and on page 2 on the 100 % monitor, each the box dragged; 2a page 3 to its filled panel, page 2 round its caption and identity, page 1 to its outermost outline with I-16's known point outside it; 2b page 3 alone; `text-letter.pdf` resized to A4 scaled to fit and centred, undone, and resized again unscaled -- the content its own size, the sheet round it; undo in `boxes.pdf` until the crops were gone. Nothing was saved: both copies byte-identical to the corpus files afterwards (`cmp`). No issue raised. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22.**

**M-09 · Watermarks** ☐ *walkable since T15 (2026-11-23), not walked*
- **Goal** Placement, layering and print-only behaviour, judged by eye and on a print.
- **Pre** T15. Copies of `text-a4.pdf` and `image-dct-rgb.pdf` (`corpus/ordinary/`), `refs-every-kind.pdf` (`corpus/assembly/`) and `seal-rgba.png` (`corpus/stamps/`) in `tests\manual\M-09`; P-1.
- **Steps** *(as built at T15: the **Organize** tab's **Page Marks** group -- **Add Watermark…**, **Update Watermark…** and **Remove Watermark**, beside the header/footer and Bates buttons. The watermark dialog: **Profile** with a name, **Save Profile** and **Delete Profile** across the top; **Source** -- **Text** (its field, **Size**, **Colour**), **Image file** with **Choose…**, **Page of a PDF file** with **Choose…** and **Page:**; **Appearance and position** -- **Opacity:** (%), **Rotation:** (degrees, counter-clockwise), **Scale:** with *% of the page* or *% of its own size*, **Position:** (nine, *Top left* … *Bottom right*), **Offset (points), across and up:**, **Tile across the page, gap (points):**, **Place:** (*Behind the page content* / *In front of the page content*), **Show:** (*On screen and when printed* / *Only when printed* / *Only on screen*); **Pages** with its parity; the **Preview** at the right. With more than one watermark in the document, *Update* and *Remove* first ask which -- **Choose Watermarks**, *Remove* with **Select All**; with one, they act on it.)*
  1. In `text-a4.pdf`: add a text watermark at 45 degrees, 30 % opacity, behind the content; then an image watermark from `seal-rgba.png`, tiled, in front; then a page watermark -- page 1 of `refs-every-kind.pdf`. Compare each preview with the result.
  2. Make the text one **Only when printed** (update it). Save; open the file in Acrobat -- the text watermark absent on screen; print it from Acrobat to **Microsoft Print to PDF**; open that output.
  3. Update the image one (a new opacity, the picture kept); remove the page one, choosing it in the list.
  4. In `image-dct-rgb.pdf`: a text watermark behind -- it shows only where the photograph does not cover the page.
  5. Copy an image (any program, `Print Screen` will do) and **Paste** (`Ctrl+V`) in the Page Thumbnails pane: one new page, the image's size.
- **Expect** *Behind* shows only where the page is empty; the print-only watermark is absent on screen in RawPage and Acrobat and present in the printed PDF; update and remove act on the one chosen; the pasted image is one page showing it whole.
- **If it fails** Note the watermark; one removed that was not chosen is S1.

**M-13 · Changing a signed document** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22, on the expectation corrected below*
- **Goal** D7-20's warning, and what Acrobat's signature panel says afterwards.
- **Pre** T8. Copies of the four `corpus/signed/` files in `tests\manual\M-13`; P-1.
- **Steps**
  1. Open each; move a page; read the message bar. Move another.
  2. Save; open in Acrobat; read the signature panel.
  3. On a fresh copy, move a page and **Save As**: answer *keep the signatures*. On another, Save As and answer *rewrite*. Open both in Acrobat.
- **Expect** The warning appears once per document, before the first change. **Every kept signature is intact** -- each saved file begins with its original bytes, and OpenSSL verifies each (`tests/gates/check-signatures.py`). **Acrobat reports a PAGE change after an approval signature as *"Signature is INVALID. The document has been altered or corrupted since the Signature was applied"***, its policy permitting only form fill-in, signing and commenting after one -- the verdict Stage 3's M-03 measured for a page rotation through the product on the same four files (`Stage3-Mutation-Testing-Guide.md` §6, its control 2), and what the warning says (*"will be reported as a modification"*); `certified-nochanges.pdf` reads *"Document certification is INVALID"*, the control. Save As asks before writing; the copy whose signatures were kept reads as step 2's; the rewritten one fails OpenSSL and Acrobat calls it invalid, as the question said it would be. *(Corrected 2026-11-22 at the walk: until then this line expected Acrobat's "changed after signing" verdict, which holds for a permitted change -- a comment, Stage 6's M-03 -- and never for a page change.)*
- **If it fails** Note the file; a kept signature OpenSSL rejects, or a kept file whose original bytes changed, is S1; an Acrobat verdict other than the two above, for a kept file, is S2.
- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** in Acrobat on the release build of `75346c6`, copies of the four `corpus/signed/` files in `tests\manual\M-13` and two more of `approval.pdf` in `step3\`, their SHA-256 in `before-hashes.txt`; the walk's chords swept first with `RegisterHotKey`, all free, the sweep's control reported held (1409). **Deviation:** each signed file has ONE page, so step 1 duplicated the page (`Ctrl+Shift+D`) -- a page command through the same path, so the warning still came before the first change -- and then moved it (`Alt+↓`, `Alt+↑`). **Step 1:** the warning once per document, before the first change, not raised again by a second command -- but one CLOSED in the first tab came back after a tab switch: **I-29** (S3), fixed after the walk by the user's choice. **Step 2:** the three approval files *"Signature is INVALID. The document has been altered or corrupted since the Signature was applied"* with *"The certifier has specified that Form Fill-in, Signing and Commenting are allowed for this document. No other changes are permitted"*; `certified-nochanges.pdf` *"Document certification is INVALID … The certifier has specified that no changes are allowed"*. **Step 3:** Save As asked first; `approval-keep-out.pdf` (*Keep the signatures*) read as step 2's `approval.pdf` -- byte-identical to it (`cmp`); `approval-rewrite-out.pdf` (*Rewrite*) *"Signature is INVALID … errors in the formatting or information contained in this signature"*. **The Expect was wrong, not the product** (corrected above, in place): it promised the *changed after signing* verdict, Acrobat's for a comment, where a page change reads as Stage 3's M-03 measured. **Read from the files afterwards:** every kept file a byte-identical prefix of its original (`approval.pdf` 19,370 → 19,768 bytes, its update the new page object and `/Kids [7 0 R 10 0 R]`); `check-signatures.py` with OpenSSL 3.5.6 verifying the five kept signatures and refusing `approval-rewrite-out.pdf` (*"the gap is not exactly the /Contents hex string"*). **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22** (*"OK. Do #1 to #3"*, on the corrected expectation). **Step 1 walked again the same day on the fixed build** (I-29; the working tree after the fix, built 08:38), fresh copies in `tests\manual\M-13\rewalk`: a warning closed in one tab stayed closed through tab switches and two more page commands, one left open in another tab still showed, and a fourth tab's later changes added none -- *"All works"*, the user's word; the log one *"pages: warned that 1 signature(s) of …"* line per document (08:51:41, 08:51:44, 08:52:45, 08:53:12); nothing saved.

**M-14 · A stamp made from a PDF page** ✅ *signed off by the user 2026-11-22*
- **Goal** Stage 6's Q7, judged in RawPage and in Acrobat.

  2. Save; open in Acrobat and Firefox.

- **Walked 2026-11-22 by the user** in RawPage, Acrobat and Firefox, on the release build of `82e4ec4`, copies of `refs-every-kind.pdf` and `text-a4.pdf` in `tests\manual\M-14`, their SHA-256 in `before-hashes.txt`; the walk's chords (`Ctrl+Alt+S`, `Ctrl+S`, `V`, `Esc`, and `Ctrl+E` when it was needed) swept first with `RegisterHotKey`, all free, the sweep's control reported held (1409). **Step 1:** the stamp made on the dialog's *PDF Page* tab from page 3 (*P-0003*), filed under the user's own category; placed six times rather than twice, one turned 30° with the properties bar's **Turn** -- the bar shown with `Ctrl+E`, which the hand-over had not said (the hand-over's omission, not the product's). **Step 2:** saved; **all as expected** -- the stamp sharp at every zoom in all three readers, Acrobat and Firefox showing it as RawPage does (*"All tests are passed"*, the user's word). **Read from the file afterwards:** `text-a4.pdf` 12,895 → 17,517 bytes, its first 12,895 the original's (SHA-256 unchanged); six `/Stamp` annotations, each appearance a form drawing ONE shared page form, object 15 -- the page written once for six placements, as T13 built it -- the turned one's `/Matrix` *0.866 0.5 -0.5 0.866 0 0*. Raised **I-30** (S3, the Stamp tool's status message) and **I-31** (S3, the palette's picture of a page stamp garbled), both outside what M-14 grades, to be fixed after the walk by the user's choice. **Signed off by the user, 2026-11-22.**

## 7. Known caveats — verify, do not assume broken

Behaviours that look like defects and are not. **A wrong row here is worse than a missing one.**

| Looks broken | Is |
|---|---|
| **Chrome or Edge follows our links exactly as RawPage does** | Not evidence — both are PDFium (D7-18). Acrobat and Firefox are the oracles |
| **A file saved with `Ctrl+S` is larger than the pages left in it** | Correct: Save appends, unless `documents/saveMode` is `full`; **Save As** rewrites and compacts (D7-25) |
| **Save As on a signed document asks a question first** | Correct — a rewrite invalidates the signatures, so you choose (D7-25) |
| **A bookmark says it has no destination after its page was deleted** | Correct — D7-2 keeps it rather than dropping your title |
| **Hiding a layer does not ask to save** | Correct — visibility on screen is view state (D7-9) |
| **A merge, an extract or an imposition opens untitled** | Correct — Q1, approved 2026-10-29: Save As to keep it (D7-5) |
| **Inserted pages lose their tags** | Correct — D7-4 |
| **A JavaScript link does nothing** | Correct — never executed (D7-12) |
| **Another program's watermark cannot be removed in RawPage** | Correct — only RawPage's (D7-7, `Features.md` §5.8) |
| **The white-ink page is not split as blank** | Correct — blank means no ink in the content box (A-49) |
| **A `Ctrl+Shift+` or `Alt+` chord does nothing** | Another program may hold it as a system-wide hotkey (Stage 5's I-17) — try `RegisterHotKey` on it |

The control is the build log itself -- 2 `warning:` lines before the fix, 0 after it in the release rebuild and in `x64-debug`'s clean build, both suites green over it. **Closed** 2026-11-22 by CI run 45029686401 on `447dab2`, green in all five jobs, its Windows build compiling `tst_stampink.cpp` with no `warning:` line anywhere in the job's log, read from its logs 2026-11-22 |
| **I-33** | S3 | After 7a's sign-off, re-deriving the documents against the tree: `ribbon-validate res/ribbon.json` printed *"9 tabs, 58 groups, 222 controls"* where the file holds **49** groups. *Mechanism:* the tool printed `Spec::catalogKeys().size() - tabs.size()` as the group count -- right when Stage 1 T10 wrote it (`dd28437`), when the list held tab and group titles only; Stage 1 guide I-42 (`488b1ed`, 2026-08-31) added each tab's `ribbon.keytip.*` access key to it, so from then it read groups + tabs. *Why no suite saw it:* nothing reads the tool's success line -- its cases and the build step judge its exit code and its diagnostics -- and `Architecture-FileMap.md`'s `res/ribbon.json` row quoted the line as *58 groups* from 2026-09-21, while `Shipped-Surface.md`, counting the file, had said 49 since 2026-10-28 | **Fixed** 2026-11-22: `ui::ribbon::Spec::groupCount()`, counted from `tabs[].groups`, and the tool prints it -- *"9 tabs, 49 groups, 222 controls"*. Regression: `ui.the group count is the resource's groups and not its catalog keys` (`[I-33]`), against the JSON as Qt's own reader reads it, with the old arithmetic as its control (it disagrees with the resource); 2133/2133 in the release suite over it. **Closed** 2026-11-23 by CI run 45070177289 on `8992142`, green in all five jobs: the case passed and `ribbon-validate` printed *"9 tabs, 49 groups, 225 controls"* (run 45059346338 had passed it too, its Windows job red for I-35) |
| **I-34** | S3 | Found at T14 by reading T9's code after planted control P2 stayed green: a RESIZE's wrapper on a page whose `/Contents` is written as a reference to an ARRAY object was taken out -- by its undo, before a save or after one -- as a DIRECT array of the same streams, not the reference the page had. The page drew the same; A-62's *"unwrapped exactly"* held for the one shape its fixture had, a single stream reference. *Mechanism:* the compose wrote `/Contents` back from the list of items, which a single stream reference survives and a reference to an array does not. *Why no suite saw it:* no corpus page writes `/Contents` that way, and A-62's case used `text-letter.pdf` | **Fixed and closed** inside T14 (2026-11-23): the wrappers and T14's decorations are composed by one function, which RECORDS such a value in `/RawPageContents` while the page is layered and writes it back with the last layer (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-44(b)). Regression: `unit.decoration.roundtrip: a /Contents written as a reference to an array …` (`[I-34]`), a built page, resized, saved, undone and saved -- its `/Contents` the very reference; P2 red against it |
| **I-35** | S3 | CI run **45059346338** on `a2ae049` (T14's push), `Windows x64`: `render.golden.decoration every case matches its baseline` failed on the `cjk` case alone -- *"297 of 248832 pixels differ by more than 8 (0.1194 %, limit 0.1000 %), of which 79 differ by more than 96"* -- 2160/2161, every other job green. *Mechanism:* the case draws Han, kana and Hangul in faces resolved from the MACHINE, and the runner's are not this one's; T14 declared it machine-dependent for the substitution census only, and the comparison compared it anyway. *Why no suite saw it:* this machine resolves the faces the capture recorded, so it passes here by construction | **Fixed** 2026-11-23: the corpus suite's rule applied -- a machine-dependent decoration case is compared only where this machine substitutes the faces `captured.json` records, else reported NOT APPLICABLE by name, and only such a case may be skipped. Control: `cjk`'s recorded substitution planted different -- skipped by name, the other eight compared, green; restored. **Closed** 2026-11-23 by CI run 45070177289 on `8992142`, green in all five jobs, `Windows x64` 2161/2161 with both decoration golden cases passed |

**I-01 (S2) — the engine facade read a page's boxes from the page dictionary alone.** *Found* at T1 by
the first scratch render of `assembly/inherit-resources.pdf`, whose pages inherit both boxes: an A4 page
turned 90° came out 1056 × 816 px, US Letter, uncropped, the identity off-centre. *Mechanism:*
`pdf/render/PdfiumDocument` read each page's boxes with `FPDFPage_GetMediaBox` and `FPDFPage_GetCropBox`,
which read the PAGE DICTIONARY only — PDFium's own renderer inherits, those two calls do not — and took
their silence for *"no box anywhere"*, substituting US Letter. So the page model disagreed with what the
engine drew, and with `pdf/ops/PageOps`' walk, which inherits `/MediaBox`: `Permissions::assess` compared
the two media sizes and refused the WHOLE document by `error.edit.pageSizeDisagrees`. *Why no suite saw
it:* **no corpus file before T1 inherited either box** (a scan of every page dictionary in `ordinary/`,
`signed/`, `compare/` and the seeds, 2026-10-29) — `rotate-inherited.pdf` inherits `/Rotate` and states
its `/MediaBox` on every page. *Fix:* the page's own boxes where it states them; otherwise
`FPDF_GetPageBoundingBox` — the box the engine DRAWS, media intersected with crop, which does inherit —
as the media box when the page states none, and as the crop box when it is smaller than the media box the
page states. `PageOps`' walk carries `/CropBox` down as it carries `/MediaBox`, and `Permissions` compares
the DRAWN box of both readers (`CosPageFacts::drawnSize`), the one box both can state through
inheritance. *Bound, stated at the code:* PDFium exposes no inherited media box apart from the crop, so a
page inheriting its `/MediaBox` AND a smaller `/CropBox` reports the drawn box as its media box — what is
shown is right; the media box is first read apart from the crop by the plan's page-box commands (D7-15,
T9). *Regression:* `render.assembly labels: a page shows the boxes and rotation it inherits (I-01)` (each
page's drawn box and rotation against the label, the document editable, and `boxes.pdf`'s own boxes as
the control); `unit.page walk resolves the media and crop boxes a page inherits`; and
`render.the shipped backend lets every ordinary, signed and assembly corpus file be edited`, which now
sweeps `assembly/`. *Control:* with the facade change reverted alone, the I-01 case failed on every
page's drawn box (off by 20 pt and the crop's width) and the sweep refused `inherit-resources.pdf` by
`error.edit.pageSizeDisagrees` on page 1; restored, both pass. The 99 baselines were captured after the
fix.

**I-02 (S3) — the runner image moved under CI, and the soak stopped at the build.** *Found* 2026-10-29
by the scheduled fuzz soak, run **40853107406** on `0ed701e`: all five targets failed at their build
step on `core/text/FolderSearch.h:163: error: private field 'engine_' is not used
[-Werror,-Wunused-private-field]`, so no target was fuzzed for a second — the *no sanitizer report* step
failed only because no log existed. No product behaviour is wrong; the severity is S3 because what was
lost is evidence: §9 item 6 is read from these jobs. *Mechanism:* two faults. `FolderSearch` stored
`engine_` (Stage 5) and read it nowhere — `session_` is built from the constructor's argument, and every
engine call goes through it. And every Linux job named `ubuntu-latest`, which GitHub began moving to
Ubuntu 26.04 on 2026-10-19: the run's own log reads `Image: ubuntu-26.04`, `Clang 21.1.8` and
`qt6-base-dev 6.10.2`, where the soak the day before (40665395393, on `db09265`) and CI run
40783851053's `portable-fuzz` job on `0ed701e` itself read `ubuntu-24.04` and `Clang 18.1.3`. *Why no suite saw it:*
MinGW's GCC has no `-Wunused-private-field`, and Clang 18 compiled the file clean; why the two Clang
versions differ on this field was not established. *Fix:* the field and its initializer removed; the
four Linux jobs in `ci.yml` and the soak in `fuzz-soak.yml` pinned to `runs-on: ubuntu-24.04`, because
the image's `qt6-base-dev` is the floor convention 24 (`Implementation-Plan.md` §1.5) holds the portable
layers to and 26.04 would raise it to 6.10.2 with nothing deciding to — `ci.yml`'s header says so, and
moving off 24.04 is a change to both files and that convention together. *Control:* none on this
machine, which has no clang; the red is run 40853107406 itself, and the pin means the next run builds
with Clang 18 again, so **Clang 21's verdict on the fix is not measured by CI**. *Closed* 2026-10-29 on
`afb6f0d`, read from the logs: soak **40875917394**, dispatched by hand, every target on `ubuntu-24.04`,
`Clang 18.1.3` and `qt6-base-dev 6.4.2`, each about 601 s with no sanitizer line — `fuzz_cos_lexer`
2,857,939 runs, `fuzz_cos_parser` 1,623,517, `fuzz_text_pipeline` 34,687, `fuzz_annot_pipeline` 15,569,
`fuzz_cos_roundtrip` 12,402 (611 s); and CI run **40875904825**, all five jobs green on `ubuntu-24.04`
for the Linux four.

**I-03 (S2) — an annotation the file holds, edited and then repaired, was written twice.** *Found* at T4 by A-128's
first green-going run, on `assembly/merge-a.pdf`: after its link -- the label's *"a link to its last page"* -- was
moved, deleting a page was refused by `error.edit.notShown`, context `error.pdf.writeDuplicateObject`. *Mechanism:*
two writers, one object. `ReferenceRepair` (T2, D7-2) clears the action of a link whose target page a command
removes, into the compose's pending map, written at the end; `composeAnnotations`' edit loop (Stage 6 T5) rewrites
an annotation edited in place straight into the delta. A link both moved and repaired got both, and the writer
refuses an object written twice -- so the verify refused the delete, which no person could then make. *Why no suite
saw it:* T2's reference cases (A-19 … A-21) run each page command over fixtures with references, and Stage 6's edit
cases edit -- **no case ever edited an annotation the repair then had to change**; A-128's script happened to do both,
on the one fixture with a link to a page it deletes. *Fix* (the user's choice, 2026-10-31, inside T4): an annotation
the repair has rewritten in this compose is edited ON the repaired dictionary and goes back where the repair put it
-- one object, written once; and a version the file held is not restored over a repair, the edit then made from the
model instead (`MutationSinkImpl::composeAnnotations`). *Regression:* `unit.a moved link whose target page is
deleted is written once` (`tests/unit/tst_composememo.cpp`): the link moved, its page deleted, saved, and the link
read back where it was moved to with no `/Dest` and no `/A`; and A-128 itself. *Control:* the fix reverted alone
(P11), the regression red by `error.pdf.writeDuplicateObject`; restored, green.

**I-04 (S2) — the number book's first number ignored the object limit.** *Found* by CI run **41141004141** on
`6cf1219` (T4's commit), its `Portable libFuzzer` job: `fuzz_annot_pipeline` stopped on
`pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl.cpp:325: runtime error: signed integer overflow: 9223372036854775807 + 1`. *Mechanism:*
D7-26's book starts above every number the file references, and T4 computed that start as `largestReferenced + 1`.
The allocator it replaced raised its floor through `ObjectAllocator::reserveThrough`, which ignores a reference past
ISO 32000-1 Annex C's limit (8,388,607 -- Stage 3 guide I-12's rule) because such a reference names nothing a reader
resolves. Without that rule a reference to 2^63-1 overflowed -- undefined behaviour, a wrap to a negative number on
MinGW -- and a reference just past the limit numbered every new object past it, where the reader refuses it, so the
edit was refused by its verify. *Why no suite saw it:* no corpus file references a number past the limit; the fuzzer
built one, and only UBSan sees the overflow -- on this machine it wraps silently. *Fix:* the start is the allocator's
floor raised through `reserveThrough`. *Regression:* `unit.a reference past the object limit does not move where new
objects are numbered` (`tests/unit/tst_composememo.cpp`): two built files, one referencing `8388608 0 R` and one
`9223372036854775807 0 R`, each edited, every new object at or under the limit, and saved. *Control:* the fix
reverted alone (P13), the 8,388,608 file's edit refused by `error.save.verifyFailed`; restored, green. *Graded in
CI:* run **41148688029** on `b4d5ab5`, the fix's push, read from its logs 2026-10-31 -- green in all five jobs; the
regression passed under `Portable ASan + UBSan` (1034/1034, no `runtime error`), and `fuzz_annot_pipeline` ran its
60 s (15,020 runs) with no finding.

**I-05 (S3) — a Stage 5 index case read one page more than it expected, once, in CI.** *Found* by the same run's
`Windows x64` job, the only red case of 1,903: `ui.a rotation drops the rotated page from the index and the next
search re-reads only it` (Stage 5 guide **A-64**), `tests/ui/tst_documentindex.cpp:277`,
`CHECK( uitest::fakeTextExtractions.load() == before + 1 )` with expansion `10 == 9` -- after a rotation of page 1, the
next search extracted two pages, where the case expects page 1 alone. *Not reproduced:* 30 runs of the case alone and
40 more beside six copies of the unit suite on this machine, all green; T3's CI run, **41042157448** on `daee550`,
passed it. *What is known:* the case runs over `tests/ui/FakeBackend.h`, whose `settleEdits` answers at once, so T4's
verify thread and gate are not in its path; the case's selection has read page 1 before the rotation, and the
selection and the search can each ask for page 1 again afterwards. *Mechanism:* NOT ESTABLISHED -- a race between
those two reads is the suspicion, and a suspicion is not a cause. *Severity S3* because what was miscounted is an
extraction, not a result: the search found what it should. *Since:* the next run, **41148688029** on `b4d5ab5`, passed it (1904/1904) -- one
green run says nothing about an intermittent cause. **Again** in run **41758087362** on `b3083f9` (T6's push), the only
red case of 1,943 in its `Windows x64` job: the same line, the same `10 == 9`. *Tried, 2026-11-04:* a 30 ms sleep in
`FakeTextExtractor::extractPage`, ten runs of the case, all green -- so a slow extraction alone does not open the window.
*A candidate path, not a mechanism:* after a search the hit painter asks again for every page on screen that has hits
(`SelectionOverlay.cpp`, `paintSearchHits`' `ensurePage`), and page 1 is on screen and has just been dropped, so a
repaint between the rotation and the case's `before` can ask for it a second time. The next two runs, **41770714539** and
**41808943466**, passed it (1946/1946 each). *Open*; the next CI runs are read for it.

**I-06 (S3) — the parser read an unterminated dictionary on into the next object.** *Found* at T5 by **A-38**: of
3,114 pages copied and inserted, `malformed/unbalanced-dictionary.pdf` page 1 drew differently from its source at
both scales. *Mechanism:* the page dictionary has no `>>`; `pdf/cos/Parser` read past `endobj` into the next object's
dictionary, keeping both `/Contents` entries -- and the LAST wins -- so our reading of page 1 was page 2's content,
while PDFium ends the object at `endobj`. Every write that copies the dictionary as we read it carried page 2's
content: T5's copy, and T2's duplicate before it. *Why the suite missed it:* Stage 2's tolerance case
(`tst_cos_parser`, *"closes and reports an unterminated container"*) asserts the note, not what follows the
dictionary; and no case before A-38 compared a written copy of a malformed page with what PDFium draws of it.
*Fixed* (the user's choice, of logging and bounding it): an `endobj` where a key belongs ends the dictionary, said by
the same `UnterminatedDictionary` note, and is left for `parseIndirectObject` -- PDFium's reading. *Regression:*
`unit.cos parser ends an unterminated dictionary at endobj, as PDFium does` (`[I-06]`), and A-38's sweep, now 0 differ.
*Control:* P9 (the stop reverted) red.

**I-07 (S2) — an inserted page drew its hidden layers.** *Found* at T5 by **A-38**: `assembly/layers.pdf` page 2
differed from its source at both scales. *Mechanism:* the page marks content with four optional-content groups, two of
which -- `fr` and `pr` -- the source's `/OCProperties /D /OFF` turns off; the copier copied the groups as objects the
page's `/Properties` reach, but the target's catalog listed none of them, and PDFium, like Acrobat, draws content of a
group no `/OCProperties` lists. Content its author hid became visible -- *S2*, because hidden content is often hidden
on purpose. *Why the suite missed it:* nothing before T5 brought pages from another document; A-38 is the first case
to draw a copy against its source. *Fixed* (the user's choice of two -- the other was bounding it and leaving it to
T10's merge): the copier narrows the source's `/OCProperties` to the groups it reached into the payload's catalog
(`ObjectGraphCopier::layers`), and the sink unions them into the target's -- `/OCGs`, `/OFF`, `/ON` under a target
`/BaseState /OFF`, `/Order`, `/Locked`, `/RBGroups`, `/AS` -- while a page draws from that payload, inserted or
replaced, before a save and after one, and takes them out when none does (`MutationSinkImpl::composeLayers`).
*Regression:* `unit.pageops layers: ...` (`[I-07]`) -- four groups and two hidden arrive in `refs-none.pdf`, leave
on undo after a save, return on redo, unite with `layers.pdf`'s own eight; and A-38 now 0 differ. *Found by its own
case while building:* the first union counted only unsaved pages as drawing from a graph, so after a save its groups
were taken out again (the redo and the undo-before-save control both red) -- a page saved from a payload is now
remembered (`graphOfPage_`). *Controls:* P7 (no union) and P8 (the saved page not remembered) red.

**I-08 (S3) — a Stage 5 timing bound red once.** *Found* in T5's first full `x64-release` run (1918 of 1919):
`render.a 900-page file's open never holds the engine longer than one page's extraction` (`tests/render/
tst_foldersearch.cpp:494`), *"T9 bound: 262 engine pieces, max 5.356 ms, p95 1.168 ms; ... one page's extraction ...
max 3.797 ms"* -- `pieceMax <= extractionMax` false. *Not reproduced:* five runs alone, max 3.14–3.49 ms against
3.68–4.86, all green. *What is known:* the case is `[run-serial]`, so no other ctest case ran beside it; it compares
one maximum with another, so a single slow piece decides it; T5's code is not in an open's path except the parser's
one new keyword comparison per dictionary key. *Mechanism:* NOT ESTABLISHED. **Again** 2026-11-04, the full `x64-release` run after T6's push fixes (1945 of 1946): *"274 engine pieces, max 4.281 ms ... extraction ... max 4.014 ms"*; five runs alone, max 3.06-3.76 ms against 3.45-4.02, all green. Those fixes are a test, the copier's reference rule and CI, none in an open's path. **A third time** 2026-11-05, T7's full `x64-debug` run (1976 of 1977), the first on that preset: *"259 engine pieces, max 5.245 ms ... extraction ... max 5.167 ms"*; five runs alone, max 3.02-3.43 ms against 4.36-4.64, all green; the same build's `x64-release` run was 1977 of 1977. T7 adds nothing to an open's path (the assembler is reached only from extract and split). **Again** 2026-11-17, the full `x64-release` run over I-24's and I-25's fixes (2116 of 2117): *"267 engine pieces, max 3.311 ms ... extraction ... max 3.294 ms"*, 0.017 ms over; and **for the first time ALONE**, one of five runs: max 3.452, 3.496, 3.454, **3.489**, 3.257 ms against 3.495, 3.774, 3.522, **3.429**, 3.668 -- the two maxima within a few hundredths of a millisecond in every run, so either side's jitter decides it. In an open's path since I-23: `PageTree` numbering each page's serial when it is built (one integer a page); I-24's and I-25's code is not in it. The same tree's `x64-debug` run was 2117 of 2117. **Again** 2026-11-17, the full `x64-release` run over `Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-39 (2117 of 2118): *"260 engine pieces, max 3.271 ms ... extraction ... max 3.053 ms"*; alone, one of five red again -- max 3.532, 2.942, 3.001, 3.200, 3.094 ms against 3.308, 3.088, 3.600, 3.914, 3.390. D7-39 is `ui/` alone, and `rawpage_render_tests` links no `rawpage_ui`, so none of it is in the binary; the same tree's `x64-debug` run was 2118 of 2118. **Again** 2026-11-17, the full `x64-release` run over I-26's fix (2118 of 2119), by the most yet: *"245 engine pieces, max 7.229 ms ... extraction ... max 3.750 ms"*, the case the first to finish in the run; alone, TWO of five red -- max 3.269, 3.115, 3.091, 3.774, 3.321 ms against 3.231, 4.322, 3.400, 3.726, 3.480. I-26's change is in the binary (`rawpage_core`) and not in an open's path -- the XFDF writer runs at an export. The same tree's `x64-debug` run was 2119 of 2119. *Open*; each full run is read for it.

**I-09 (S3) — a fuzz target built and counted, and run by nothing.** *Found* 2026-11-04 by reading **A-46**'s 60 s half
from run **41180314162** on `bcfbb1b` (T5's push): the `Portable libFuzzer` job's configure printed *"fuzz_assembly (6,
as expected)"*, its build linked `bin/fuzz_assembly`, and it then ran five targets -- the step list and the *"all five
targets linked"* loop were Stage 6's. `fuzz-soak.yml`'s matrix named the same five, so the soaks of 2026-10-31 …
2026-11-03 did not fuzz it either. No product behaviour is wrong; *S3*, like I-02, because what was lost is evidence --
§9 item 6 and A-46 are read from these logs, and D7-23 says *"the soak runs it"*. *Mechanism:* the targets a workflow
fuzzes are written by hand in two YAML files no build reads, while `cmake/RawPageFuzzTargets.cmake` asserts only how
many tests/fuzz BUILDS; T5 changed the build and its count and not the workflows. *Why no suite saw it:* the count
assertion was written for the opposite failure -- a target that does not exist passing *"no crashes"* -- and every job
was green, because a target nobody runs finds nothing. *Fix:* `fuzz_assembly` added to `ci.yml`'s linked loop and as a
60 s step, seeded from `corpus/assembly/` and `corpus/malformed/`, and to the soak's matrix with the same seeds; and the
class closed by `tests/gates/CheckFuzzWorkflows.cmake`, which reads `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt`'s
`rawpage_add_fuzz_target()` calls and both workflows as text, comments dropped, and refuses a target with no ci.yml run
step, no place in its linked loop, or no soak entry. *Bound, stated at the script:* it does not check seeds, times, or
that a step is not skipped by an `if:`. *Regression:* `gates.fuzz_workflows.repository` over the real three files,
