with `clean_pair_passes` (the control), `ci_unrun_fails`, `soak_unrun_fails` and `no_targets_fails` over
`tests/gates/fixtures/fuzz-workflows/`. *Control:* the repository case run BEFORE the workflow edits, red with all three
omissions named (`fuzz_assembly: no ci.yml step runs it`, `… not in ci.yml's linked loop`, `… no fuzz-soak.yml matrix
entry`); after them, green. *Fixed*, not closed: closed when a push's `Portable libFuzzer` log shows `fuzz_assembly`'s
60 s with no finding. *Since:* T6's push, run **41758087362** on `b3083f9`, RAN it -- the fix works -- and its first input
stopped on **I-12**, and so did the next two, **41770714539** and **41808943466**. The soak's
half works too: **41816834053**, the scheduled soak on `37ec179`, ran `fuzz_assembly` for the first time, and stopped on
**I-12**. **Closed** 2026-11-04 by run **41832843136** on `279fe66`: *"Done 11347 runs in 61 second(s)"* with no finding (A-46).

**I-10 (S2) — after a save, a page an undo put back was drawn as a page the engine never opened.** *Found* at T6 by
**A-124**'s raster half (`render.fullwriter.identity`): after a Save As that rewrote, each undo and redo drew the two pages
the rewrite had dropped and the undo brought back differently from the same step before the save -- render indices *0–3*
and *2–5*, both scales of exactly those two pages, every other page equal. A probe settled where: what Save would write
at that moment, reopened in a fresh engine, drew every page as before the save (0 differ), so the BYTES were right and
the screen was not -- D6-2's *what is shown is what is saved*, broken. A second probe found it older than T6: the same
undo after an ordinary APPEND save drew the same two pages wrong. *Mechanism:* the backend's page tree (D7-3) places each
drawn page in the tree read at open through `MutationSinkImpl::desiredDiskIndices` and `diskFrom` -- the newest revision
ON DISK. A page deleted and saved is in no disk revision, so an undo that puts it back answered `-1`, and the engine's
tree carried it as a page it had never opened, without the boxes, rotation and identity it read at open; `drawnOriginals`
likewise called it not the original object, so a destination naming it was not carried to it. *Why no suite saw it:*
T2's undo-after-a-save cases grade the SAVED file (A-24, `unit.roundtrip chains three saves and an undo after a save
writes the page back`) and T3's remap cases (A-30 … A-34) a move with no save between; no case drew the live engine after
a delete, a save and an undo. *Fix:* the page OBJECT each desired page shows (`MutationSinkImpl::desiredPageObjects`) is
looked up in the open tree's page objects (`DocumentBackendImpl::Open::openObjects`, read at open) when no disk revision
has it -- a page put back is the object the document had at open, and after a rewrite the object D7-29 writes back.
*Regression:* `render.fullwriter I-10 a page an undo puts back after an append save draws as it did` (`[I-10]`), and
A-124's raster case. *Control:* P7 (the fallback removed) red in both.

**I-11 (S2) — recovery cannot replay an undo, after a save, of a page delete from before it.** *Found* at T6 while writing
A-124's recovery clause, by a probe (removed after the run): `assembly/refs-every-kind.pdf`, page 2 deleted, saved, the
delete undone, the owner gone, the journal replayed over the saved file -- *"applied 0 stopped 1 pages 11"*, both after an
APPEND save and after a Save As that rewrote. *Mechanism, as far as read:* `RecoveryJournal` replays an undo of a step the
rebuilt history no longer holds as that command's `revert()` (`RecoveryJournal.h`), and `DeletePagesCommand`'s revert is
`MutationSink::restorePages(pages, key)` -- which puts back the pages the SINK took out under that key (`stash_`), held by the
process that ran the delete. The replaying process never ran it, so the key is unknown and the step refused -- by the
code, `error.command.malformed`; the probe read only that the replay stopped; after a rewrite the deleted pages' objects are not even in the file (D7-29 keeps them in the
memory of the session that rewrote). Stage 3's A-64 (`unit.recovery replays an undo after a save as the command's revert`)
proves the rule over a rotation, whose revert needs nothing the session held. *Why it is S2:* a replay that stops loses the
undo and every step after it; *open, not fixed in T6*, because the repair is a decision about what a page command's line
must carry across a save -- the pages' objects, or the file they lived in -- and no surface runs a page command before T8, so no
person can meet it yet. The duplicate and the insert, whose undos are removals by key too, were not probed. *Fixed* 2026-11-22, by the user's choice of three repairs and then of three again (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-43): read in the code, every command whose revert puts back what the sink KEPT under its key fails the same way -- the delete, the replace, the crop, the two halves of a resize, the renumber, the thumbnails -- and an exact journal of that kept state would have meant serializing some ten sink-wide structures, so an undo of a step from before the base is now a CHECKPOINT: the document as Save would append it, written beside the journal, which then starts from it; recovery opens it with the user's file's identity. The duplicate and the insert, whose undos remove, need nothing kept -- a checkpoint is taken for them too, because the rule names no command. Regression A-134, its controls red; *What it does not cover*: a process killed while the checkpoint is written (the kill harness's cycles do not undo past a save), and a checkpoint's cost on a large file, which is one append save of it, measured by `rawpage_bench_save` for a save (D3-4). The user tried I-11's own scenario by hand on the fixed build, the product killed by `Stop-Process -Force` after the rotation (`tests\manual\I-11`): recovered into the file with the checkpoint line, 12 pages, the rotation kept, a Save over the file -- as expected. *Closed* 2026-11-22 by CI run 45019723125 on `95549a4`, green in all five jobs, read from its logs 2026-11-22: A-134's five unit cases on Windows and under ASan + UBSan, its composition case on Windows.

**I-12 (S3) — a catalog or a tree node the walk never met was copied with the pages it lists.** *Found* by **A-46**'s
first 60 s run, CI run **41758087362** on `b3083f9` (T6's push), the first run that fuzzed the target at all (I-09's
fix): *"assembly invariant violated: the payload holds the source's tree"*, before any mutation (`MS: 0`), on one of the
1,484 seeds -- of which 1,462 were in `corpus/malformed/`, a directory of 24: ci.yml's lexer and parser steps, earlier in
the same job, had been given it as libFuzzer's FIRST directory, which is where libFuzzer writes what it finds, so the
crashing seed was one of their inputs and no checked-in file. The input was too large for libFuzzer to print and the job
kept no artifact, so it is lost. *Mechanism, read from the code and reproduced:* `ObjectGraphCopier::localFor` stopped at
the trailer's `/Root` and at every node and page in the walk's sets (D7-4's *"the page tree never followed"*, A-41); an
object that IS a catalog or a tree node but that the walk never MET -- a `/Pages` node no `/Kids` lists, an earlier
revision's `/Catalog` -- was copied like any resource, and with it, through its `/Kids`, every page it lists.
*Consequence:* objects of the source's structure in the payload, then in the document an insert lands in, referenced by
nothing it draws; no drawing changes -- *S3*. *Why the suite missed it:* `copier bounds` and the replay's hostile graph
name the trailer's catalog and the walked root, the two objects the sets hold. *Fix:* an object TYPED `/Catalog`, `/Pages`
or `/Page` that is not a copied page is never followed, by its type and not by the walk's sets -- the reference reads null,
as one naming nothing does. *Regression:* `unit.copier I-12: no part of the source's tree comes along and a page is copied as a page`
(`[I-12]`), each input through the fuzz body and read directly. *Control:* the case run BEFORE the fix, red on both inputs
with the fuzz job's own words. **And the job:** each fuzz step's first directory is now scratch (`fuzz-work/<target>`),
every run writes a crashing input to `fuzz-artifacts/` (`-artifact_prefix`), a failed job uploads it, and
`gates.fuzz_workflows.corpus_written_fails` refuses a run whose first directory is under `corpus/` -- the repository case
red against the pushed `ci.yml`, naming `fuzz_cos_lexer` and `fuzz_cos_parser`. **Found again** by the next run,
**41770714539** on `d238834`, the same words on a MUTATED input (`MS: 1 CMP- DE: "\012\000"`) from the 46 checked-in
seeds, this time kept (`fuzz-artifacts`, `crash-e0e7077d…`): the mutation split one intermediate node's `/Kids` key, so
that node -- still `/Type /Pages` -- has no `/Kids` array and the walk reads it as a PAGE (its rule, and PDFium's: a node
is what has `/Kids`). It was one of the pages copied, and `copyPage` kept a page's own `/Type`, so the payload held a
second `/Pages`. *Fix:* a copied page is written `/Type /Page` whatever its source says. *Regression:* the `[I-12]` case's
third input, `a leaf typed /Pages`, red before the fix with the job's words; and the CI input itself, run through the fuzz
body by a temporary case (removed after the run) -- *"copied 0 ok 0 the payload holds the source's tree"* with the fix
stashed, *"copied 1 ok 1"* with it. In the same run every other target ran its 60 s with no finding, each seeded from the
24 checked-in `malformed/` files rather than ~1,460. **Found a third time** by the run after, **41808943466** on
`37ec179`, the same words (`MS: 3 InsertByte-InsertByte-CrossOver-`, kept as `crash-24182741…`): a page's `/Annots` lists
the `/Type /Pages` node above it, and `listAnnotations` took every dictionary `/Annots` names for an annotation and
`copyAnnotation` wrote it -- a path out of the source that does not go through `localFor`'s stop. *Fix:* an `/Annots`
entry that is the catalog, a node or a page the walk met, or a dictionary typed `/Catalog`, `/Pages` or `/Page`, is no
annotation and is not copied. *Regression:* the case's fourth input, `an /Annots entry that is the tree` -- the node above
the page, the catalog, the page itself, an untyped root, a direct dictionary typed `/Pages`, and a node and a page the
walk never met, beside one real annotation that must still be copied -- red before the fix with the job's words. The CI input through the fuzz body by a temporary
case (removed): *"copied 0 ok 0 the payload holds the source's tree"* with the fix stashed, *"copied 1 ok 1"* with it.
*Control* for the other half (convention 22): a plant dropping every `/Annots` entry, red at the case's check that the
real annotation stays. **And the soak**, the same day: `fuzz-soak.yml`'s scheduled run **41816834053**, also on
`37ec179` and the first soak to run `fuzz_assembly` (I-09), stopped on the same words about 5 s in (`MS: 1 CrossOver-`,
kept as `crash-be1ef340…` in the `fuzz-soak-fuzz_assembly` artifact): the same path -- two pages' `/Annots` listing two
`/Type /Pages` nodes and three `/Type /Page` dictionaries, each written with the `/P` `copyAnnotation` adds. Through the
fuzz body by a temporary case (removed): *"copied 0 ok 0 the payload holds the source's tree"* with `37ec179`'s copier,
*"copied 1 ok 1"* with the fix above -- so it needed no fix of its own. The fourth input gained a node and a page typed so
and met by the walk nowhere, which only the fix's type half stops; *control:* that half planted out (the number half
kept), red at the job's words. **Closed** 2026-11-04: run **41832843136** on `279fe66`, `fuzz_assembly` 60 s (*"Done 11347 runs"*), and soak **41833219442** on `279fe66`, 601 s
(*"Done 92800 runs"*), each with no finding (A-46) -- a bound of that search time, not a proof no fourth form exists.

**I-13 (S3) — `qpdf` judged a fault Save As was given as one it wrote.** *Found* by **A-122**'s `qpdf` half, CI run
**41758087362**, the `Portable ASan + UBSan` job: *"qpdf check: 210 file(s), 3 failed"* -- `rewrite-40.pdf` (*"stream
inflate: invalid bit length repeat"*, object 3), `rewrite-44.pdf` (*"page object 9 0: object is supposed to be a stream
... but is neither"*) and `rewrite-54.pdf` (*"Loop detected in /Pages structure"*), every other rewrite clean. Read
locally, the three are the rewrites of `malformed/bad-flate-stream.pdf`, `duplicate-object-number.pdf` and
`pages-loop.pdf` -- a corrupted stream copied as its bytes, the object 5 the repair reads (the second, a font, so page 3's
`/Contents` names a font and the first object 5, page 3's content stream, is unreached and dropped), and a page-tree
cycle kept as its objects. *Is anything lost?* Measured with a case written for it, `render.fullwriter an unedited Save As
draws every page as the original drew it` (`[I-13]`): every corpus file Saved As with no edit, reopened in a fresh engine,
against the SOURCE as PDFium drew it before anything was written -- *"118 files Saved As unedited, 7708 renders, 0 differ"*.
PDFium reads all three sources as the rewrite does, `duplicate-object-number.pdf`'s page 3 included. So the files are
right and the CHECK was wrong: it holds every file we write to qpdf's clean line, and these keep damage they were given.
*Why no suite saw that question:* A-122's raster bar grades a rewrite against the APPEND of the same state, which carries
the first-party reading of a damaged file as the rewrite does -- the two depart from PDFium together or not at all.
*Fix:* a rewrite of `malformed/<name>.pdf` is kept as `written/rewrite-malformed-<name>.pdf`, and `check-qpdf.py --source
rewrite-malformed-=corpus/malformed` (ci.yml) counts its failure as INHERITED, printed with both verdicts, only when qpdf
refuses the source too; a rewrite of a source qpdf accepts, or one whose source is missing, fails as any file does.
*Regression:* the `[I-13]` case, which stays as the stronger bar; the script's rule run here over a stand-in qpdf (an
inherited failure passes, a clean source's and a missing source's fail, and without `--source` nothing is exempt).
*Control:* P2b (the rewrite skips `/XObject`, the verify's reachability off) red in the `[I-13]` case, *"26 differ or
refused"*. **Closed** by run **41770714539**'s `qpdf` log: *"qpdf check: 210 file(s), 0 failed, 3 inherited from a source
qpdf refuses too"*, and qpdf names the SAME fault in each source as in its rewrite -- the inflate error on object 3, the
page-tree loop at object 5, and *"page object 9 0: object is supposed to be a stream"* in `duplicate-object-number.pdf`
itself, so qpdf reads that file's object 5 as we and PDFium do. Whether a rewrite should
MEND a damaged page tree rather than keep it is not decided -- D7-25 writes objects value for value.

**I-14 (S3) — PDFium's first substitution of an unknown font draws unlike every later one.** *Found* 2026-11-04 by the
`[I-13]` case's first run: `malformed/bad-name-hex-escape.pdf` (`/BaseFont /Helve#GGtica`) differed on every page from
its unedited rewrite, which writes the name `/Helve#23GGtica`. *Measured* with a probe (removed after the run), one
process, the same file with the name respelled at the same length so no offset moves: the source opened TWICE gave two
digests; the second equalled `/Helve#00tica`, `/Helve` alone, `/HelveGGtica`, `/Helve#23GGti` and `/Xyzzy`; `/Helvetica`
was stable in both places; and with `/Helvetica` drawn first, the first UNKNOWN name drawn (`/Helve#00tica`) took the odd
digest instead. So the spelling does not matter and the order does: the first unknown font substituted in a process draws
one way, every later one another. *Consequence:* the first document of a session with a font PDFium does not know draws
unlike the same document opened again -- *S3*, nothing written changes. *Why no suite saw it:* every engine case compares
two drawings inside one process, and the first substitution happened to fall on a file both sides of a comparison drew
after it. *Cause inside the engine:* not established. *Bound, at the case:* the `[I-13]` case takes the first substitution
before its sweep and says it does not cover what a session's first document shows. **Met again at T14 (2026-11-23)** by A-91's
raster half -- `malformed/bad-name-hex-escape.pdf`, the sweep's first unknown font, ~54,000 pixels apart on each page with its
objects equal in value -- and bounded the same way, the first substitution taken before the sweep. *Open*; the user's decision.

**I-15 (S3) — the vcpkg binary cache restored nothing, and a rate limit stopped a fuzz run.** *Found* 2026-11-04 by
run **41824929474** on `f68f0c1`, the `Portable libFuzzer` job: configure stopped on *"curl operation failed with
response code 429"* for `gitlab.com/libtiff/…/libtiff-v4.7.2.tar.gz`, three attempts, and *"building tiff:x64-linux failed"*
-- no fuzz target was built or run, so the run says nothing of I-12. Not a product defect, and not the fault the job
reported: why a job downloads libtiff at all is. *Mechanism, read from the logs:* every vcpkg cache step reported a hit
on its key, and vcpkg then printed *"Restored 0 package(s)"* -- in run 41808943466's `Windows x64`, `Portable ASan +
UBSan` and `Portable libFuzzer`, in all six jobs of soak 41816834053 and in this run -- and built all nine ports. The key
hashed `vcpkg.json` and `vcpkg-configuration.json` alone, but a port's ABI also follows the RUNNER IMAGE, which ships the
vcpkg tool and its scripts: on image `20261025.353.1` the ABIs were identical across runs and across the ASan, fuzz and
soak jobs (`tiff` `64562705…`, `zlib` `e5a44375…`), and on `20261101.360.1` every one differed (`tiff` `f2df6356…`).
Archives saved under an older image matched nothing, and a key that still HIT is never saved again -- and on Linux
`actions/cache`'s combined step saves only when the whole job passes, so a red run never saved what it built. *Why
nothing caught it:* a cache that restores nothing is slower, never red. *Fix* (`ci.yml`'s three vcpkg caches,
`fuzz-soak.yml`'s): the key names the image (`ImageOS`-`ImageVersion`, a step that FAILS when either is unset rather than
fall back to the old key); no `restore-keys`, since another image's archives match nothing; restore and save as two
steps on Linux, as the Windows job already had; and every save only after a configure that SUCCEEDED -- a configure
stopped part-way holds some ports' archives, which saved under the key would be restored as a hit and never saved again
(the Windows save ran on any configure that ran). *Bound:* the image is what the logs show the ABIs following; anything
else that moves them is not keyed, and the same *"Restored 0 package(s)"* line is where it would show. `gates` 145/145
over the edited workflows (`gates.fuzz_workflows.*` read both files). *Pushed* in `279fe66`, 2026-11-04, and read from the logs of run **41832843136** on `279fe66` and soak **41833219442** on `279fe66`, which ran
together: every vcpkg cache step logged *"Cache not found"* for its image's key, built the ports, and the first job on each key
logged *"Cache saved with key"* -- `vcpkg-mingw-win25-vs2026-20261030.294.1-…`, `vcpkg-linux-ubuntu24-20261026.354.1-…`, and
`vcpkg-linux-fuzz-` for THREE Linux images, `20261018.96.1`, `20261026.354.1` and `20261101.360.1`, handed out to jobs
started within a minute of each other (12 MB on Windows and 15 MB each on Linux, `gh cache list`). The jobs that lost the race
to a key -- CI's `Portable libFuzzer` and three soak jobs -- logged *"Failed to save: Unable to reserve cache with key …,
another job may be creating this cache"* and *"Cache save failed"* as a warning, and stayed green. Every job still printed
*"Restored 0 package(s)"*, as a miss must. *Bound:* with the image in the key, a job restores only on an image a job before
it saved, and the runners hand out several images at once. ***Closed* 2026-11-14**: CI run 43464207081 on `c2d7e25` restored
its key in all three jobs that build ports -- *"Restored 9 package(s)"* in `Windows x64`, `Portable ASan + UBSan` and
`Portable libFuzzer`, no port built.

**I-16 (S3) — the content box a white-margin crop is made from reaches past a stroked path's ink.** *Found* 2026-11-08 by
**A-61**'s first run. `boxes.pdf`'s page 3, a FILLED rectangle, cropped to its content box: the ink touched every edge
within a device pixel of the padding asked. `cropbox-margins.pdf`'s page 1, whose panel is the same rectangle filled and
STROKED 2 pt wide: the engine's content box was `142 158 453.276 683.89` around a path at `144 160 451.276 681.89`, and the
ink at 144 dpi `143 158.89 452.5 682.89` -- the box 2 pt past the path on every side, the ink 1 pt, so the crop left
**1 point more white than asked** on each edge. *Mechanism, as far as measured:* the content box is the union of
`FPDFPageObj_GetBounds` (Stage 4's D4-14), and for a stroked path PDFium's bounds reach the full line width past the path
where the stroke draws half of it; why, inside the engine, was not read. *Why it is not fixed here:* D7-15 makes the crop
read the content box BECAUSE Fit Visible does, so the two agree on what the ink is; measuring the ink off a raster instead
would be a second definition of *white margin* (D7-15's argument) -- the user's to choose. A-61 grades each fixture as
measured: the filled one within a pixel of the padding, the stroked one one point further, and a control crop inside the
content box that leaves ink at the edge. *Open*, the user's decision: keep the content box, or measure the ink.

**I-17 (S3) — a Stage 7 T7 split case died once under the full run.** *Found* 2026-11-08 by T9's second full
`x64-release` run: `ui.composition.page.split at blank pages writes its files through the product's pumps` ended
`SEGFAULT` after 0.13 s, its output nothing but Catch2's `Filters:` and `Randomness seeded to: 3361317809` lines -- before
the case's first assertion, by its timing (it runs for seconds when green). *Not reproduced:* alone 4 of 4 green, then 32
runs eight at a time (the presets' `-j 8`), all green; the next full run green. Nothing T9 changed is on that case's path --
`core::SplitRunner`, the assembler, the blank pages' content boxes -- except that `DocumentBackendImpl::showEdits` now reads
the sink's boxes, which the split never sets. The run's log was copied before the next run overwrote it (CLAUDE.md's
trap); the case's whole output is the two lines quoted. *Open*: read again at each full run, as I-05 and I-08 are.
***Seen again at T13 (2026-11-13), and REPRODUCED -- older than T13.*** Two of T13's full `x64-release` runs each lost two
composition cases the same way -- `SEGFAULT` (an access violation, exit `-1073741819`) after 0.13 … 0.17 s, nothing printed
past Catch2's seed line: `pages remove white margins …` and `links link.autoDetect …` in the first, `pages embed thumbnails …`
and `links link.autoDetect …` in the second -- each green alone (3 of 3); the composition suite alone at `-j 8` lost one case in
two of three runs. **Every case it has taken runs ENGINE TASKS through the window** (`core::SplitRunner` at T9, and
`core::PageWorkRunner` now). *Reproduced:* `pages embed thumbnails …` alone, **eight processes at once**, **3 crashes of 32** with
T13's tree, and **9 of 48 on the T12 commit** (`8ca56ad`, the T13 work stashed and the tree rebuilt) -- so T13 did not bring it;
under `gdb` 0 of 48 (the debugger changes the timing). *Open* at T13, its mechanism not established.
***CI run 43464207081 on `c2d7e25` (2026-11-14) lost `links link.autoDetect …` the same way -- its only red -- and the mechanism
is FOUND.*** An unhandled-exception filter added to the suite's `main.cpp` for the run (not kept) walked the faulting thread's
stack: 6 of 48 runs of `pages embed thumbnails …` eight at once crashed, every one at the same place --
`core::PageWorkRunner::runningFor()` from the lambda `ui/shell/MainWindowPages.cpp` connects to the document's `destroyed`,
from `~DocumentWindow`, from `~QWidget` of the `MainWindow`, from `~Product`. **The case had passed; it died tearing down.**
Each engine run started from the window -- the split (`splitRunner_`, `MainWindow.cpp`), the margins and the thumbnails
(`MainWindowPages.cpp`), the bookmarks' and the links' reading (`MainWindowBookmarks.cpp`, `MainWindowLinks.cpp`) -- connects
the document's `destroyed` to a lambda on `this` that reads `splitRunner_` or `pageWork_`; `~QWidget` deletes the child
documents AFTER `~MainWindow`'s members are destroyed and BEFORE its `QObject` part drops the connection, so the lambda read
a freed runner -- harmless when the freed memory still said *not running*, an access violation when it had been reused. That
is why only cases that ran engine work were taken, why the timing was a fraction of the case's (ctest's figure, not the
case's start), and why `x64-debug` and `gdb` did not see it (libstdc++'s `~unique_ptr` nulls its pointer, a store `-O3` may
drop). Stage 1's I-12 and convention 10, a fifth time. *Why no suite saw it:* the read is undefined rather than wrong, and a
case's assertions were all done before it. *Fix:* `~MainWindow` disconnects every `DocumentWindow` below it from its
`destroyed`, beside the I-12 disconnects. *Case:* `tests/composition/tst_pagesproduct.cpp`'s `[I-17]` -- a slot connected
before the window's asks, as the document dies with the window, whether the window is still connected (deterministic, where
the crash is not). *Control:* the fix out, red -- `CHECK_FALSE( stillConnected )`, `!true`; a first form of the case, a
child of the document, stayed green over the plant (a widget emits `destroyed` before deleting its children) and was
replaced. *Measured:* with the fix, 0 of 96 (`[A-58]`) and 0 of 48 (`[A-84]`) eight at once, against 6 of 48; the full
`x64-release` run 2103/2103. ***CLOSED 2026-11-15*** -- pushed in `e52b719`, and CI run **43555926975** on it green in all five
jobs, `Windows x64` 2103/2103 with `links link.autoDetect …`, `pages embed thumbnails …` and the `[I-17]` case among them.

**I-20 (S2) — a bookmark to the first page made before a save was written with no destination.** *Found* 2026-11-12 at T12, writing `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl`'s link targets beside T11's outline targets: a new page -- one a duplicate or an insert made -- is numbered NEGATIVE until a save gives it a number, from `nextNewPage_ = -1` down, and T11's `OutlineTarget::page` used `-1` for *the target names no page*, so a GoTo set to the first new page was taken for one with no page: `composeOutline` wrote no `/Dest`, and a save then wrote the bookmark going nowhere; after the save `adopt` renumbered `-1` to the real page and the NEXT compose would have written it, so the file on disk was wrong exactly when it mattered. *Why no suite saw it:* T11's cases set bookmark targets to pages the file holds, or after a save; its contents page (A-80) inserts a page but its links are ANNOTATIONS resolved when the insert runs, not outline items; no case set a bookmark to a page a duplicate or an insert made before the save that wrote it. *Reproduced* first by `tests/unit/tst_outlineedit.cpp`'s `[I-20]` case -- duplicate page 1, a bookmark to the duplicate, save, read back: `-1 == 1`, no `/Dest`. *Fix* (inside T12, the user's choice): the target's page is `std::optional<long long>`, as T12's link targets' is from the start. *Control:* P12, the `-1` meaning *no page* again, red -- the case failed at its destination, `-1 == 1`.

**I-21 (S3) — PDFium draws a page stamp's large text one device row from where it draws the page's own. OPEN, its mechanism not established.** *Found* 2026-11-13 at T13 by A-89's first run: page 3 of `refs-every-kind.pdf` as a stamp, against the page drawn by a fresh engine at the same scale, *"2.02242 % of its pixels above 8, 0.313718 % above 96, the worst 245"* -- the golden thresholds are 0.1 % and 0.01 %. *Measured:* the 48-point identity's ink a whole row higher in the stamp (its row profile the source's shifted by one), the caption's rules 0.05 px apart and its 7-point text in place; not the stamp's transform -- the stamp then placed on exactly the engine's own pixel grid for the page (153 × 216, both draws one transform), *"1.77318 %"*; nor a scale written in the stream -- the appearance rewritten so the scale is the reader's Algorithm 8.1 (D7-36(d)), *"1.75805 %"*; nor the stamp's graphics state -- dropped, unchanged. What is left is the engine itself: a glyph's baseline snapped to a pixel in an annotation's form, and its page's, from origins a few hundredths of a pixel apart. Registered to one device pixel the two draws agree -- *"0 % above 16, 0 % above 96, the worst 14"* at 100 %. **At 400 %**, where A-89 is now graded (a pixel's registration at 100 % forgave regular for bold -- planted P1), the gap is still there: *"0.645382 % above 8, 0.147186 % above 96"* unregistered, *"0.000378857 % above 16, 0 % above 96, the worst 19"* registered. *Why no suite saw it:* nothing before T13 drew a page's own content inside an annotation's appearance; Stage 6's image stamps (A-77) are pictures, which the engine resamples without snapping. *Decision* (the user's, 2026-11-13): A-89 graded registered to one pixel -- at most 1 % above 16, none above 96 -- its unregistered figures printed every run; this issue open, S3, for the mechanism, as **I-05** and **I-08** are. *What the registration does not see:* a glyph one row off -- this; *what it does:* two rows, a glyph missing, a wrong colour (its control: page 4 for page 3 fails it). *Open:* read at each full run; M-14's walk, Acrobat's and Firefox's reading of the same stamp, passed 2026-11-22 -- the user saw it sharp and the same in all three readers.

**I-22 (S3) — CI's runner too slow for A-107's kill case to reach a side file. CLOSED 2026-11-14** -- CI run 43464207081 on `c2d7e25`, the fix's push, green on all six `platform.recovery.kill_*` cases. *Found* 2026-11-14 in CI run 43450746795 on `17e4f4b`, `Windows x64`, the only red of 2102: `platform.recovery.kill_annotations` (Stage 6 guide A-107, `tests/platform/tst_recoverykill.cpp`), seed 260784057, *"between apply and the flush: 6; inside apply: 4; inside the flush: 10; runs replaying a side file: 0; most steps replayed: 4"* -- `CHECK( runsWithPayload >= 1 )`, `0 >= 1`. *Mechanism:* the floor needs a kill after step 5 of `killprobe/AnnotationSteps.h`'s cycle, the first 200 KiB payload, and every kill was timed 20-400 ms from the probe's `ready`; this machine replays up to 10-12 steps in that window, the runner reached at most 4 (its flush slower, under eight cases at once), so the floor was a test of the runner's speed. Not a recovery defect: every replay in the run was graded and agreed. `kill_pages` (`mostSteps >= kPageCycle`, 5) and `kill_rewrite` (`>= 2` past the rewrite) were timed the same way, green in CI so far. *Why the suite missed it:* each floor was set from this machine's figures (A-107's recorded run, *"most steps replayed: 10"*), and earlier CI runs, the last two among them, happened to pass it. *Fix:* `killProbe` takes an ANCHOR, a line the probe writes, and times the kill from it; every fourth run (`kAnchoredEvery`, 5 of 20) anchors on the line saying the floor's step is WRITTEN (`flushing 5` for the annotations, the first payload, and for the pages, the cycle's last; `flushing 7`, the second step after the rewrite) -- so those five reach the floor on a runner of any speed, the delay after the anchor random as before, and fifteen land from `ready` as they did. *Control:* the probe's dwell raised from 15 to 60 ms to stand in for the runner, the anchor off -- `kill_annotations` *"runs replaying a side file: 0; most steps replayed: 3"*, `0 >= 1`, CI's failure, and `kill_pages` `3 >= 5` beside it (`kill_rewrite` green at 3); the anchor on, the same dwell -- *"runs replaying a side file: 5; most steps replayed: 7"*, pages 7, rewrite 5, all three green. *What it does not cover* (convention 19): an anchored run's landing is chosen after a known step, not from `ready`; the windows' floors (`inside the flush`, `between apply and the flush`) are still reached by all twenty. *Closed* when a CI run after the push is green on it, or the user closes it on the code.

**I-23 (S2) — a page cut and pasted back into its own document loses every reference to it. FIXED 2026-11-17, M-01's step 3 walked again the same day; CLOSED 2026-11-17 by CI run 44086248480, green in all five jobs on `b1b4ef9`, the push that carried the fix (`54f2757`).** *Found* 2026-11-17 by the user at **M-01**'s step 3 on `refs-every-kind.pdf` (release build of `81295a6`): P-0006 cut (`Ctrl+X`) from slot 2 and pasted (`Ctrl+V`) after P-0012 -- the page back, and its bookmark **P-0006** greyed with the *"Goes nowhere"* tooltip (`panes.destinations.unresolved`), exactly as **P-0008**'s, whose page step 3 deleted. *Mechanism, as designed* (D7-31(e), `Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` §3; D7-13): `MainWindow::cutPages` (`ui/shell/MainWindowPages.cpp`) is `copyPages` then `deletePages` -- the copy is a self-contained graph on the clipboard (`core::encodeCopiedPages`), the delete one `DeletePagesCommand` that sets every reference to the page to no page (D7-2) -- and `pastePages` is an `InsertPagesCommand` of that graph: new page objects that nothing in the document names, so nothing is reconnected. A drag within the pane and `Alt+↑`/`Alt+↓` are ONE `MovePagesCommand`, which carries every reference with its page, so the same rearrangement made by cut and paste and by drag differs in its references. *The user's requirement* (2026-11-17): **a cut and paste should keep the cut pages' bookmarks and links**, as a move does -- named destinations are the same mechanism and were not asked about. *Why no suite saw it:* the behaviour is the design's, and the cases grade it as designed -- A-55 checks that a cut is one undo step and that the pasted pages show their identities, never what points at them afterwards; no case or walk before M-01 asked a cut and paste to behave as a move. *Severity:* S2 as logged on 2026-11-17 -- a page command's reference repair a later stage builds on -- the user's to change; while it is S2 it blocks Stage 7a's sign-off (§9 item 9). *Design* (the user's three answers, 2026-11-17, `Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` **D7-37**): (a) what named the cut pages AND the cut pages' own links to other pages are kept; (b) for a paste into the same open document in the same process, any time later; (c) only what still goes nowhere, for a copy as for a cut. *Fix* (2026-11-17): a copy records, with the document it came from and under the clipboard payload's SHA-256, what named each copied page -- bookmarks by id, named destinations by name, links on other pages by id -- and each copied page's own links to pages not copied, the target by a new model identity, `core::Page::serial`, kept through every page command (`core/doc/PageReconnect`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `MainWindow::copyPages`); a paste of that payload into that document makes the record concrete for its insert (`core::reconnectionFor`, every page an index after it) and `InsertPagesCommand` carries it -- once its pages are in, it points again what still goes nowhere, sink first, all or nothing with the insert, in one undo step and one journal line (`"reconnect"`, only when there is one). The sink now takes a new TARGET on a link copy not yet saved (`MutationSinkImpl::replaceAnnotations`, held in `linkTargets_` and written in `copiedDictionary`); any other edit of such a copy is still `error.edit.copyNotSaved`. *Regression cases:* `tests/unit/tst_pagereconnect.cpp`, eight cases through the shipped first-party sink (the `[I-23]` tag also selects an older case of another stage's I-23 -- read the counts below with it), saved and read back -- the serial through a move, an undone delete, a duplicate and an insert; P-0006 cut and pasted after P-0012, its bookmark and page 1's link at the pasted page, undone and redone as one step and saved so; P-0001 cut, P-0009 moved between, pasted -- its bookmark and its own two links at P-0009 and P-0006 where they now are; `dest-alpha` with P-0003, saved, then the paste undone after the save and saved again, the file the cut's; a bookmark pointed elsewhere between cut and paste stays, and the command asks again when it runs; a copy pasted back changes only its own links, a second paste of a cut is a copy; the control, an insert with no record, leaves them going nowhere; a journal line replayed over the same document gives the same model hash. And `tests/composition/tst_pagesproduct.cpp`'s `[I-23]` case through the product, the live engine reading what each named page SHOWS: M-01's step 3 (P-0006 after P-0012), P-0001 and its links to P-0009 and P-0006, one undo step, the clipboard pasted into another document plain, and a record never offered to another document's payload. `tst_command_serialize.cpp`: two inserts carrying a reconnection round-trip, fifteen hostile `reconnect` fields refused, and the bounds read. *Controls* (ten, each built and its cases run): P1 the insert never reconnecting -- six unit cases and the product case red; P2 `reconnectionFor` naming what is already named -- two red; P3 the command not asking again -- one; P4 a link's target not moved past the insert -- one; P5 the sink writing no target on a copy -- one (read back from the saved file); P7 the paste ignoring the record and P8 the record offered to any payload -- the product case red each; P9 a duplicate keeping its source's serial and P10 an inserted page given none -- one each. **P6, the revert undoing the reconnection itself before removing the pages, STAYED GREEN** -- with a save after the undo added for it: everything the reconnection points names a pasted page, so removing those pages leaves the model and the written file as before the paste. The explicit undo was taken out as redundant, the reason at `InsertPagesCommand::revert`. P1's first run also found a defect in the CASE: a destination the save had dropped was read through a null `find`, an access violation reported as no case run; the case reads it through `destinationPage` now. *Not covered* (convention 19): a link whose identity is DIRECT, structure elements, beads and `/OpenAction` (D7-37(a)); the second process (D7-37(b)); a redo of an insert gives its pages NEW serials, so a record taken before an undone-and-redone insert does not find a link target among those pages. *Runs* (2026-11-17, after the controls): **2112/2112 on `x64-release` (321 s) and on `x64-debug` (437 s)** at `-j 8`, the six named skips of 2026-11-14's run, nine cases more (eight unit, one product); `checks` clean; the five changed `core/` and `pdf/ops/` files compiled at `-O2 -fsanitize=address,undefined -Werror` clean (the `portable-asan` job's own run is CI's, after a push).

**I-24 (S3) — `Shift`+click on a link with the Selection tool armed selects it instead of following it. CLOSED 2026-11-17 on the user's word.** *Found* 2026-11-17 by the user on `refs-every-kind.pdf` (release build of `54f2757`): `Shift`+click on a link did not follow it; the Hand tool armed explicitly did. `Shift` is the temporary hand (`Features.md` §1.2, `ui/viewer/ToolController.h`), and a click with the hand follows a link (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-35(h)) -- and Claude had told the user it would, without trying it. *Mechanism, read in the code:* (1) with the Selection tool ARMED, `PageView::mousePressEvent` hands a press on any annotation to the annotation overlay first (`overlayFirst()` keys on `armed()`, not the effective tool), which Stage 6 made on purpose -- *"a temporary hand still reaches the overlay first: `Shift` over an annotation constrains its drag"* -- so the overlay picks the link up and the press returns before the link is remembered; (2) the link is remembered only when `tools_->effective()` is the hand, read BEFORE `ToolController::press` applies the click's own modifiers, so a `Shift` whose key-press went to another widget (the thumbnails pane, focused) is not yet the hand there. *Why no suite saw it:* A-86's following cases click with the Hand tool armed and press `Enter`; no case `Shift`+clicks a link. `Shift` does not ADD to an annotation selection (`Ctrl` does, `AnnotationOverlay::press`), so a click and a constrained drag can both keep it. *Fix* (2026-11-17, the user's go-ahead on the proposal): `PageView::mousePressEvent` applies the click's own modifiers (`ToolController::setModifiers`) and remembers a link under the hand BEFORE the overlay can take the press; `mouseReleaseEvent` follows it at a release within the drag distance whoever took the press -- the overlay's release path included. A `Shift`+drag on a link is still the overlay's constrained move; the link the overlay picked up stays selected. *Regression case:* `tests/composition/tst_linkproduct.cpp`'s `[I-24]`, through the product with no `Shift` KEY event at all: a `Shift`+click on link 19 under the Selection tool shows P-0009 and adds no history step; a `Shift`+drag on link 20 moves it (one step) and follows nothing; the control, a plain click, selects link 21 and follows nothing. *Controls:* Q1 the click's modifiers not applied first and Q2 the overlay's release following nothing -- the case red each.

**I-25 (S1) — an extract and a split write no bookmarks and no form fields. CLOSED 2026-11-17 on the user's word, the extract and split made again on the fixed build.** *Found* 2026-11-17 by the user preparing M-02 (release build of `7ebed47`): *Extract Pages* of pages 3-8 of `refs-every-kind.pdf` as one new document -- its Bookmarks pane empty. *Mechanism, read in the code:* extract and split copy their pages through `ObjectGraphCopier::copyFile` with the DEFAULT options (`pdf/DocumentBackendImpl.cpp`); `Options::outline` and `Options::fields` exist since T10 and only merge sets them (`pdf/ops/DocumentMerger.cpp`, D7-16, D7-33). So no bookmark reaches an extract or a split file, and a page's form-field widgets are left behind (each reported, `WidgetNotCopied`). Neither D7-5 nor D7-30 (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` §3) records it, and `Shipped-Surface.md` names no such absence. *Why no suite saw it:* A-48 and A-49 (§5.9) grade each new document by the identities its pages show -- its first and last against the label -- and a split's boundaries by the outline it reads, never the outline or the fields it WRITES. *Severity:* S1, the table's own words (*"a bookmark ... or a field value ... lost on ... extract"*), the user's choice; it blocks Stage 7a's sign-off (§9 item 9). *Design* (the user's answers, 2026-11-17): (a) an extract and each split file carry the bookmarks that point at THEIR pages, with the parents those need so the tree still reads, and drop the ones that point only at pages left behind; (b) they carry the form fields whose widgets are on their pages, with their values, as merge copies them, and fields only on other pages stay behind. Recorded as `Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` **D7-38**. *Fix* (2026-11-17): `DocumentAssembler::Source::make`, which every extract and split file goes through, copies with `ObjectGraphCopier::Options::outline`, `fields` and a new `pruneOutline` -- the items that name a copied page and the ancestors they need, read before anything is copied, so an item left behind numbers and copies nothing; an ancestor kept for its children alone is cleared and reported. `build` keeps the payload's catalog, `/Outlines` and `/AcroForm` with it. `Source::copy`, an insert's payload, is unchanged. *Regression cases* (`tests/unit/tst_assembler.cpp`, `[I-25]`, read back by the model a reader builds): pages 3-8 of `refs-every-kind.pdf` -- `P-0001=-1[P-0003=0] P-0005=2[P-0006=3 P-0008=5]`, the parent reported cleared; page 2 alone under its parent; a page no item names, no `/Outlines`; the split at its top-level bookmarks, each file its own pages' bookmarks; pages 2-3 of `annots-on-pages.pdf` -- `field-2` and `field-3` with their values, no widget reported left behind; the control, an insert's payload with neither. A-47's shape checks over every corpus file stayed green with the fields and outlines now in. *Controls:* Q3 the whole outline kept and Q4 none -- two cases red each; Q5 no fields and Q6 an ancestor not kept for its children -- one each. *Runs* (2026-11-17, with I-24's fix): **2117/2117 on `x64-debug` (367 s)**, the seventh skip `ui.diagnostics_ignore_the_bus` (a RawPage window was open); on `x64-release` the first run 2116 of 2117, **I-08** (its entry), and the second **2117/2117 (326 s)**, the six named skips; `checks` clean; `ObjectGraphCopier.cpp` and `DocumentAssembler.cpp` compiled at `-O2 -fsanitize=address,undefined -Werror` clean.

**I-26 (S2) — an XFDF we wrote that we cannot read: a rich text's body cut from inside its prolog. FIXED 2026-11-17; CLOSED by CI run 44118269002 on `20bf88c`, the fix's own push (2026-11-17), green in all five jobs and the case passing on Windows and under ASan + UBSan, read from its logs 2026-11-22.** *Found* by the scheduled fuzz soak, run **44076823592** on `e52b719`, 2026-11-17: `fuzz_annot_pipeline` aborted after 66 s and 6,012 runs with *"annotation pipeline invariant violated: an XFDF we wrote that we cannot read"* (Stage 6 guide A-23's EXCHANGE invariant), no sanitizer report; the other five targets ran 600 s clean. The soaks of 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16 ran the same target over the same code clean -- a new input, not a regression. *Reproduced* on `x64-debug` from the run's artifact (`crash-ca2713df42c0e4c52f17979ba3bd12503cddb10c`, 6,933 bytes): an FDF of 13 comments read, the XFDF written from them refused at its line 35 (`error.exchange.malformed`). *Mechanism:* one comment's `/RC` had had its XML declaration grown by the fuzzer into a processing instruction spanning lines -- `<?xodDate (D:20260831000000Z) >> endobj xref ... trailer << /Size 8 /Root 6 0 R /ID [ <D7CFB5Eml version="1.0"?><body ...>...</body>` -- which is WELL-FORMED, so `core/doc/XfdfCodec`'s writer kept it (`Writer::richText` parses the rich text first and writes nothing when it is not). But it then found where the body begins by scanning for the first `<` that did not open `<?` or `<!`, and that was the `<<` INSIDE the instruction: `<contents-richtext>` received the instruction's tail and the body, and the file was not XML. The same scan fails on any prolog holding `<` -- a comment `<!-- <p> -->` before the body, an instruction's data -- so it is a file another producer can write, and an XFDF export of its comments would be refused by our import and any reader. *Why no suite saw it:* every rich text the cases and the corpus carry is `<?xml version="1.0"?><body ...>` or a bare body -- the shapes Acrobat 26.2 and `AnnotationOps::richTextFor` write -- and A-23's replay runs the corpus, which by construction finds nothing new (convention 19). *Severity:* S2, the table's *"a new document another reader refuses"*: the export is a file. *Fix* (2026-11-17): the body begins where the PARSER met the root element -- the root's start tag ends where `QXmlStreamReader` stands once it has read it, and begins at the last `<` before that, since well-formed XML has none inside a tag; and what is about to be written is parsed once more inside a `<contents-richtext>` of its own first, and left out if that fails, so the comment's promise -- *written as the elements it is, or nothing* -- is kept by a reading. **The first attempt was wrong, and its own case said so:** the offset BEFORE the root's token is not the `<` -- with no declaration, the reader's first token (`StartDocument`) has already read two characters of the tag (measured on Qt 6.11.1: offsets 0 → 2 before `<body ...>`) -- so a bare body, which the old scan wrote, was left out; the case's fifth rich text caught it before anything else ran. *Regression case:* `tests/unit/tst_fdf.cpp`'s `[I-26]` (with `[fuzz]` and `[A-23]`), five rich texts written to XFDF and read back: the soak's shape, an instruction holding `<p>`, a comment holding `<body>` with an `xml-stylesheet` instruction after it, the Acrobat shape and a bare body -- every one read back as the declaration and the body WHOLE, the plain text kept; a rich text not well-formed on its own still left out and the file still read; and the fuzz body's exchange branch over the FDF carrying all five. The soak's own input through the whole fuzz body passes on the fix (a throwaway probe, reverted). *Controls* (three, each built, the case run through the environment): P1 the old scan with the self-check kept, P2 the old scan without it -- the code the soak met -- and P3 the offset before the tag, the first attempt: the case red each. *Runs:* 2026-11-17, after the controls: **2119/2119 on `x64-debug` (427 s)**; `x64-release` 2118 of 2119 (315 s), the one red **I-08** (its entry); six named skips each; one case more; `checks` clean; `XfdfCodec.cpp` compiled at `-O2 -fsanitize=address,undefined -Werror` clean (the `portable-asan` job's own run and the next soak are CI's).

**I-27 (S3) — a merge report's reason for a file that is not a PDF shows an unfilled `%1`, and words it otherwise than the dialog. FIXED 2026-11-22; CLOSED 2026-11-22 by CI run 44972695756 on `82e4ec4`, green in all five jobs, read from its logs 2026-11-22.** *Found* 2026-11-22 by the user at **M-03**'s step 3 (release build of `20bf88c`), the report's details for `tests\manual\M-03\in\17-merge-not-a-pdf.pdf` (a copy of `corpus/assembly/merge-not-a-pdf.pdf`): *"… 17-merge-not-a-pdf.pdf was skipped: %1 is not a PDF file."* The merge dialog's row for the same file had said *"Will be skipped: This file contains no PDF objects and cannot be opened."* *Mechanism, read in the code:* `DocumentMerger::merge` (`pdf/ops/DocumentMerger.cpp`) skips an input whose first 1,024 bytes hold no `%PDF-` with `error.open.notPdf` and NO context, and that key's catalog text takes the file's name as `%1` (`PdfiumDocument` passes `info.fileName()` with the same key); the report's line already names the path, so the reason repeats an empty name. The dialog counts the file through `IDocumentBackend::filePageCount`, which reaches the first-party repair and refuses it as `error.pdf.noObjects` -- so one file gets two reasons. *Why no suite saw it:* `tests/unit/tst_merge.cpp` grades a skip by its KEY (`{3, "error.open.notPdf"}`), never by the sentence the key and its context make, and no case reads the dialog's reason beside the report's. *Severity:* S3, the table's *"a message is unclear"* -- the file is skipped and named as it should be; nothing is lost. *Decision* (the user's, 2026-11-22): pass the file's name. *Fix:* `DocumentMerger::merge` skips with the input's name as the key's context, and `mergeFiles`, which holds the path, puts the file's own name in its place -- suffix and all, as `PdfiumDocument` names one -- so the line reads *"… 17-merge-not-a-pdf.pdf was skipped: 17-merge-not-a-pdf.pdf is not a PDF file."* The dialog's reason (`error.pdf.noObjects`, from the repair `filePageCount` reaches) is unchanged: the user chose the name, not one key for both. The other skip keys were read for the same fault: `error.open.notFound` carries its path, `error.pdf.pageOutOfRange` its page, and the rest name no `%1`. *Regression case:* `tests/unit/tst_merge.cpp`'s A-70 case, tagged `[S7-I-27]` (Stage 6's guide has an I-27 of its own): the false PDF's context is `merge-not-a-pdf.pdf`, the missing file's its path. *Control:* the fix reverted, the case built and run -- red, the context empty. *Runs:* 2026-11-22, after the control: **`x64-release` 2119/2119 (322 s)**, six named skips, the 27 `merge` cases among them (A-72's 30-way case included); `checks` clean; `DocumentMerger.cpp` compiled at `-O2 -fsanitize=address,undefined -Werror` clean. `x64-debug` not run for this fix.

**I-28 (S3) — a merge's Cancel took 1.42 s to stop it, against §3.5's 1 s. CLOSED 2026-11-22 on the user's word, its mechanism not established.** *Found* 2026-11-22 at **M-03**'s step 2, read from the product's log (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\RawPage\logs\rawpage-20261122-060354-216.log`): the merge of 29 inputs started at `06:08:38.645`, *"merge: cancel asked"* at `06:08:39.498`, *"merge ended -- error.merge.cancelled"* at `06:08:40.922` -- **1.42 s** from the cancel to the merge stopped, both stamps on the UI thread. No tab opened and no file was written. The same 29 inputs merged to the end at the third run in 1.83 s of reading and copying and 1.07 s of build and verify (`06:18:33.069` → `34.903` → `35.971`). The user judged the cancel prompt. *What is known:* `DocumentMerger::merge` asks whether to go on before each input and before the build and the verify, never inside a copy, a build or a verify (D7-33(b)'s *"at its next step"*), and these inputs are uneven -- nine 900-page files, three of them `annots-900.pdf`, 682 KB. *Not established:* which step the cancel landed in -- the log stamps no step -- so whether one input's copy runs over a second, or something else delays the stop. *Why no suite saw it:* A-72 grades the bound on `merge-a`, `merge-b` and `merge-c` ten times each with `pages-900.pdf` -- 990 pages, its cancel stopped in 53 ms -- and no case cancels a merge of heavy inputs. *Decision* (the user's, 2026-11-22): *"It's fast enough, consider it's 30-way merge"* -- the bound is not to hold for any input, and nothing is changed in the merge. §3.5's row now says the 1 s is A-72's inputs and names this reading beside it; which step the cancel waited on stays unmeasured.

**I-32 (S3) — a test file's two warnings in a clean build, since T13. FIXED and CLOSED 2026-11-22.** *Found* 2026-11-22 by **A-01** in Stage 7a's closing run: `cmake --build --preset x64-release --clean-first` exited 0 and printed *"tests/composition/tst_stampink.cpp:256:119: warning: missing initializer for member 'rawpage::ui::StampChoice::page' [-Wmissing-field-initializers]"* and the same at `:261:129`. *Mechanism:* Stage 6 T10's composition case for the Stamp tool sets the palette's choice with `ui::StampChoice{Kind::Custom, name, QString(), image}`, the four members `StampChoice` then had; T13 (`17e4f4b`) added a fifth, `core::StampPage page`, for a stamp made from a PDF page, and left the case alone -- it still compiled, `page` value-initialized, an image stamp's empty page, which is what the case meant. *Why the suite missed it:* nothing fails on a warning outside `core/` and `pdf/` -- every test target takes `rawpage_common_flags` without `rawpage_strict()`'s `-Werror` -- and an incremental build shows a warning only in the build that recompiles the file, T13's, and no gate or case reads that build's output; A-01, the one case that counts a whole build's `warning:` lines, is a stage's close. *Fix:* `{}` for `page` on both lines, naming the member the case leaves empty. *Verified:* the release tree rebuilt, 0 `warning:`; `x64-debug`'s clean build after it, 0; both full suites green over it (A-116). *Closed* by CI run 45029686401 on `447dab2`, its push: green in all five jobs, `Windows x64` 2132/2132 with `tst_stampink.cpp` compiled and no `warning:` line in the job's log. **What it does not settle:** a warning in a test file can still pass every case until the next A-01 -- the targets' warning policy is unchanged.

**I-33 (S3) — `ribbon-validate` counted the tabs' access keys as groups, since Stage 1 I-42. FIXED 2026-11-22; CLOSED 2026-11-23.** *Found* 2026-11-22, after Stage 7a's sign-off, re-deriving the document set against the tree: `ribbon-validate res/ribbon.json` printed *"ribbon-validate: clean -- 9 tabs, 58 groups, 222 controls"*, while `res/ribbon.json` holds 49 groups (counted from `tabs[].groups`; 58 is its count of `"id"` lines, tabs and groups together) and `Shipped-Surface.md` had said 49 since Stage 6's sign-off. *Mechanism:* `tools/ribbon-validate/main.cpp` printed `parsed.spec.catalogKeys().size() - parsed.spec.tabs.size()` as the group count. When Stage 1 T10 wrote it (`dd28437`, 2026-08-24) `Spec::catalogKeys()` listed each tab's title and each group's, so the subtraction left the groups; Stage 1 guide I-42 (`488b1ed`, 2026-08-31) added each tab's `ribbon.keytip.*` access key to the same list, so the dump would cover them, and from then the line read groups + tabs. Nothing the tool decides used the figure -- its rules, its diagnostics and its exit code were right throughout. *Why the suite missed it:* no case reads the success line: the build step and A-51 judge the exit code and the diagnostics, and `tst_ribbonbuild`'s cases compare the widget tree with the parsed spec, never with this arithmetic. So the wrong figure lived in documents: `Architecture-FileMap.md`'s `res/ribbon.json` row quoted it as *58 groups* from 2026-09-21. *Fix:* `ui::ribbon::Spec::groupCount()` (`ui/shell/RibbonModel`), counted from `tabs[].groups` as `controlCount()` counts controls, its header saying why it must never be derived from `catalogKeys()`; the tool prints it. *Verified:* the tool prints *"9 tabs, 49 groups, 222 controls"*, as the product's startup log does for tabs and controls; `ui.the group count is the resource's groups and not its catalog keys` (`[I-33]`, `tests/ui/tst_ribbonbuild.cpp`) reads `res/ribbon.json` with Qt's own JSON reader rather than `ribbon::parse`, requires `groupCount()` to equal it, and carries the old arithmetic as its control, required to DISAGREE on the shipped ribbon; `[ribbon]` 14/14, `ctest --preset x64-release` 2133/2133 (321 s, the six named skips), `checks` clean. **What it does not cover:** the tool's printed line itself -- no case runs the tool's success path and reads its stdout; the line calls `groupCount()` and nothing else. *Closed* 2026-11-23 by CI run 45070177289 on `8992142`, green in all five jobs, its Windows log printing *"ribbon-validate: clean -- 9 tabs, 49 groups, 225 controls"* and the `[I-33]` case passed.

**I-34 (S3) — a resize's wrapper taken out of an indirect `/Contents` array wrote it back direct, since T9. FIXED and CLOSED 2026-11-23, inside T14.** *Found* 2026-11-23 at Stage 7 T14: planted control P2 -- the record T14 keeps of a `/Contents` written as a reference to an array object, taken away -- stayed GREEN against `decoration.roundtrip` over the whole editable corpus, because no corpus page writes `/Contents` that way; reading the compose to see what P2 should have broken showed that T9's resize wrapper, composed by the same code before T14, had never kept such a value: wrapping wrote a direct array `[wrap-q, a, b, wrap-Q]`, and unwrapping -- an undo before a save or after one -- wrote `[a b]` direct, where the page had held `/Contents 7 0 R`. *Consequence:* the page draws the same and every reader reads the same streams; the page dictionary is not what it was, so A-62's *"unwrapped exactly"* was true only of the shape its case used -- *S3*. *Why the suite missed it:* `resize: the content is wrapped …` (A-62) uses `text-letter.pdf`, a single stream reference, and the reversibility sweep compares deltas, which an undo before a save leaves empty either way. *Fix:* the resize wrappers and the decorations are composed by one function (`MutationSinkImpl::composeContentLayers`), which records a `/Contents` that is a reference to an array in the page's private `/RawPageContents` while it is layered and writes it back with the last layer gone (`Stage7-Assembly-Plan.md` D7-44(b)). *Verified:* `decoration.roundtrip: a /Contents written as a reference to an array, and /Resources two pages share, come back exactly` (`[I-34]`) builds such a page and requires, after a header applied and removed and after a scaled resize saved and undone, the page, its `/Contents` and its `/Resources` equal in value to the original -- the `/Contents` the very reference; P2 red against it; both full suites green. **What it does not cover:** a `/Contents` array object SHARED by two pages -- the record restores each page's own reference, and the shared array is never edited, so it holds; no corpus file has one and no case builds one.

**I-35 (S3) — the decoration golden suite compared its machine-dependent CJK case on a machine with other faces. FIXED and CLOSED 2026-11-23.** *Found* by CI run **45059346338** on `a2ae049`, T14's push: `Windows x64` 2160/2161, `render.golden.decoration every case matches its baseline` failing on `cjk` alone -- *"297 of 248832 pixels differ by more than 8 (0.1194 %, limit 0.1000 %), of which 79 differ by more than 96 (0.0317 %, ...); worst channel delta 242"*; `Gates` 131/131, `Portable ASan + UBSan` 1167/1167 with no sanitizer line and *"qpdf check: 415 file(s), 0 failed, 5 inherited"*, `Portable libFuzzer` six targets 61 s each with no finding, `Coverage` green. *Mechanism:* `tests/golden/DecorationCases`' `cjk` case sets Han, kana and Hangul in the predefined CJK collections, never embedded, so PDFium draws them in faces the MACHINE resolves (here `STSong -> SimSun`, `KozMinPro -> Yu Mincho`, `HYSMyeongJo -> Malgun Gothic`); the runner resolves its own. T14 marked the case `machineDependent` -- and applied that only to I-13's substitution tripwire, not to the comparison, which compared it on every machine. *Why no suite saw it:* on the machine that captured the baseline the faces are the capture's, so the comparison passes there by construction; only another machine can fail it. *Fix:* the corpus suite's own rule (`golden/README.md`, point 3; `tst_golden`'s `substitutionsMatchCapture`): a machine-dependent decoration case is compared only where this machine substitutes exactly the faces `captured.json` records for it, and is otherwise reported NOT APPLICABLE by name (a `WARN` naming both lists); only a machine-dependent case may be skipped, and every other is still compared. *Verified:* here, all nine compared, green; the CONTROL -- `cjk`'s recorded `STSong -> SimSun` planted as another face -- reported *"not applicable -- decoration/cjk: this machine substitutes ..."*, the other eight compared, green, `captured.json` restored. **What it does not cover:** the `cjk` baseline is graded on no machine but one that resolves this one's faces -- as every corpus `machineDependent` case is. *Closed* by CI run 45070177289 on `8992142` (`8992142`), green in all five jobs: `Windows x64` 2161/2161 with CI's seven named skips, both `render.golden.decoration` cases passed and 0 `warning:` lines; `Gates` 131/131; `Portable ASan + UBSan` 1167/1167 with no sanitizer line, `qpdf` 415 files, 0 failed, 5 inherited; `Portable libFuzzer` six targets with no finding; `Coverage` green. Whether the runner compared `cjk` or reported it not applicable the ctest log does not say -- a passing case's `WARN` is not printed.

---
